  ============================================================
  MAINTENANCE LOG — READ THIS BEFORE EDITING
  ============================================================
  Instruction for Claude (or anyone else editing CRATE): every time you
  make a change, bump CURRENT_VERSION below AND the matching
  APP_VERSION constant in js/01-core.js (search "APP_VERSION =")
  to the same value — that constant is what actually gets
  printed to the browser console and shown on-site (in the ⚙ Layout menu),
  so the two must never drift apart. Also add a new entry to the top of
  this log — one or two lines is enough: what changed and why. This is the
  only running record of what's been done to this file, so keep it going
  rather than skipping it for "small" edits.

  The ⚙ menu's "Update log…" reads THIS file (CHANGELOG.txt), so keep each entry's
  header line exactly in the form  "  X.Y.Z — YYYY-MM-DD — summary"  (two leading spaces, em
  dashes, a real version and date), put the summary in the FIRST paragraph (the list shows
  it; everything after the first blank line becomes "Details"), indent the rest of the entry
  by ten spaces, and don't put a line of equals signs inside an entry. Newest entry on top.

  CURRENT_VERSION: 1.93.2

  1.93.2 — 2026-11-09 — A tab can be opened in a window of its own. Page only.
          ⧉ at the end of the tab bar opens the tab you are on in a new
          window; right-clicking a tab (Music, Diary, Scrobbles, Stats,
          Artist, or an artist's or genre's tab) offers "Open in new window",
          and a middle-click does it straight away, as in a browser. The new
          window is CRATE itself, opened on that tab once the crate has
          loaded, with the same sign-in, and its title names the tab ("Frank
          Zappa — CRATE"). Not on a phone. If nothing opens, allow pop-ups
          for the site.

  1.93.1 — 2026-11-09 — Search finds and suggests songs by their titles. Page only.
          The search box's suggestions have Tracks: a song whose title
          matches what you type, with its artist and release ("Black Napkins
          · Frank Zappa · Zoot Allures"). Picking one puts in track:"Black
          Napkins": the releases with it (in the Tracks view, the song itself).
          Searching a song's title, and track:, now also look in tracklists
          CRATE has already looked up this visit (MusicBrainz, iTunes), not
          only the saved ones; nothing is fetched for it.

  1.93.0 — 2026-11-09 — Search suggests as you type; Crate Galaxy follows your crate while it is open; sort and filter by whether a release has been updated from RYM; where each release was recorded, from MusicBrainz. Page only.
          SUGGESTIONS: under the search box, artists, releases, genres and
          labels of your crate that match what you type (a word's start
          first). An artist, genre or label goes in as an exact filter
          (artist:"Frank Zappa") so more can follow; a release opens. After
          "genre:" (artist:, label:, type:…) that field's values; the start of
          a field's name offers it. ↑/↓, Enter or Tab, Esc; mouse and touch.
          GALAXY: while it is open, a release added, changed or deleted (or,
          with only what is showing, the filter changing) rebuilds the map a
          moment later; the stars already there keep their places and the
          camera stays where it is.
          UPDATED FROM RYM: ⇅ has "Recently updated from RYM" and "Not updated
          from RYM first", and a filter, "Updated from RYM: Any / Updated /
          Not yet". Fixed: the RYM rating filters there ("At least …",
          "Missing an RYM rating") were shown but never acted on.
          RECORDED AT: a release with no Location is looked up on
          MusicBrainz: the place most of its songs were "recorded at", with
          its area ("Abbey Road Studios, St John's Wood"; a second studio with
          a third of the songs is added) becomes its Location. New releases
          by themselves; ⚙ → "Find where releases were recorded…" for the rest
          (about one a second, with a Stop). A Location you have is never
          changed; one MusicBrainz doesn't know isn't asked again for 60 days.

  1.92.9 — 2026-11-09 — A genre's "On Rate Your Music" row can count only releases with the genre among their primary genres. Page only.
          A second switch on the row: "Primary genre" (the default) or
          "Primary + secondary". With Primary genre, King Crimson's Red is no
          longer one of Experimental's: Free Improvisation (under Experimental)
          is only among its secondary genres. A release with the genre, or one
          of its sub-genres when "Include sub-genres" is on, among its primary
          genres is still there. The choice is remembered for every genre.

  1.92.8 — 2026-11-09 — A shared credit ("Don Shinn and The Soul Agents") is listed under each of its artists. Page only.
          A credit with "&" or "and" counts as several artists when every part
          is an artist of your crate on its own (Paul Simon & Art Garfunkel,
          with both in it), or when it is a full name and a "The …" band (Don
          Shinn and The Soul Agents, Nick Cave & The Bad Seeds). One word and
          a band (Echo & the Bunnymen, Florence and the Machine) and names like
          Simon & Garfunkel or Earth, Wind & Fire stay one artist. The release
          keeps its credit; the artist list, the artist filter, each artist's
          page (releases, plays, stats), Top artists go by its artists, and
          opening the credit opens its first artist. An artist's page lists
          its "Shared credits". In ✎ Edit details: "Keep together" on a shared
          credit, or "Split into …" on a credit kept whole; your choice wins,
          and is kept with the artist details.

  1.92.7 — 2026-11-09 — An artist's details read like RYM's (Type, Born, Died, Also known as, Members, Member of) with an Edit button, and Fill from RYM takes them from the artist's page on Rate Your Music. Page and RYM add-on 1.21.0.
          TIDIER: under the artist's name, the details are a short list of
          labels and values; names are links to their pages. The boxes, the
          type menu and ★/✕ are only there after "✎ Edit details" (Done puts
          them away), and only on the artist you pressed it on.
          FROM RYM: the add-on (1.21.0) notes what an artist's page on Rate
          Your Music lists beside the name (born or formed, died or
          disbanded, members with their roles, member of, also known as) and
          hands it to CRATE. "Fill from RYM" adds it: the type (born: Person;
          formed or members: Band), the dates, the members, this artist as a
          member of each band it was in, and the other names as aliases (one
          that is already another artist's alias is left there). It only
          adds; nothing you set is changed. For an artist whose RYM page the
          add-on hasn't read yet, it opens that page.

  1.92.6 — 2026-11-09 — Artists have a type (Person, Band, Group…), a band has members, and a member's page says which bands they are in. Page and Apps Script backend.
          On an artist's page, under "Also known as": a Type (Person, Band,
          Group, Duo, Trio, Orchestra, Choir, Collective, Project, Other) and,
          for anything but a person, Members: type a name (your crate's
          artists are offered) and a role if you like (guitar), and Add; ✕
          removes one, and a member's name opens their page. A person's page
          shows "Member of" with the bands that list them (through their
          aliases too). "Fill from MusicBrainz" takes the type, and a group's
          members with their instruments, from the MusicBrainz artist the page
          is matched to. Kept by the main name, with your account by a new
          backend action (saveArtistDetails, an ArtistDetails setting): update
          the Apps Script and redeploy. Until then they are kept in this
          browser, and the page says so.
          Also: with too little room beside the covers, an artist's Top tracks
          go under them instead of over them.

  1.92.5 — 2026-11-09 — An artist's Top tracks are easier to read: whole song titles, a rank, and the RYM rating, how many rated it and your plays in their own columns. Page only.
          The list is wider and a long title wraps onto a second line instead
          of being cut ("Chunga's Reven…"). Each song has its place in the
          list, and the RYM average (as RYM prints it, 4.0), how many rated it
          and your plays sit in aligned columns under their headings. Hover a
          title for its release.

  1.92.4 — 2026-11-09 — A track's lettered parts ("a) Part 1") are no longer songs of their own; an alias can be made the main name; an artist's Top tracks show each song's RYM rating and how many rated it, beside your plays. Page and RYM add-on 1.20.1.
          PARTS: Rate Your Music lists the parts of a track under it with no
          position of their own ("A4 The Nancy & Mary Music", then "a) Part 1",
          "b) Part 2", "c) Part 3"). They were read as songs, so the release had
          too many and the parts were flagged with no link. Now a lettered part
          ("a)", "b.", "i.") with no position, under a track that has one, is
          part of it, and adds its length to a track that had none. The add-on
          (1.20.1) reads them so; CRATE also folds them in lists the add-on read
          before. A release that already saved them as songs: Edit tracks →
          Fill from RYM again (or delete the part rows).
          MAIN NAME: each alias on an artist's page has ★, "Make this the main
          name": it becomes the main name, and the old one (and every other
          alias) a lesser name of it. On Zappa's page, add "Frank Zappa" and ★
          it, and Frank Zappa is the main name with Zappa beneath it.
          TOP TRACKS: on an artist's page, each song shows its RYM average and
          how many rated it (4.1 · 260), beside your plays. The order can be
          Top rated (RYM, weighted by how many rated it; the default), Most
          popular (RYM, most rated), Most played or Album order. With no RYM
          ratings for the artist's songs yet, it is by your plays.

  1.92.3 — 2026-11-09 — "Update from Rate Your Music" says it reads in the background: with the RYM add-on 1.20.0 no tab opens, unless Rate Your Music asks for a check. Page only (the change is in the add-on).
          The add-on 1.20.0 reads the release pages in an invisible frame of
          its own instead of a background tab each; a page Rate Your Music
          answers with a check opens in a tab (and stops the run there if the
          tab gets one too). CRATE's question before a run no longer says each
          page opens in a background tab. With an older add-on, runs still use
          tabs.

  1.92.2 — 2026-11-09 — Artist aliases: other names an artist goes by ("Zappa" is Frank Zappa), so their releases group under one artist. Page and Apps Script backend.
          On an artist's page, "Also known as" under the name: type another
          name (your crate's artist names are offered) and Add; ✕ removes one.
          The artist list, the artist filter, the artist's page (releases,
          plays, stats), a genre's Top artists and search go by the main name:
          searching "frank zappa" or artist:frank zappa finds Zappa's
          releases, and opening "Zappa" opens Frank Zappa's page. Each
          release keeps the artist name it has; nothing in the sheet is
          renamed. An alias of an alias points at the main name.
          They are kept with your account by a new backend action
          (saveArtistAliases, an ArtistAliases setting): update the Apps
          Script and redeploy. Until then they are kept in this browser, and
          the artist's page says so.


  1.92.1 — 2026-11-08 — On a phone, the ☰ drawer uses the whole screen: icon-only rail with ✕ to close, tighter genre tree, bigger ▾. Page only.
          The rail (Music / Playlist Explorer) took 84px with its labels cut
          to "PLAY…", and the list didn't reach the edge of a wider phone. On
          a phone the rail is icons only (56px) with ✕ at its foot to close
          the drawer, and the list fills the rest of the screen. The genre
          tree steps in 12px a level instead of 16 (so deep genres keep their
          names), its ▾ is a finger-sized target, and the Processing strip
          steps aside while the drawer is open. (A genre row's depth is now a
          CSS variable, --depth: computers keep their 16px a level.)

  1.92.0 — 2026-11-08 — CRATE for iPhone: an app (ios/) that opens CRATE full screen and keeps the music playing with the phone locked. Page: what's playing goes to the app's lock screen.
          THE APP (ios/, Swift, generated with XcodeGen): CRATE's page in a full
          screen web view, as an audio app to iOS, so it may keep playing with
          the phone locked. Its page keeps running in the background (the next
          song starts by itself); YouTube's player isn't told the app was
          hidden, and is started again if iOS pauses it anyway; YouTube and
          SoundCloud may start without a tap; the lock screen has the song,
          cover, play / pause and previous / next; Google's sign-in window,
          CRATE's confirmations and links to other apps work. Install with
          Sideloadly or AltStore: ios/README.md. Untested on an iPhone so far.
          THE PAGE (js/39-phone-playback.js): inside the app, it reports what's
          playing to it and answers the lock screen's buttons
          (window.crateRemote). In a browser nothing changes.

  1.91.4 — 2026-11-08 — "Play in the YouTube app" (Quick actions, on a phone): hands a release's YouTube videos to the YouTube app, which can keep playing with the phone locked. Page only.
          iOS pauses YouTube in a web page when the phone locks; the YouTube
          app keeps playing (with YouTube Premium). A release with one video
          (a full-release video, a single) opens it from where its first song
          starts; one with a video per song opens them in order as a YouTube
          playlist (up to 50). It is a real link you tap, so iOS can give it
          to the app; CRATE's own player pauses. Shown only on a phone, and
          only for releases with YouTube links.

  1.91.3 — 2026-11-08 — On a phone, a YouTube video or SoundCloud track that doesn't start is shown large, to be tapped once to start. Page only.
          A phone lets a page start sound only in answer to a tap, and CRATE
          starts a video a moment after the tap (once it has looked it up),
          so a phone could refuse: the video loaded but never played, while
          the bar showed it as playing. A tap on YouTube's (or SoundCloud's)
          own player always works, and after that first one the same player
          starts the following songs by itself. So when one hasn't started
          3.5 seconds after CRATE asked, the player is shown large above the
          bar with "Tap the video to start it" (SoundCloud's widget, kept off
          the screen until now, is brought on), and goes back to its place
          once it plays. The bar shows Play meanwhile. Computers unchanged.

  1.91.2 — 2026-11-08 — On a phone, a cover's stars tag shows the RYM average instead of cutting it to "…", and the player no longer has an empty strip under it. Page only.
          STARS TAG: it holds the stars and the Rate Your Music average (RYM
          3.45) on one line, which didn't fit a phone-sized cover: it was cut
          to "★★★★ …", with the type tag (LP) covering its end. On a phone it
          is two lines, stars over the average, kept clear of the type tag,
          and a long catalogue number ends in "…" before the red ! corner.
          PLAYER: it padded its bottom for the iPhone's home indicator, but on
          a phone it sits above the tab bar, which already does: an empty
          strip under the player. That room is only added now while a dialog
          has moved the player to the bottom of the screen.

  1.91.1 — 2026-11-08 — Fix: the catalogue number, type and stars on the covers could only be changed from the Album covers view, though they show in the Albums view too. Page only.
          Panel Settings for the Albums grid now has "Tags on artwork" (top
          left, bottom right, bottom left: set one to Empty to hide it) above
          its "Fields below cover". It is the same setting as Album covers',
          so a change in either view shows in both.

  1.91.0 — 2026-11-08 — On a phone, Spotify plays through the Spotify app, so it keeps playing with the phone locked; and the lock screen shows what's playing, with working buttons. Page only.
          SPOTIFY ON A PHONE: CRATE's in-browser Spotify player only runs on
          computers (Spotify doesn't support it in phone browsers), so a
          Spotify release never started on a phone. There, CRATE now sends it
          to the Spotify app on the phone through Spotify's Web API: the music
          comes from the app, so locking the phone doesn't stop it, and
          Spotify's own lock-screen controls work. The player bar's play /
          pause, previous / next and seek control the app the same way. If
          Spotify doesn't list the app (it hasn't been opened for a while),
          CRATE says to open it and play and pause any song first, with a tap
          to open it. "Play Spotify on…" in Quick actions (phone only) picks
          another Spotify device instead (a speaker, a computer); CRATE plays
          there from then on. New file: js/39-phone-playback.js.
          LOCK SCREEN: the song, artist, album and cover from the player bar,
          with play / pause and previous / next. iOS still pauses YouTube and
          SoundCloud when the phone locks (they are embedded video and
          widgets); this names what was playing and gives working buttons.

  1.90.8 — 2026-11-08 — Panels configuration and Panel Settings fit a phone, and no dialog is covered by the player any more. Page only.
          PANELS CONFIGURATION / PANEL SETTINGS are floating windows on a
          computer. On a phone they ran off the bottom of the screen, under
          the player; the page behind scrolled instead of the window; the
          "Clicking an album cover opens" dropdown ran off the right edge with
          its label squeezed into a column one word wide; and the layout boxes
          didn't use the full width. On a phone they are now full-screen
          sheets that scroll inside, with each dropdown under its label at the
          full width, the layout boxes stacked full width, and CRATE's gold
          checkboxes instead of the browser's blue.
          PLAYER: a full-screen dialog covers the tab bar, but the player
          (kept above dialogs on purpose) stayed at tab-bar height, floating
          over the dialog with the dialog showing beneath it. While one is
          open the player moves to the bottom of the screen and the dialog's
          last rows scroll clear of it (Add a release, Edit, these two, …).
          These two also count as an open dialog now, so the Processing strip
          and the RYM waiting bar step aside for them as for every other.

  1.90.7 — 2026-11-08 — Fix: on a newer iPhone, holding a cover still brought up the phone's enlarged copy with Save to Photos / Copy Subject / Look Up. Page only.
          That iOS ignores 1.90.5's setting for images. On touch screens,
          images now let touches pass through to the card or button around
          them (where every tap is handled anyway), so there is no image to
          hold: a long-press on a cover opens only CRATE's Quick actions. The
          player's cover and Quick Peek's cover, which have taps of their
          own, are left as they were. The Processing strip also steps aside
          while Quick actions is open (it covered its last rows).

  1.90.6 — 2026-11-08 — Fix: the page showed without its styles for a moment while loading (bare text and grey buttons on white). Page only.
          The stylesheet was linked at the very end of the page (since the
          split into files in 1.78.0), so the browser drew all the markup
          before reaching it. It is in the <head> now, after Leaflet's, so
          nothing is drawn until CRATE's styles are there.

  1.90.5 — 2026-11-08 — Holding an image on a phone no longer pops up an enlarged preview. Page only.
          On a touch screen, holding a cover opened the phone's own preview
          (iPhone: an enlarged copy with "Save to Photos"; Android: the
          browser's image menu), which also got in the way of a card's own
          long-press (Quick actions). Both are off for images on touch
          screens, and holding a card, the player or Quick Peek no longer
          starts selecting text. A mouse's right-click on an image is as
          before.

  1.90.4 — 2026-11-08 — A pinch or a double tap no longer zooms the page on a phone. Page only.
          Three parts, since iPhone Safari ignores the usual one: the viewport
          tag (maximum-scale=1, user-scalable=no) for Android, touch-action:
          manipulation on the page for double-tap zoom, and Safari's own pinch
          gesture cancelled. The maps and Galaxy still zoom with a pinch inside
          their own boxes.

  1.90.3 — 2026-11-08 — CRATE can be added to a phone's home screen and opens full-screen, with no browser address bar. Toasts no longer cover the tab bar. Page only.
          THE APP FILES: index.html already pointed to manifest.webmanifest
          and icons/, but neither was ever uploaded, so a home-screen CRATE
          opened in ordinary Safari. Both are here now: the manifest (name,
          full-screen, the app's colours) and the icons (the gold wordmark on
          the dark background; a C for the browser tab). iPhone also gets its
          own tags: full-screen, a see-through status bar, the name CRATE.
          To use it on an iPhone: Safari → Share → Add to Home Screen, then
          open CRATE from that icon.
          NOTCH: the page now reaches the screen's edges (viewport-fit=cover),
          so the safe-area padding the CSS already had takes effect: the
          header clears the notch, the tab bar the home indicator, and dialogs
          centred in the window (the album page, Galaxy, …) start below the
          status bar.
          TOASTS ("Copied …", "Saved …") sat at the bottom right, over the tab
          bar on a phone: they show above it now, and above the player and the
          Processing strip.

  1.90.2 — 2026-11-08 — Search on a phone: tapping ⌕ always puts you in the box, it shares its row with ▤ and ⚙, ✕ clears it, and the keyboard no longer capitalises or autocorrects. Page only.
          TAPPING ⌕: pressing it gave the icon focus, which opened the box and
          moved the icon from under the finger, so the tap never became a
          click and the box never got focus: the first letters typed went
          nowhere (seen in Chrome; Safari doesn't focus a tapped button).
          ROW: open, the box shares the row with ▤ and ⚙ instead of taking a
          row of its own and pushing them onto a third. ✕ empties it and closes
          it; ▾ (Advanced search) is big enough to tap. No width animation as
          it closes (the icons jumped to a second row for a moment).
          KEYBOARD: no auto-capitals, autocorrect or spellcheck on artist
          names, and the return key reads Search and puts the keyboard away.
          ADVANCED SEARCH: the Processing strip no longer sits over its
          fields while a full-screen dialog is open.

  1.90.1 — 2026-11-08 — On a phone the ⚙ menu fits the screen: it opened pinned to the gear's right edge at its full width, so its left side was cut off. Page only.
          It is now a full-width panel under the header that scrolls inside,
          ending above the tab bar (and above the Processing strip while one
          is showing). The Actions in it (Listened filter, Add album, …) are
          full-width rows again; 1.90.0's plain header icons had squeezed
          them into small squares.

  1.90.0 — 2026-11-08 — A better layout on a phone: three covers to a row, a slimmer header, a bottom tab bar with icons, and the Processing strip no longer covers the tabs. Page only.
          TAB BAR: Processing (and the Match songs and background strips)
          sat at the very bottom, on top of the tab bar, so Diary, Scrobbles,
          Stats and Artist couldn't be tapped while anything was running.
          They now float above the bar (and above the player), and the page
          ends above them. Each tab has an icon over its name; the one open
          is in the accent colour with a line above it.
          MUSIC: the side margins are 12px instead of 40px, which fits three
          covers to a row (two before). On a phone a card shows two lines
          under the cover (usually title and artist): the stars and format
          are already on the cover. The header is one row of plain icons
          beside a smaller wordmark, and the stats under the grid are one
          line. Wider than 900px nothing changes.

  1.89.7 — 2026-11-08 — Match songs: a full-release video's songs can be ticked in one click, and a finished run can be applied or closed from its pill. A genre's releases can be sorted by when you added them. Page only.
          FULL-RELEASE VIDEO: one that isn't sure (its title or length a
          little off) was offered with nothing ticked, so a run that found
          one had nothing to apply unless each song was ticked by hand. Its
          YouTube box now has "Tick all its songs" (and "Untick its songs").
          THE PILL: "Matching finished" at the bottom has "Apply N links" when
          something is ticked, and ✕ to close the run (asked first if ticked
          links would be lost), so it doesn't stay there waiting.
          GENRE PAGE: the Releases sort has "Recently added": the newest in
          your crate first, ones with no date added last. (The main grid's ⇅
          already had it.)

  1.89.6 — 2026-11-08 — Right-click the player: look the release up on RateYourMusic, or copy a link to what's playing. The artist's name opens the artist's page. Page only.
          The player's right-click menu (and the Up Next queue's, for the
          track playing) has "Look up on RateYourMusic ↗" (the release's RYM
          page, found the way "View on RateYourMusic" finds it; for a Spotify
          track not in your crate, RYM's search for its album) and "Copy link
          to what's playing": the Spotify track, the SoundCloud track, or the
          YouTube video, at the song's start when it is part of a full-release
          video. A release's own right-click menu offers the copy too while it
          is the one playing.
          ARTIST: the artist's name in the player (on whichever of its lines
          shows the artist) and on the album page now opens the artist's
          information page, as it already did in Quick Peek. The album page
          used to go to the Artist tab's discography instead.

  1.89.5 — 2026-11-08 — A genre's "On Rate Your Music" list is a row of covers, like Top artists, instead of a list under About. Page only.
          It sits full width above "Top artists in this genre" and scrolls
          sideways: up to 25 releases, each cover with its rank (top left)
          and RYM average (bottom right), the title, the artist and how many
          rated it (9.2k). The Top rated / Most popular switch, what the order
          is by, and the link to RYM's own chart are kept. Click a cover to
          open it, double-click to play. The About card is on its own again.

  1.89.4 — 2026-11-08 — Match songs: no full-release video for a single or a two-song release, outtakes count against a video, and "Search song by song" works while a video is offered. Page only.
          SHORT RELEASES: a release of one or two songs isn't matched to a
          "full-release video" (it found a video of the A-side, even studio
          takes of it, and cut it in two); it goes to the song-by-song search.
          OUTTAKES: "take 4-6", demos, outtakes, rehearsals, alternate takes,
          acoustic and unplugged versions count against a video, like covers
          and live versions (unless the release is one).
          SONG BY SONG: chosen in the YouTube menu while a full-release video
          was offered, it did nothing, as every song already had a match (its
          part of the video). Now the video is set aside, every song not
          linked is searched, and the video stays in the menu to take back.
          Also fixed: Preferences → edition (original or remastered) never
          had any effect; the words it looks for were never matched.

  1.89.3 — 2026-11-08 — A genre's page lists its releases as Rate Your Music rates them: top rated, or most popular. Page only.
          A card under About, "On Rate Your Music", with a switch: Top rated
          (RYM's average, weighted by how many rated it, so a 4.3 from 12
          people doesn't outrank a 4.1 from 9,000) or Most popular (most
          ratings). Ten at first, 25 with "Show more"; each row has the RYM
          average and how many rated it, and your own rating; click to open,
          double-click to play. It follows "Include sub-genres". Links to
          RYM's own chart for the genre. Releases with no RYM rating aren't
          listed (the RYM add-on and "Update from Rate Your Music" fill them).

  1.89.2 — 2026-11-08 — A genre's page shows how far you are through it on Rate Your Music: the pages done, the ones opened but not done, and the next one to check. Page and RYM add-on 1.19.0.
          The RYM add-on (1.18.0) notes each genre and chart page you open on
          Rate Your Music and which are done; from 1.19.0 it hands that to
          CRATE. A genre's page has a card, "On Rate Your Music", in the side
          column: for the genre's own page and any chart of it ("Top · album
          · 1980s"), what is done ("1–5, 8"), what was opened but not done,
          when you last looked, and a link to the next page to check. With
          nothing yet, it links to the genre on RYM. CRATE keeps the last copy
          it was given (crate_rym_pages_v1), so the card shows when the
          add-on isn't answering. Install the new crate-rym-addon.zip.

  1.89.1 — 2026-11-08 — Match songs: releases can be removed from a run's queue. Page only.
          The Queue tab has a ✕ at the end of each release's row, and
          "Remove" among the actions for the releases you select. Links
          already applied stay; ticked links not applied yet are dropped with
          the release (you are asked first). A run left with nothing in it
          closes. The "Waiting in the match queue" line has "Clear" beside
          "Add them to this run".


  1.89.0 — 2026-11-07 — Full-release videos are timed by the video itself. When Match songs (or ⋯ → Full-release video… → Find) finds a video
          of the whole release, it now reads the video's own chapters, or the "0:00 Song" times in its description, and puts each song
          where they say, the last one ending where the video does (an "Intro" chapter is passed over). Only when the video has
          neither are your song lengths used: stretched evenly to the video's length when they come within 4% of it (a faster
          transfer, gaps between songs), else back to back as before. The best two videos are read, so one with chapters that name
          your songs is chosen, and is sure even when its length is a little off. The YouTube box says how the songs were timed.
          Finding: a reaction, review, cover, tribute, karaoke, slowed/reverb, sped-up, nightcore, 8D, remix, instrumental or live
          upload scores far lower (unless the release is one); the artist's "- Topic" channel and the release year in the title
          count for it; through the add-on, a second search without "full album" when nothing sure turned up. Chapters need the
          CRATE for YouTube add-on 1.4.0 (it reads a video's page); with the API, the description comes with the search at no
          extra cost. An older add-on still works, placing songs from their lengths.

  1.88.0 — 2026-11-07 — Ask your crate (⚙ → "Ask your crate…"): search in plain English, e.g. "90s shoegaze I rated 4+ and haven't played in a year, not on Creation". What it understood is shown back as chips. Page only.
          No AI service: a hand-written grammar over a lexicon built from your own crate. A genre takes in everything under it in
          your Genre Taxonomy ("rock" finds shoegaze); descriptors, scenes, movements, labels, artists, places and titles are known by
          name, longest first. It reads your rating and RYM's ("rated 4+", "5 stars", "unrated", "favourites", "rym 3.8+",
          "acclaimed", "obscure", "popular", "bold"), years and decades ("early 90s", "1990-1995", "between 1975 and 1985",
          "before 1970", "since 2015"), when you added and played things ("added this year", "in the last 3 months", "haven't
          played in a year", "never played", "played more than 10 times"), places ("from Japan", "british"), types ("eps",
          "singles", "live"), "by", "on", "not / except / without", "or", and sorting and counts ("best 20", "10 random",
          "newest first", "most played").
          A word it can't place is searched in titles and artists; a near miss of a name is read as that name ("shoegase" as
          Shoegaze) and says so; anything else matches nothing and says so, rather than being quietly dropped. Excluding something
          your crate doesn't have excludes nothing, and says that too. Suggestions as you type (Tab or the arrows to take one);
          Enter keeps the question in Recent. Results can be played, shuffled or saved as a playlist.

  1.87.0 — 2026-11-07 — Taste Engine (⚙ → "Taste Engine…"): learns how you rate from the releases you have rated, predicts the rest with how sure it is, says why, and picks what to play next. Page only.
          Each release is described by what it is made of (genres with their taxonomy parents, descriptors, scene, movement, label,
          artist, decade) plus its RYM rating and how many rated it there. A ridge regression gives each a weight: its pull on your
          rating in stars. It is solved by conjugate gradients without ever forming the full matrix, so thousands of features cost
          little; how strongly to hold the weights back (λ) is chosen by 5-fold cross-validation.
          Listen next: unrated releases by predicted rating, plus a share of how unsure it is (the Safe ↔ Adventurous slider), each
          one less like those already picked, so the list isn't one artist ten times. "Play these" queues them. "Why" shows what
          moved each prediction.
          Teach it: the twelve releases it is least sure about (sixteen models fitted to resamples of your ratings disagree most
          on them), one per artist; rate one with the stars and it saves the rating and learns again straight away.
          Your taste: what pulls your rating up and down, by genres & scenes, descriptors, labels, artists and decades, and how much
          you go by the RYM crowd. Surprises: ratings it got most wrong while they were held out. How good is it: its held-out error
          against guessing your average and against the RYM rating alone, how well it ranks, and its guesses plotted against yours.
          When it finds no pattern (no better than your average), it says so instead of pretending.

  1.86.0 — 2026-11-07 — Crate Galaxy (⚙ → "Crate Galaxy…"): your whole crate as a map of stars, each release placed near what it shares the most with, and wormholes between any two releases to play as one queue. Page only.
          How close two releases sit comes from their genres (with their parents in your Genre Taxonomy, fainter each level up), RYM
          descriptors, scene, movement, label, artist and decade, each weighted by how rare it is in your crate (tf-idf), so a shared
          descriptor few releases have pulls harder than a shared "Rock". Each release keeps its closest eight, plus two from further
          afield so one artist's albums are never an island; anything still cut off is joined to the rest by its closest member.
          The map forms as you watch: each genre family is held round its own point, links pull, crowding pushes, until it settles.
          A wormhole is the smoothest chain from one star to another: each step costs more the less the two share, so it takes many
          small steps over one big leap. "Play the wormhole" queues the chain in order.
          Colour by genre family, decade, your rating or RYM rating; light up one decade; type to dim all but matching releases
          (Enter flies to the best match); "Surprise me" flies to a random star. Bold releases have a gold ring. Click a star for its
          nearest neighbours; double-click to open it. Drag, scroll and pinch to move and zoom. Nothing here is saved to the sheet.

  1.85.23 — 2026-11-07 — YouTube's "too many requests" (429) is waited out and asked again, and said when it isn't, instead of an unhandled error. Page only.
          A YouTube API request answered 429 now waits as YouTube asks (2, 4,
          8 s when it doesn't say) and is asked again, three times at most;
          then it fails with "YouTube says too many requests: wait a minute,
          then try again". A song-by-song search you start yourself ("Search
          YouTube for the rest", or that choice in the YouTube menu) says why
          it stopped instead of leaving an uncaught error in the console; in
          a run, the YouTube lane stops for Resume. (The Scenes & Movements
          test no longer depends on timing.)

  1.85.22 — 2026-11-07 — Match songs on one release runs YouTube when the add-on answers after it was opened, instead of leaving it "Not run". Page only.
          Match songs on one release holds YouTube back on the API route (it
          costs quota: "Find on YouTube" starts it). Whether the add-on is
          there was decided when the window opened, so opened before the
          add-on had answered the page, YouTube was held back too: "Not
          looked up", every song "Not run", and the YouTube lane "Done" with
          nothing done. Now, when the add-on answers, those releases are
          looked up on YouTube straight away.

  1.85.21 — 2026-11-07 — Match songs looks for a full-release video when no YouTube playlist fits, and links each song to its part of it. Page only.
          YouTube in Match songs now goes: a playlist that fits, else a
          full-release video, else each song searched one by one. The video
          is looked for on the same terms as the song-by-song search: by
          itself through the YouTube add-on, or with an API key when song by
          song is on (one 100-unit search for the release). It needs every
          song's length: each song is linked to its part of the video, back
          to back from the lengths (no intro). A video whose title fits and
          whose length is within 3% of your songs' is confident, ticked like
          any match, and then the song-by-song search isn't needed. The
          YouTube box names the video and how its length compares, and its
          menu lists the other videos found, "Find a full-release video"
          (on request, with the API) and the Full-release video editor.

  1.85.20 — 2026-11-07 — Full-release video: "Find videos" searches YouTube for the whole release and ranks what it finds by how close each video's length is to your songs'. Page only.
          It searches "artist title full album" through the YouTube add-on (no
          quota) or the API (100 units, asked first; remembered like other
          searches). Each video is scored on the release's title and artist
          in its title or channel, words like "Full Album", and its length
          against your songs' total; one much shorter than the release (a
          song, a trailer) is left out. The best six are listed with their
          length and how much longer or shorter they are. Use puts the link
          in and, with no times typed yet, places the songs from their
          lengths; a video longer than your songs suggests setting Intro.

  1.85.19 — 2026-11-07 — Scenes & Movements is one group in the genre list. Page only.
          The group CRATE makes for the Scene and Movement fields was spelled
          "Scenes and Movements"; a genre you filed under your own "Scenes &
          Movements" made a second group beside it. The two spellings are now
          one group, named as your taxonomy names it ("Scenes & Movements"
          when it has none), holding only Scenes and Movements: a genre filed
          straight under the group goes under Scenes (Movements if it is a
          movement by its field or ends in "Movement").

  1.85.18 — 2026-11-07 — Match songs waits out Spotify's "too many requests" (429) instead of giving up on the release. Page only.
          The request queue already waited for Spotify's Retry-After, but
          Match songs never asked again, so the first 429 failed the whole
          release ("Spotify answered 429") while its songs still said
          "Queued". Now a lookup is asked again after the wait (up to four
          times). When Spotify asks for more than a minute, the Spotify lane
          stops with "wait about N minutes, then press Resume" and the
          releases left wait for Resume rather than failing one by one. A
          release whose lookup failed shows the reason on its songs.

  1.85.17 — 2026-11-07 — A release with a full-release video has no red "!" flags. Page only.
          A release counts as having one when its own link is a YouTube
          video, a song's YouTube link says where it ends in a video (as the
          Full-release video dialog saves it), or two songs share one video.
          Its songs and the release itself are then not flagged. A song with
          no time saved in the video is still skipped when played, and the
          message now says so (⋯ → Full-release video…). That dialog also
          starts from the release's own link when it is a YouTube video.

  1.85.16 — 2026-11-07 — A search by artist no longer hides some of a release's songs. Page only.
          "artist:the jesus and m" is read as artist "the" plus the words
          "jesus", "and" and "m". Those words are in the release's own artist
          name, but its songs were still narrowed to the titles that have one
          of them ("Vegetable Man", "Never Understand"), in Quick Peek and the
          Tracks views. Now a word the release itself has (artist, title,
          genres…) doesn't narrow its songs; a track: term, or a word only its
          songs have, still does.

  1.85.15 — 2026-11-07 — Full-release video: the song lengths you have place each song in the video, and an Intro time allows for a video that doesn't start with the first song. Page only.
          LENGTHS: each song's row shows the length you have for it. Opening a
          video that has no times yet, with every song's length known, places
          the songs back to back; "From song lengths" does it again at any
          time. A part more than 3 seconds longer or shorter than its song is
          marked amber.
          INTRO: how long the video runs before the first song (0:12, say).
          Changing it moves every time already there by the same amount, so
          it also works on times pasted or set with ⏱.

          Releases just added from Rate Your Music get their tracklist
          with their first RYM update, a few minutes later. A Match songs
          run that reached them first (the match queue, started by you)
          marked every one "No tracklist" and moved on. Now such a release
          says "Tracklist from RYM first", its RYM update is queued if it

  1.85.0 — 2026-11-05 — Fill blanks from other sources: a new release gets what it is still missing from MusicBrainz, Spotify, iTunes or Discogs, in the order you choose, between its RYM update and Match songs. Page only.
          The order for a new release is now: added, updated from Rate
          Your Music, blanks filled, songs matched. Rate Your Music has no
          song lengths, so they are taken from the sources you put first
          (MusicBrainz, then Spotify, then iTunes to begin with), song by
          song, by title. The release date (or a year made a full date in
          that same year), the label and the cover are filled the same way
          when they are blank. Nothing you have is replaced, and each
          source is asked at most once per release, only when a blank
          needs it.
          The order is in ⚙ → RYM ratings → "Fill blanks from other
          sources": up to three sources per detail, or none. Off: new
          releases go straight to Match songs as before.
          Processing shows the release being filled, the ones waiting for
          their RYM update first, and what was filled under Done today.
          What none of your sources had waits under Needs you: "Pull
          details" opens the side-by-side window for that release, "Skip"
          lets it go. Match songs doesn't wait for those.

  1.84.3 — 2026-11-05 — Releases sent from Rate Your Music that are being added by themselves aren't shown as waiting for your review. Page only.
          With "add releases sent from RYM by themselves" on, a page you

          under Needs you, until all of them had been added. The bar was
          drawn before the automatic import took the batch. Now it is
          drawn after, and a batch being added by itself appears only
          under Running in Processing. With the setting off, the bar is

  1.84.2 — 2026-11-05 — A tracklist filled by an automatic Rate Your Music update no longer turns empty again. Page only.
          An update writes a release's details first and its tracklist
          just after. The quiet sync that keeps CRATE in step with your
          sheet could read the sheet between the two: the row had changed
          (the new details), so all of it was taken, with the tracklist as
          it was before, empty, over the one just saved. In an automatic
          run, with syncs and writes going on together, that happened to
          most releases. The sheet itself was right (reloading showed the
          songs). A sync that reads the sheet while it is being written is
          now set aside and done again once the writes are through.
          Also: the positions from 1.84.0 ("A", "B") are kept when an
          update compares its tracklist with yours.

  1.84.1 — 2026-11-04 — Tracks view: columns keep the width you give them, and a narrow pane scrolls sideways instead of squeezing them. Page only.
          When the columns didn't fit, all of them were squeezed to fit,
          down to their narrowest in a narrow pane, where none could then
          be widened; and starting a drag saved every column at its
          squeezed width, so they stayed narrow once the pane was wide
          again. Now each column keeps its width, the Track name column
          fills what is left (at least 120 pixels), and past that the
          table scrolls sideways. Dragging the Track name column's edge
          right widens it; left narrows it and the column beside it takes
          the room. Widths saved by 1.83.3 start over once.

  1.84.0 — 2026-11-04 — A song is numbered by its place in the tracklist, with the release's own position beside it ("1 A", "2 B"). Page and RYM add-on 1.15.0.
          A song's stored number is what its ratings, links and plays are
          kept under, and after a tracklist was replaced it could run on
          past the list: a two-song single showed 3 and 4. Songs are now
          shown (release view, Album Quick Peek, Tracks view, playlists)
          by their place in the tracklist, 1, 2, 3, and the Tracks view's
          # sorts by it. Beside it, dimmed, is the position the release
          itself gives the song when that says more: "A" and "B" on a
          single, "A1"... on a record, from Rate Your Music (add-on 1.15.0
          on) or MusicBrainz ("2-1" for a second disc). Nothing stored is
          renumbered, so nothing loses its ratings or links.
          Saved tracklists get their positions with their next Rate Your
          Music update (applied by itself when that setting is on); the
          track editor keeps them.

          The artist was judged by the channel alone, so a fan's upload
          ("The Telescopes - Deep Hole Ends" from martian1138) scored as a

          only Likely and was never ticked for you. The artist named at the
          start of the video's title now counts as the artist. Releases

          RESIZING: dragging a column's edge used to take the width from
          the column beside it, and the whole row was then squeezed to fit,

          In the Tracks views (and the queue and mini player fields), both

          PULL DETAILS (a release's menu, "Pull details…"; a first version,
          to see whether it helps, and where it lives may change): RYM (the

          are asked at the same time, and their cover, release date, label,
          genres, tracklist and each song's length are shown beside what you
          have. Click what you want used, detail by detail and song by song:
          RYM has no lengths, so take them from MusicBrainz. What you have
          is picked where you have it; a blank is filled from the first
          source that has it. "Use all" takes everything a source has (your
          songs stay yours, only their lengths are taken). Another source's
          tracklist keeps the links and ratings of songs you already had.
          Nothing changes until Apply, and only what differs is written; a
          cover is saved only if it loads from here.
          UPDATES MOVE INTO PLACE: a release changed by an update (a new
          date, a rating) is sorted again at once, and the cards slide to
          their new places. The quiet sync slides them too, not only when
          something is added.
          RATE YOUR MUSIC NOT ANSWERING: a page that didn't load or couldn't
          be read used to stop the run and hold everything behind it until
          you pressed Continue. Now it is tried again by itself after 1, 2,
          5 and then 10 minutes, and the run and the queue carry on;
          Processing says when, with Try now and Skip it. A check to pass is
          never retried for you.
          Also: a test now checks that every script parses.

  1.82.0 — 2026-11-04 — Drop a CRATE export anywhere on the page to import it. Page only.
          Drag the .json file that Export makes onto CRATE: "Drop to import"
          covers the page, and on letting go the import opens with the file
          already read, at its review (new releases ticked, ones already in
          your crate left out), exactly as "Choose a file…" does. Nothing is
          added until you press Add. Only a drag that carries a file is
          taken, so moving cards and playlists is unchanged; a file that
          isn't an export (a picture, say) is refused with a message, and a
          drop while an import is adding waits for you to drop it again.

  1.81.1 — 2026-11-04 — Match songs shows which YouTube video a kept link is, not just "Your link". Page only.
          A song whose YouTube link you already had now shows that video's
          title and channel ("kept · checked: works" still follows). The
          title comes from YouTube's oEmbed, which needs no key and costs no
          quota, three at a time and once per video; it is remembered in this
          browser. A link check also keeps the titles it reads (with an API
          key, from the same request, at no extra quota). A video YouTube
          won't describe still says "Your link".

  1.81.0 — 2026-11-04 — What the RYM add-on sends goes in without a review, and updates apply what differs too. Page only.
          Two new settings in RYM ratings & genres, both on:
          "Add what the add-on sends": a page sent with "Send to CRATE" is
          added on its own, in the background, as the Add button in its
          review would add it: its new releases only, with one already in
          your crate (or maybe in it) left out. Not while an import of yours
          is open or running, and never the same pages twice: a run that
          fails leaves them in the bar for you.
          "Apply differences too": an update from Rate Your Music changes
          what differs from what you have as well (a genre, a date, a
          tracklist), with nothing left in Review. A rating you typed is
          never replaced. Off, what differs waits for you as before.

  1.80.0 — 2026-11-04 — New releases are updated from Rate Your Music on their own, then their songs matched. Page only.
          A release you add that has a Rate Your Music link (from the
          add-on, an import, or the Add form) now gets its first "Update
          from Rate Your Music" by itself, in the background, as a queued

          to 15 seconds), and behind a run that is already going. Only

  1.79.1 — 2026-11-04 — Releases sent from the RYM add-on keep their cover and RYM link. Page only.
          Both were written on the same line as a comment in the code that
          reads the add-on's batches, so they were part of the comment:
          every batch arrived without a cover or a link, however well the
          add-on had read them, and the import review fell back to a blank
          or a guessed picture. They are read again now, and a cover address
          with an apostrophe in it (RYM leaves "Tomorrow's" as it is) is
          encoded rather than dropped.

  1.79.0 — 2026-11-04 — Adding, deleting and updating no longer reload the whole crate on screen. Page only.
          Until now each change ended with the grid replaced by
          "Cataloguing…" while every release, listen and playlist was fetched
          again, then the whole grid redrawn. Now a deleted release leaves at
          once (the rows below it move up here as they do in the sheet), and
          CRATE checks with your sheet quietly behind it: the grid stays as it
          is, ⟳ turns while it syncs, and only if something really differs is
          it redrawn, keeping your place. Releases that arrive with a sync fade
          in. Several changes in a row share one sync. An open release, the
          player and the queue keep pointing at the same releases through it.
          Opening CRATE and pressing ⟳ still do the full reload; if a quiet
          sync can't reach the sheet you're told, and nothing on screen is
          lost.

  1.78.6 — 2026-11-04 — Deleting looks and feels better: CRATE's own dialog with the covers, and each release fades out as it goes. Page only.
          The browser's plain "OK / Cancel" box is replaced by a dialog that
          fans out the covers of what you're deleting (up to five, "+3" for
          the rest), lists their titles and artists, and says what goes with
          them. Cancel is the default: Escape or a click outside also cancels.
          While they're deleted, the releases waiting their turn are dimmed;
          each one's card shrinks and fades out as it goes and the cards
          around it slide into its place. One that couldn't be deleted shakes
          and comes back. With "reduce motion" switched on, nothing moves.

  1.78.3 — 2026-11-04 — The background check for new releases runs once a day. Page only.

  1.73.0 — 2026-11-04 — A release's Scene and Movement count as genres, under Scenes and Movements > Scenes / Movements: in the genre tree and taxonomy, genre pages, genre filters and searches, grouping, and everything else that reads genres. Page only.

          WHY: Rate Your Music files scenes and movements in its genre tree (Canterbury Scene, Madchester, Rock in Opposition…), so in CRATE they were missing from everywhere genres are used.
          HOW: combinedGenreStr (which every genre list, filter, search, group, chart, export filter and genre page reads) now adds a release's Scene and Movement after its secondary genres
            (sceneGenreStr). With "A genre counts when it's… secondary only" they count as secondary. The deck's genre line shows them in its brackets, and the cover popup's About tab has a Scenes row of genre links.
            Nothing is moved or rewritten: the Scene and Movement fields stay as they are, and editing them changes the genres they give.
          ONE NAME: a scene already in the release's genre lists isn't added twice, and a scene typed as "Canterbury" is the genre "Canterbury Scene" when CRATE knows a genre by that name
            (in the taxonomy or any release's genres; the same for "… Movement"), so both land under one genre.
          WHERE THEY SIT: Scenes and Movements > Scenes > every genre from a Scene field, and Scenes and Movements > Movements > every genre from a Movement field, besides any parents the taxonomy
            gives them (Canterbury Scene can be under Progressive Rock as well). This comes from CRATE's rules (getGenreParents), not saved into your taxonomy: change a release's Scene and its genre moves.
            A genre that is also in another release's genre list still counts as a scene. If your taxonomy has Scenes, Movements or Scenes and Movements, its spelling is used, and parents you give
            Scenes or Movements there replace the Scenes and Movements one. Picking a parent shows every release under it (left panel, genre: search, Play genre); its genre page lists its sub-genres.
          CHECKED in the test copy: the left panel's genre tree (Scenes and Movements > Movements > Madchester, Rock in Opposition; > Scenes > Canterbury Scene, one for a typed "Canterbury" and a RYM
            "Canterbury Scene"), the filter at each level, the genre pages' sub-genres, the secondary-only role, the popup row, and the cost (about 4 ms to work out every release's genres for
            4,000 releases; the genre tree 13 ms).

  1.72.0 — 2026-11-04 — Scene and Movement come from Rate Your Music: adding a release from a RYM page, and "Update from Rate Your Music", now fill them in. Needs RYM add-on 1.13.0.

          BEFORE: the add-on recognised a release's scenes and movements only to keep them out of its genres, and never sent them, so CRATE's Scene and Movement fields stayed as you typed them.
          NOW: the add-on sends them (from the info table's Scene(s) / Movement(s) row, /scene/ and /movement/ links in the release's info, and a genre name ending in "Scene"), CRATE takes them in
            (rymCleanOutbox: bounded text), and the update review offers them like Label: a blank field is filled and ticked; a different one you have is shown and never ticked for you; the same names
            in another order or case aren't offered at all. They're saved with the other ticked fields (editAlbum Scene / Movement). A release added from RYM gets them through the import review as before.
          CHECKED in the test copy: the add-on's reader on a mock info table (Scenes row, Movements row, "Canterbury Scene" genre, a /scene/ link outside the release left out), the review for blank,
            different and same-but-reordered fields, and the save. NOT CHECKED on a real RYM page: the exact markup of RYM's scene rows (the reader looks in all three places for that reason).

  1.71.0 — 2026-11-04 — Export RYM ratings and popularity: in Export your crate, as part of the file or as a spreadsheet. Page only.

          IN THE FILE: a new Include option, "RYM ratings and popularity" (off unless you tick it; remembered like the others). Each release then carries rymRating, rymVotes, rymPopularity
            (0-100, worked out by CRATE: where its rating and its number of ratings both stand among the rated releases of your crate, as the "Most popular (RYM)" sort uses) and rymAdjusted,
            and rymTracks lists its rated tracks: number, title, rating, number of ratings, popularity, adjusted rating, and vsAlbum (against the album's other tracks, with enough rated).
            An import ignores these (it never writes ratings), so such a file still imports anywhere.
          AS A SPREADSHEET: "Ratings as CSV" next to Download saves the releases the dialog lists (with its filters) as a CSV: a row for each release, then a row for each of its rated tracks,
            with RYM rating, number of ratings, popularity, adjusted rating and vs album average. UTF-8 with a byte-order mark so Excel keeps accents; cells are quoted, and one starting with = + - @
            is kept as text, never run as a formula.
          CHECKED in the test copy: the option on and off, the figures in the file for a release and its tracks, the CSV's rows and quoting ("Live, \"Loud\" & =SUM(A1)"), and the file still importing.

  1.70.0 — 2026-11-04 — Fix: "Update from Rate Your Music" now really updates each track's RYM rating and count. Page only; works with RYM add-on 1.12.7 or later, which reads the exact figures.

          THE BUG: the update's review offered "RYM track ratings · already rated (rounded, or an older count)", but applying it (tlApplyTrackRatings) only ever filled a track that had NO RYM rating,
            and changed the count only when the rating was exactly the same. So a track saved earlier as 4.4 from 6,000 (read from the row's rounded "4.4 | 6k") kept that for good, even once
            the add-on could read 4.37 from 6,380.
          NOW: a track's RYM rating is replaced when it came from Rate Your Music (marked rymFrom: 'rym' in its track data whenever an update writes it), or when the saved one is a rounded
            reading of the new one (one decimal place, within 0.05). A different RYM rating you typed under Edit track properties is still never replaced; leaving it as it was there keeps its mark.
            The review offers the same tracks it will change (rymUpdatePlan), and a rating RYM has since moved (4.37 → 4.36) is offered too for tracks marked as from RYM.
          CHECKED in the test copy: 4.4/6,000 → 4.37/6,380 and 4.0/6,000 → 4.05/5,922 applied, an empty one filled, a typed 3.2 left alone, and a later 4.37 → 4.36 applied.

  1.69.0 — 2026-11-04 — Genres you can click in the cover popup and the player, up to four details in the player, and double-clicking a cover works again with the popup open. Page only: no add-on and no backend change.

          GENRES: in the Album covers popup (the genre line at the top, and Genres / Also on its About tab) and in the player's lines (when one of them is set to Genre), each genre name is a link, as in Track Info.
            A click opens that genre's page (and closes the popup); a right-click opens the genre's menu (play, shuffle, export…). In the player this takes the place of the line's own click (artist or track page).
          THE PLAYER'S DETAILS: the player could show two fields under the track title. It now takes four: lines 1 and 2 as before, and a third line with two fields side by side ("2008 · melancholic, nocturnal"),
            hidden when both are empty. Any field can go there: genre, release year, release date, descriptors, label, RYM rating and the rest. Right-click the player → "Choose what the player shows…" opens the settings
            (also at Panels configuration → Now Playing Bar → Panel Settings…). Now Playing Bar, when placed in Main, shows the same third line.
          DOUBLE-CLICK IN THE COVERS VIEW (it plays the release): with the inline popup, the first click opened the popup and at once scrolled it into view, and opening it on a cover below one that already had
            the popup also pulled that cover up (the old popup shrank and moved away). Either way the second click hit a different cover. Now the clicked cover is scrolled back to exactly where it was,
            and the popup only scrolls into view after 500 ms (Windows' double-click time), not at all if it was closed by then.
          CHECKED in the test copy (4,000 releases): genre click and right-click in the player and the popup; the player's third line and its hiding; the settings dialog from the right-click; a cover under an open
            inline popup stays put (inline and floating styles) and a double-click on it plays it and closes the popup; a popup on the bottom row still scrolls into view, after the wait.

  1.68.0 — 2026-11-04 — Install CRATE as an app on your PC: its own window, a Start-menu entry and a taskbar icon. Upload manifest.webmanifest and the icons folder next to index.html; no add-on and no backend change.

          HOW: in Chrome or Edge, open CRATE and use the install icon at the right of the address bar, or ⚙ → "Install CRATE as an app…". It then opens without the browser's bars, has the record icon in the Start menu and on the taskbar, and can be pinned.
          WHY THIS KIND: it is still this page at your GitHub Pages address, so Google sign-in, Spotify, YouTube, the RateYourMusic and YouTube add-ons and your saved settings all work exactly as before, and it updates itself whenever you upload a new index.html.
            (A separate .exe would run from a different address, which breaks the sign-in, the Spotify login and the add-ons.)
          NEW FILES, to upload beside index.html: manifest.webmanifest (name, colours, icons) and icons/ (crate-192.png, crate-512.png, crate-maskable-512.png, apple-touch-icon.png, favicon-32.png: a record with an amber label). The page now also has a tab icon.
          ⚙ → "Install CRATE as an app…" shows in the browser and is hidden inside the installed app; when the browser has no install offer ready, it says where the install icon is.

  1.67.0 — 2026-11-04 — Fix: the covers view no longer freezes. Hovering a cover, clicking one and scrolling are smooth again with a big crate, and the grid draws in about a seventh of the time. Page only: no add-on and no backend change.

          THE CAUSES, measured in a test copy with 4,000 releases at 1280x800 (medians of many runs; before = 1.64.0, which has the same grid as 1.65.0):
            1. Hovering: 1.60.0 gave every card content-visibility (so cards off screen weren't laid out) and switched it off on a hovered card so its lift wasn't clipped. That switch made the browser lay out the whole grid again,
               each time the mouse entered or left a cover: 315 ms per cover. Moving across the covers froze the page.
            2. Every card was built, about 50 elements each: 147,000 elements on the page, and every change to it was paid for over all of them.
            3. Ten CSS :has() rules (body:has(.mini-player.docked-top:not([hidden])) and others) made the browser re-check the whole page's styles whenever any element was shown, hidden, added or removed: 57 ms each time.
            4. The covers' scrolling area (#gridWrap) sat in flexible panels that measure their contents, so a change to one card made the browser measure the whole grid through them.
          THE FIXES: only the cards near the screen are built (buildGridSection: the first 120 of each drawing; the rest are placeholders of the same size and class, built as they come within about two screens of view and emptied again
            when far away; the cards on screen are built at once after a redraw that keeps your place). The hover switch and content-visibility are gone. The :has() rules are classes now (body.mp-docked-top, body.dialog-open, body.qa-open,
            .topbar.search-focus / .search-has-value), kept current by a few observers on just those elements (bodyStateClasses). #gridWrap is contained (contain: strict; its size comes from the panels, never from the covers).
          BEFORE → AFTER: drawing the grid 1,532 → about 200 ms; elements on the page 146,800 → 33,400; hovering a cover (on and off) 315 → about 20 ms; clicking a cover and closing its popup 517 → about 115 ms;
            showing or hiding anything 57 → 0 ms of style work; long freezes while scrolling 3 (197 ms in all) → none. A scroll step set from code measured about 24 ms both before and after (the test window's own cost).
          CHECKED in the test copy: selection (click, shift-click across placeholders, Ctrl+A on all 4,000), keeping your place across a redraw, the cover popup in all three styles and ‹ › onto a placeholder, grouping by year (60 groups),
            list view, and phone width (the covers area keeps its size). NOT CHECKED: a playlist's own covers view (the test crate has no playlists; it uses the same drawing), and real cover images (decoding them costs extra on any version).

  1.66.0 — 2026-11-04 — Staying signed in: CRATE renews your Google sign-in quietly before its hour runs out, and a save or refresh that is refused is renewed and sent again, instead of sending you back to the sign-in screen. Page only: the backend is unchanged (no re-deploy).

          THE CAUSE: CRATE signs in with Google, and the token Google gives lasts ONE HOUR. Every request to your sheet carries it and nothing renewed it, so an hour after signing in the next save or refresh came back "unauthorized" and CRATE showed
            "Your session expired — sign in again". Opening CRATE more than an hour after the last sign-in also started at the sign-in screen.
          NOW: about five minutes before the token runs out CRATE asks Google for a new one (authRenew: Google's automatic sign-in, auto_select, so there is nothing to click while you are still signed in to Google in this browser; scheduleAuthRenew sets the timer
            from the token's own expiry). A request whose token is about to run out renews first. A save (postUpdate) or a refresh (fetchCatalog) the backend refuses as unauthorized is renewed and sent again once: the change you made isn't lost.
            The sign-in screen only comes back when Google won't give a new token.
          LIMITS: Google can hold back its automatic sign-in (signed out of Google, third-party sign-in blocked in the browser, or a pause after you closed its prompt), and a tab left in the background may renew late; the retry on refusal covers that
            for the next thing you do. Opening CRATE after a long break still asks you to sign in if the stored token has run out and Google won't renew it at once.
          THE LASTING FIX (not done: it needs a backend change you would re-deploy): after checking your Google sign-in once, the backend could give CRATE its own session that lasts weeks. The backend file kept on this computer (Downloads) is an old
            version without sign-in, so it can't be changed from here.
          VERIFIED in a test copy with a fake Google sign-in and a backend that refuses old tokens: a refused save renewed and sent again (it went through), a refused refresh renewed and loaded again, a token about to run out renewed before sending,
            the timer renewing by itself (60 minutes more), and, when Google won't renew, the sign-in screen as before. NOT VERIFIED with real Google sign-in.

            rating matched exactly. The row says "already rated (rounded, or an older count)"; a rating that moved by more than 0.05 is still left as it is.

  1.63.0 — 2026-11-04 — Fix: a release's number of RYM ratings no longer flips between the real figure, a tiny one and blank as you browse RateYourMusic. Needs RYM add-on 1.12.5 for the full fix; the backend is unchanged.

          THE CAUSE: the add-on reads a release's ratings count on every RYM page you browse (its own page, an artist's discography, genre lists), and the newest sighting won. A page that writes the count shortened ("6k", "1.2m") was read as 0 by the add-on's list reader and as 6 by CRATE's,
            and a 0 replaced the real count. So a release saved with 6,123 ratings from its own page could become 6, then blank, then a real figure again, depending on what you looked at last. CRATE also read a typed "7.000" or "7 000" as 7, and "6k" as 6.
          NOW, in CRATE: rymVotesNum reads "7,000", "7.000", "7 000", "6k", "6.1k", "1.2m". What the add-on sends (rymPlan): a sighting with no count keeps the count you have; a shortened count that the exact one fits (add-on 1.12.5 says how rough it is) keeps the exact one;
            and a count under 2% of the one you have (what an older add-on makes of "6k") is ignored, so even add-on 1.12.3 can no longer blank or shrink a count. A real change (a new exact count, a rating that moved) still comes through.
          NOW, in the add-on (1.12.5): the same reading of shortened counts, and its own store keeps the known count the same way, so the trail of how a rating changes no longer records the flips. With your own rating selector set (its Settings), the release page's count is now read too (it was always 0).
            It also drops scenes from genre lists, which CRATE 1.58.2 described as add-on 1.12.4: that version was never saved with CRATE's add-ons, so 1.12.5 is built on 1.12.3 with that change made again.
          COUNTS ALREADY WRONG in your sheet (a 6, or blank): the next time you open that release's own RYM page (or run Update from Rate Your Music on it) the exact count replaces it.
          VERIFIED: the add-on's reading and merging, old against new, on the sequence release page (6,123), artist page ("6k"), list with no count, release page (6,300): old 6123 → 6 → 0 → 6300, new 6123 → 6123 → 6123 → 6300; ten count formats; scene names;
            and CRATE's rymPlan on: no count, "6k" from 1.12.5, 6 from an older add-on, a real new count, a small release whose count really fell (40 → 38), a moved rating with no count. NOT VERIFIED on a real RateYourMusic page (it asked the test browser for a human check).

          ONE WINDOW: every "Match songs" on a saved release (the card menu, the tracklist, a red ! link flag, Edit tracks) opens it, straight on that release; a selection, ⚙ → "Match songs…", or a genre's menu ("Match songs in this genre…") opens it on Start. If a run is open, a release you match joins it.

          START: a health check (Spotify connected? the YouTube add-on and how many pages it reads at once, or your API key; and with the add-on, one test YouTube search: "read 0 videos" says YouTube may have changed its page). Which releases (your selection, the view, a genre, the match queue),

          QUEUE: every release with a bar per service (green matched/linked/ticked, amber needs a look, red none) and a status (Queued, Working, N to check, Tracklist differs, No tracklist, Ready, Applied). Filter, search, sort (needs a look first), and for selected releases:
            tick confident, re-run, skip, include, move to top; "Export unmatched (CSV)" lists every song with nothing ticked and why.

            play it, compare with the other service's match, and "Never offer this". Rules: accept all above 60–80, untick live versions and remixes, untick all. Apply this release on its own.
            KEYBOARD: J/K song, H/L service, 1–5 pick, X none, Space play, W why, A apply, N next release that needs a look, [ ] previous/next release.
          CHECK LINKS: asks Spotify (several tracks at once, for your country) and YouTube (with an API key, 1 unit per 50 videos; without one, YouTube's oEmbed) whether each saved link still plays: removed, not playable here, deleted or private, embedding off, blocked in your country,
            or a Spotify track replaced by a new id (its new id is offered as the replacement). A broken link is matched again like a missing one; only replacements for broken links are ticked. Preferences: a reminder once a month.
          ANALYTICS: this run (matched, % confident, requests made, times Spotify asked to slow down, a histogram of best scores with the "confident from" threshold, by service, and why songs weren't matched), or the whole crate (share of songs with a Spotify / YouTube link,
            songs that can't play, the least playable genres and decades, over the releases whose tracklist CRATE has on hand) with "Match the releases with unplayable songs".

          PREFERENCES: confident-from threshold (85), length tolerance, edition (original / remastered), marking down live/remix/cover/sped-up versions, preferring "- Topic" channels, putting new releases in the match queue, the monthly reminder; and what CRATE has learned:
            candidates you said never to offer, and a YouTube channel you chose for an artist (preferred for that artist from then on).
          THE MATCH QUEUE (crate_match_queue_v1): releases added from a card's menu ("Add to the match queue"), releases with a song the player skipped ("no link saved" / "not found on Spotify"), and new releases however they were added (Preferences). It is matched when you choose it on Start (it doesn't run on its own).
          SCORING: smScore now returns its parts and reads the preferences; Spotify and YouTube lookups keep every edition and playlist they read (editions, lists), so changing one doesn't look anything up again.
