  1.61.0 — 2026-11-03 — Match songs for many releases works on several releases at once (Spotify 4, YouTube 3 unless you change it), not one by one. Parallel YouTube pages need the YouTube add-on 1.3.0 (crate-youtube-addon.zip); an older add-on still works, one page at a time. No backend change.

          WHERE: the setup screen of "Match songs for many releases" has "Releases at once" under each service (1, 2, 3, 4, 6 or 8; remembered on this device, crate_bm_parallel_v1). The time estimates take it into account.
          HOW: each service's lane runs that many workers, each taking the next waiting release (bmRunLane; releases finish in whatever order their lookups come back). Pause, Stop and Resume work as before: Stop lets the releases already being looked up finish, then stops; a lane that stops
            (Spotify not connected, a session that expired) leaves the rest waiting and Resume carries on with them (lane.run).
          SPOTIFY: every Spotify request used to go through a strict one-at-a-time queue. It is now a pool (spotifyApiPump): one at a time as before, except while a batch runs, when it allows as many as the releases at once, with starts 80 ms apart (SPOTIFY_PARALLEL_GAP_MS). A 429 still makes every
            request wait out Retry-After. Back to one at a time when the lane finishes.
          YOUTUBE: with an API key, that many releases are looked up at once (it only uses the day's quota sooner). Through the add-on, how many PAGES it reads at once is the add-on's own setting: version 1.3.0 adds Options → Speed → "Pages at once" (1 to 4, 3 unless changed; only the add-on's
            page can set it, never CRATE). The add-on tells CRATE the number and the setup screen shows it. Pages it has to open in a tab are still one at a time.
          THE RISK, which is why it was one at a time: more at once makes it likelier that Spotify answers "slow down" (then everything waits as long as it says) and that YouTube shows the add-on a check (it stops until you Continue). If either keeps happening, lower the numbers.
          VERIFIED with Spotify and the YouTube API faked (400 ms an answer): 12 releases took 20.6 s one at a time and 4.4 s with 4 and 3 at once, with the same 36 Spotify requests and the same 117 confident matches; at most 4 Spotify and 3 YouTube requests in flight; Spotify back to one at a time after;
            Spotify not connected stops only that lane and Resume finishes it; Stop. NOT VERIFIED: the add-on's new background.js in a real browser, and real Spotify/YouTube at this speed.

  1.60.1 — 2026-11-03 — Fix: pressing Next could leave the old track playing while the queue showed the new one, after which the queue stopped advancing. No add-on and no backend change.

          WHAT HAPPENED: the queue moved to the new track at once, but the old one played on until the new one was ready (a Spotify lookup can take seconds). Three things could go wrong in that time:
            the old track ending counted as the end of the queue's track, so the queue advanced twice and skipped one; a Spotify start from an earlier press could finish late and begin playing over a YouTube or SoundCloud track chosen after it (the queue then showed one track while another played);
            and if Spotify refused the new track, the old one played on, and when it ended nothing advanced, because the queue was waiting to hear from the new one.
          NOW: the old track is paused the moment the queue moves on (pauseForQueueSwitch), every queue step takes a play intent so a late Spotify start from an earlier press is dropped, and a Spotify start that fails is tried once more after 1.5 seconds (startCrateQueueSpotify).
            If it still fails, a message says so, and Play tries that track again (crateQueueStalledIndex) instead of resuming the paused old one; Next skips it. showMiniPlayer and playSpotifyUri now say whether Spotify accepted the request.

  1.60.0 — 2026-11-03 — Speed: playing a genre with thousands of releases no longer slows the page down, and a big crate opens, redraws and plays much faster. No add-on and no backend change.

          PLAYING A BIG GENRE (or All Albums, the mood picker, the map's Play): it used to look up the tracklist of EVERY release at once and put every track in the queue, redrawing Up Next over and over as they came in (with 2,666 releases, each redraw took longer and longer). Now it starts on the first release
            and keeps only about 60 tracks loaded ahead of the one playing, loading more as the queue moves on (crateQueuePending, crateQueueTopUp; topped up from playCrateQueueIndex). Shuffle on/off also reorders the releases not loaded yet. Clear and a new Play drop them.
          UP NEXT draws the next 150 tracks and then a line, "+ N more tracks, M more releases to come" (QUEUE_PANEL_MAX_ROWS). It is redrawn on every track change, so this keeps that quick.
          COVERS: applyCachedArt searched the whole page once for every cached cover; with 4,000 releases that was over 20 seconds, and it ran on every grid redraw and on every track change (the track info and Last.fm history panels call it). It is now one pass over the page's images (about 20 ms).
            applyCachedArtFor (a cover that just arrived) gathers the covers arriving together into one such pass. Startup with a 4,000-release test crate: about 28 seconds frozen before, about 2.4 now.
          COVER LOOKUPS: each card without a cover started its own iTunes lookup the moment it was drawn, so a big crate fired thousands at once (iTunes refuses that many, and the tracklist lookups for playing had to wait behind them). They now take turns, 4 at a time (artLookupSlot).
          THE GRID: cards off screen are not laid out until they come near it (content-visibility on .grid > .card, with the first card's real height as the stand-in, --card-h): layout of 4,000 cards went from about 2.6 seconds to under 0.2. A hovered or focused card is left out of that, so its lift and shadow aren't clipped.

  1.59.0 — 2026-11-02 — Export to Obsidian: the crate as a zip of Markdown notes (releases, artists, genres, labels) to unzip into a vault. No add-on and no backend change.

          WHERE: ⚙ menu → "Export to Obsidian…" (right after "Export crate…"). Choose Everything or What's showing now, tick whether to include tracklists and your reviews, press Export: it downloads crate-obsidian-<date>.zip. Unzip it into the vault: everything lands in a folder called CRATE. One way: nothing in CRATE changes, and nothing is read back from the vault.
          WHAT IT MAKES: CRATE/Releases (one note per release), CRATE/Artists, CRATE/Genres, CRATE/Labels and a CRATE.md index. The relationships are PROPERTIES on the notes, named for what they are, so Obsidian's graph, backlinks and Bases can use them: a release has artist (a link), genres and secondary_genres (lists of links), labels (a list of links); a genre has parent (links, from YOUR GENRE TAXONOMY, so the genre tree shows in the graph; a parent that only the taxonomy mentions gets a note too) and a Sub-genres list;
            an artist has genres (the ones its releases carry, most used first). Plain facts are properties too: year, released (a real date, when the release has a full one), format, catalog (CRATE's automatic CRT-007 numbers are left out), your rating, rym_rating, rym_ratings, rym_rank, descriptors, region, location, rym, link, cover, added, and a tag (crate/release and so on).
          THE BODY of a release note: its title, the artist link, the cover picture, "My review" with your review exactly as you wrote it, and a Tracklist table (side, title, length, your rating as stars, the RYM rating with its count, your note, links). A genre note carries your description and the Rate Your Music text, quoted with its source, then its sub-genres and releases. Artist and label notes list their releases by year.
          NAMES: every note name is unique across the whole export, so a [[link]] can never go to the wrong note: where two kinds want one name the earlier keeps it (genres, then labels, artists, releases) and the other becomes "Pop (artist)" and the link still SHOWS "Pop". "/" and ":" in a name become "-", other characters Obsidian can't have in a link are dropped; two editions with the same artist, title and year become "... (2)". A release on two labels ("Sire / Warner Bros.") is listed under both.
          TRACKLISTS: only a tracklist you keep yourself, or one already loaded, is included: the export makes NO web lookups (checked), so a release whose tracklist is looked up automatically has none until you have opened it. The zip is a plain archive with nothing compressed (browsers have no zip writer; the notes are text and small).
          LIMITS AND WHAT IS NOT INCLUDED: more than 65,000 notes will not fit one zip (it says so and suggests What's showing now). Not exported: your diary, scrobbles, playlists, the queue, and a note per TRACK. Exporting again makes the same notes afresh: write your own notes elsewhere in the vault and link to these, or they will be overwritten if you unzip over them.
          BUGS FOUND BY THE TESTS WHILE BUILDING IT, now fixed: Escape did nothing once the download had taken the cursor; an automatic catalogue number would have been exported as if real; and a test gap (a name with a double quote or backslash) that would have let invalid properties through.
          VERIFIED: 27 unit checks on the notes and the zip (every note's properties read back with a real YAML parser, every [[link]] resolved, every name unique, names with quotes, colons, slashes, accents and Japanese, the zip checked by Python's zip reader and the system's unzip, the standard CRC-32 value, 3,000 notes) and 16 browser checks (the menu entry, the dialog, a real download opened as a zip, the properties, the tracklist table, the taxonomy as parents, two-label releases, both options, What's showing now, no web requests, Escape, no changes to the crate). Breaking six things (name collisions, quoting, the genre parent,
            the zip's checksum, the scope choice, the tracklist option) fails a test. The earlier suites still pass.
          NOT VERIFIED: IN OBSIDIAN ITSELF. I checked the notes with a YAML parser and my own link checker, not by opening them in Obsidian: that links in properties show as links, and that a Base can sort by these properties, are as Obsidian documents them, not something I have seen. A real crate (thousands of releases) was not tried: only small ones. THE EARLIER FULL REGRESSION (109 suites) STILL CANNOT BE RE-RUN; the earlier suites I rebuilt pass.


  1.58.2 — 2026-11-02 — Scenes are no longer pulled into a release's genres: RYM add-on 1.12.4, and CRATE drops them from what it receives.

          THE CAUSE (from the Diagnose report you sent, for Joni Mitchell's "Blue"): the add-on had read "Singer-Songwriter, Contemporary Folk, Laurel Canyon Scene" as the Genre list. RYM lists a release's Scenes in a row of their own (the report's headings show Genres, then Scenes), with links under /genre/ addresses just like genres, and the add-on accepted any link inside an element carrying the genre classes without asking which row it was in.
          THE FIX, in the add-on: a link in a row headed Scenes or Movements is no longer read as a genre, and any name ending in "Scene" is dropped from a genre list by the add-on's cleaner (which also covers a scene link sitting in the Genres row itself). In CRATE: the same name rule when it receives genres, so a scene from an add-on you have not updated is dropped too ("Scenecore" and names like "Madchester" are genres and are kept). "Diagnose this page" now shows the markup of the genre, scene and movement rows, which the old report cut off before reaching.
          RELEASES YOU ALREADY HAVE: run Update from Rate Your Music on them. A release whose Genre or Secondary genre contains a scene gets a row showing the clean list beside yours. It is a "differs" row, so it is NOT ticked for you: nothing is removed unless you tick it.
          NOT DONE: the scene is dropped, not filed anywhere. CRATE has a Scene field; filling it from RYM's Scenes row is a separate change I can make if you want it.
          TO USE IT: install add-on 1.12.4 (a new zip and folder: remove the old one, load the new folder, reconnect it to your CRATE address in its options, reload CRATE). The page change alone already drops scenes whose name ends in "Scene" (Laurel Canyon Scene) when you run an update, but not a scene named like a genre ("Madchester"): that needs the add-on.
          VERIFIED: the real add-on in a real browser, the previous version as the control and the new one, on pages built from the start of your Joni Mitchell markup with the Scenes row marked up in three ways (reusing the genre classes; the scene link inside the Genres row; a scene named "Madchester"): the control reproduces your symptom, the new add-on reads Singer-Songwriter, Contemporary Folk and Folk Pop in every case, and a release with no scenes reads identically in both.   1.58.1 — 2026-11-02 — A release's full list of genres: the RYM add-on (1.12.3) no longer cuts it to four, and CRATE keeps it whole.

          THE CAUSE (from the Diagnose report you sent, for Sweet Trip's "Seen / Unseen"): a release page lists its genres in two groups, primary and secondary, and either can be long. The add-on kept only the FIRST FOUR of each, and CRATE then cut whatever arrived at 200 characters (which could slice a name in half). Slacker Rock sits in that page's secondary group after the fourth entry (Glitch Pop, IDM, Neo-Psychedelia, ...), so it never reached you.
          THE FIX: the add-on reads both lists whole (primary into Genre, secondary into Secondary genre, as CRATE has always kept them): up to 30 names each, each once, in RYM's order, no name cut. Chart and artist list pages too (their rows used the same four-name limit). CRATE takes them whole as well. The add-on's "Diagnose this page" now shows the secondary genres it read (your report couldn't, which is why the second list was invisible in it).
          RELEASES YOU ALREADY HAVE: run Update from Rate Your Music on them again. Where your Genre or Secondary genre is EXACTLY the first four names of RYM's longer list (the fingerprint of the old limit), the full list is offered as a ticked "refine" row. Any other difference is still a "differs" row that is never ticked for you, so a list you trimmed yourself is not quietly extended; an empty field is filled as before.
          TO USE IT: install add-on 1.12.3 (a new zip and folder; remove the old one and load the new folder, then reconnect it to your CRATE address in its options and reload CRATE). The page change alone does nothing for the pull, because the cut was made in the add-on.
          VERIFIED: the real add-on, old 1.12.2 as the control and new 1.12.3, loaded in a real browser on a page built from the markup in your report (the control reproduces your symptom exactly: it keeps Glitch Pop, IDM, Neo-Psychedelia, Chillwave and loses the rest; the new one keeps all 17, over 200 characters, and six primary genres instead of four); the add-on's genre rules in 8 unit checks (and the old core.js fails them); the page keeping long lists whole and the update plan's rule in 11 checks. Breaking five things (the reader's limit, the add-on's cut, the page's cut, the refine rule and its first-four test) fails a test.
          NOT VERIFIED: a real Rate Your Music page (the sandbox has no network): the fixture uses your report's real markup, but your report cut the page's secondary list off after "Neo-Psyche", so the names after it are my stand-ins; whether RYM ever shows a list longer than the 30 this keeps; the update plan on your real sheet. THE EARLIER FULL REGRESSION (109 suites) STILL CANNOT BE RE-RUN (those files were lost when my working files were cleared); the earlier suites I rebuilt (release features, YouTube page, track editor) pass.


  1.58.0 — 2026-11-02 — Three changes to releases: no single-release song match when several are selected; "Selected genre only" for the genre you pick; sort (and show) releases by popularity. No add-on and no backend change.

          1. SONG MATCH: with two or more releases selected, right-clicking one of them now offers only "Match songs for N selected…". The single-release "Match songs to Spotify / YouTube…" is gone from that menu (it is still there with one or none selected, and when you right-click a release that is not in the selection). "Full-release video…" and "Edit tracks…" are still listed with several selected: they act on the release you clicked.
          2. SELECTED GENRE ONLY: ⇅ menu → Genre search → "Selected genre only (not its sub-genres)" (off by default, so nothing changes until you tick it, and it is remembered). With it on, the genre you pick in the left panel shows only releases tagged with THAT genre, not ones filed under it in the taxonomy (Rock shows Rock, not Alternative Rock or Shoegaze). It is applied everywhere that pick is used: the grid, "Closest genre match", the genre weight bar, the figures beside it, and
            the left list's COUNTS (each genre's number becomes its own releases, so a number never promises what clicking it can't show; a parent with none of its own stays as a heading). The panel title says "(not sub-genres)". Not touched, because they have their own choices: genre: terms typed in the search box ("Exact genre only / Include sub-genres" buttons just below), and the Advanced Filter dialog's own sub-genres tick.
          3. POPULARITY FOR RELEASES: ⇅ menu → "Most popular (RYM)" (also in the playlist sort) and a new field "RYM popularity" (pick it like any field; it shows "Popularity 83"). It is the track popularity worked out for releases: where a release's number of RYM ratings and its adjusted rating (pulled toward your library's average until it has about 25 ratings, so a 4.8 from 12 people doesn't beat a 4.0 from 30,000) stand among ALL the rated releases of your library, combined so it is high only if it
            is high on both. Level ones are ordered by how many rated them. A release needs BOTH a rating and a count (from "Update from Rate Your Music") to have one; the rest come last. It is relative to your library and shifts as you add releases; with fewer than five rated releases the adjusted rating orders them. The 25 is a judgment, not a measurement.
          VERIFIED: 27 browser checks and 3 unit checks: the menu in all four selection cases and the batch starting; the genre option (the grid, counts, title, a sub-genre, turning it off, closest genre match, remembered after a reload); the popularity sort against an independent calculation, how it re-ranks when the data changes, and the field text.           WHERE: the deck's "Edit tracks…" button (next to "Full-release video…") and "Edit tracks…" in a release's right-click menu. The existing tracklist builder and the per-track properties dialog are untouched and still there.

            with data but no title), and Save stays off until there are none. Nothing is saved until Save. Undo and Redo cover the whole session. Find in titles narrows the rows. Cancel or Escape asks before discarding changes.
          SPREADSHEET HABITS: Tab and Enter move between cells (Enter down the column, Shift+Enter up, the arrow keys too); Alt+Up / Alt+Down move a track; Ctrl+D fills down (from the cell above, or from the first selected track to the others); pasting several lines into a cell fills down the column and adds tracks at the end; drag the handle to reorder.

          MATCH LINKS…: hides the editor, opens the song matcher for this release, and when it closes brings back the links it saved, except in cells you have edited yourself.
          HOW A SAVE WORKS: ONE request writes the tracklist and the track data together, so it is saved whole or not at all (if the sheet refuses, the editor stays open with your changes). Only what changed is written. Two traps are handled: a link saved in a track's own data OVERRIDES the tracklist's link and an empty one falls through to it, so clearing a link clears it in both places (otherwise the old link reappears
            after a reload); and a title or length typed over an old override removes the override. A length you did not touch is kept to the millisecond. A release whose tracklist comes from an automatic lookup is only turned into a tracklist you keep yourself if you change a title, length, side or the order or add or remove a track (the footer says so and Save asks); changing only ratings, genre, notes, ticks or links does not.

  1.56.0 — 2026-10-29 — Track popularity, worked out from Rate Your Music's rating of a track and the number of ratings behind it: three new columns for the Tracks view. No add-on and no backend change.

          DOES IT MAKE SENSE? Partly, and the useful way to do it is not one blended number. A rating says how well a track is liked and the number of ratings says how many people have reached it; popularity is mostly the second. Three things shape the design. (1) A high rating from a handful of people means little (5.0 from 3 people should not beat 4.5 from 5,000), so ratings are ADJUSTED toward the library's average until a track has enough
            ratings behind it. (2) Within one album, the counts of its tracks are nearly the same ("6k", "6k", "5k" on Rubber Soul), because the same people rate the whole album: across your library a count mostly says how well-known the ALBUM is, and what tells its tracks apart is their rating. (3) Counts of thousands are rounded by Rate Your Music, so small differences between big counts mean little.
          WHERE: the three new Tracks-view columns (choose them like any column): "Adjusted rating (RYM)", "Popularity (RYM)" and "Vs album (RYM)" (all sort as numbers, tracks without a value last). I also renamed the two RYM columns from 1.51.0 to "Track rating (RYM)" and "Rating count (RYM)", because with five columns the old names were cut to the same words.
          ADJUSTED RATING: (ratings x the track's rating + 25 x the library's average track rating) / (ratings + 25). With the library's average at 3.96, 4.5 from 3 people becomes 4.01, 4.6 from 5,800 stays 4.60 and 3.0 from 15 becomes 3.60.
          POPULARITY, 0 to 100: where the track stands among all the rated tracks of YOUR library on number of ratings, and on adjusted rating, combined as the geometric mean of the two, so it is high only if the track is high on both (no weighting to argue about). It is RELATIVE TO YOUR LIBRARY: a good track on a little-known album scores low, and the numbers shift as you add releases. Shown only once the library has five tracks with both numbers.
          VS ALBUM: how far the adjusted rating is above (+) or below (−) the average of its own album's rated tracks, e.g. +0.37 for the standout; it is only given for an album with three or more rated tracks. It is what finds the best track on an album.
          ONLY tracks with BOTH a rating and a count are worked out; a rating you typed yourself, or a rating with no count, gives a blank, and does not affect anyone else's number. The counts come from the add-on (1.12.0 or later) when a release is updated from Rate Your Music: tracks you haven't updated since then have none.
          VERIFIED: the maths in 14 unit checks (percentiles with ties, the adjusted rating against the formula, low counts pulled toward the average, popularity needing both, ordering, vs-album summing to zero, what is left out, the five-track minimum) and 11 browser checks against a second, independent calculation of the same rules (every number shown, blanks, the 4.5 from 3 people, the album rules, numeric sorting both ways, the numbers following changed data). Breaking the adjustment, the combining, the comparison with the album
            or the one-second cache each fails a test. NOT VERIFIED: whether these numbers match your sense of which tracks are popular on your real library. The constants (25 ratings of prior weight, the three- and five-track minimums) are judgments, not measurements: if the ranking looks wrong to you, tell me which tracks and I'll look at it. NOT BUILT: the same for whole albums, a list of the most popular tracks, and using it to order playlists or shuffles.


  1.55.0 — 2026-10-29 — Export and import the genre taxonomy, and a visible, safer sync to your account, so it persists across browsers and devices. No add-on and no backend change.

          WHAT I FOUND: the taxonomy was already meant to follow you: every edit is saved to your account (the spreadsheet), and every browser takes that copy when it loads the catalog. Two things made that unreliable, and both would look exactly like "it doesn't persist". (1) A save that failed (a backend script older than 1.32, a taxonomy too long for it, a busy sheet, no connection) left only a console message: nothing told you
            that this browser's copy was now the only one. (2) On every catalog load the account's copy REPLACED this browser's, so after a failed save the next load swapped your newer edits for the account's older copy, on any browser. I can't see your account, so I can't tell which of these happened to you.
          WHERE: ⚙ → Genre Taxonomy now starts with "Back up, move and sync": Export to a file (crate-genre-taxonomy-<date>.json), Copy as text, Import…, Save to my account now, and Undo the last import (once you have imported). Under the buttons a note says how many genres, how big, and whether the account has this browser's copy: "Saved to your account on …", "Changes not saved yet", or "NOT saved to your account: the last try failed (why)",
            or that it is too long for an old spreadsheet script (update apps-script-backend.gs).
          IMPORT: choose a file or paste the copied text, press Read it, and it says how it compares with yours (new, different, the same, only yours) before anything changes, with three ways to bring it in and the result of each: (1) add what's new, keep mine where we differ (the default: a missing description of mine is filled in, nothing else of mine changes); (2) add what's new and use the imported version where we differ (a description of yours is
            never blanked by an import that has none); (3) replace my whole taxonomy (it asks first, because genres only you have are removed). Your taxonomy as it was is kept first (on this device), so any import can be undone. An import is checked: every entry is rebuilt from the few fields it may have with their sizes capped, files that aren't a taxonomy (or are from a newer CRATE) are refused with a reason, at most 5,000 genres are read,
            and a file that would put a genre under itself (A under B under C under A) has the one link that closes the loop left out, and says so. It also accepts a plain taxonomy without the envelope.
          SAFER SYNC: (a) a failed save is now recorded and said once; "Save to my account now" tries again; (b) if this browser has changes the account never received, loading the catalog now KEEPS them and sends them again instead of replacing them with the account's older copy; (c) a browser that has its own taxonomy (made before this version, never sent) when the account has none puts it there once, so the next browser gets it; a browser still on the default sends nothing.
          THE TRADE-OFF in (b): if two browsers both have unsent changes, each keeps its own and the last to send wins on the account: nothing is lost silently, but they won't merge by themselves. Export from one and Import into the other (option 1) to combine them. Not built: automatic merging between browsers, and saving single genres.
          VERIFIED: the export, parsing, comparing, merging and loop-cutting logic in 19 unit checks; the dialog, a real file download, a real file import, paste, the three ways of importing, undo, refusals, a loop, a failing account (message once, note, unsent flag), local changes surviving a catalog load, retry, taking another browser's copy, the first send, no send from the default, and the too-long note in 26 browser checks. Breaking each of six things (keeping unsent changes, recording a failure, the keep-mine merge,
            undo, the first send, loop cutting) fails a test. NOT VERIFIED: against your real account and backend script: if "Save to my account now" fails for you, the note under the buttons gives the backend's own reason; send it to me.

  1.53.0 — 2026-10-28 — Match songs for many releases: select several releases, have their songs matched on Spotify and YouTube at the same time, and choose exactly which matches are kept. No add-on and no backend change.

          HOW YOU START IT: select two or more releases (click, shift-click, Ctrl+A; a bar appears: "N selected · Match songs… · Clear"), or right-click one of them ("Match songs for N selected…"), or ⚙ → "Match songs for many releases…" (which offers the current view when nothing is selected).

            or "Save the confident matches as they come, and let me choose the rest".
          THE RUN: each service is its own LANE working through the releases at its own pace, at the same time. It uses the song matcher's own lookups, so the results are the ones "Match songs" would give for each release. A lane has its own progress card. If YouTube asks for a check, only the YouTube card says so (with the Continue button) while Spotify
            carries on; if Spotify isn't connected, only that lane stops (with a Resume button) while YouTube finishes; when the add-on's daily limit is used up YouTube stops and says so. Pause and Stop apply to both. "Keep working" hides the window and leaves a strip above the player with both lanes' progress; it says when the run has finished.
          THE SCREEN FOR CHOOSING: releases on the left with a tick each and a Spotify and YouTube result for each ("S 4/4", "Y 3/4", "Y no match"); filters (All songs, Confident, Needs a look, No match, Already linked) with counts; show or hide either service; "Tick all confident" and "Untick all". On the right the release's matched album and playlist, and a table of its songs with, for each song and
            service, the match found (title, artist or channel, length, how much longer or shorter than yours) with a tick and a Confident, Likely or Weak tag. Ticks at the start of a song, at the head of a service's column, and per release (and the header ticks show whether everything under them is ticked). Where there is more than one candidate, "Choose…" opens them right under the song,
            with "Neither". "No match" on YouTube has "Search YouTube…" for that one song. Only confident matches are ticked for you. A song that already has a link of that kind shows "Kept" and is never replaced. "Open in the song matcher…" hands the release over with what was already found (nothing is looked up again) and comes back when you close it.
          THE FOOTER says exactly what Apply will write ("Will add 17 links (10 Spotify and 7 YouTube) to 5 releases"), how many need a look, have no match or are kept.   1.52.0 — 2026-10-28 — Full-release videos: when one YouTube video is a whole release, say where each song starts and ends in it, and CRATE plays each song as its own part. No add-on and no backend change.

          THE EDITOR: "Full-release video…" on a release's page (next to "Edit details"), in its right-click menu, and as a button under the YouTube link in a song's properties box. You give the video's link and, for each song, when it starts and when it ends (m:ss, h:mm:ss, or seconds). Three ways to fill the times:
            (1) PLAY THE VIDEO HERE ("▶ Play it here") and press the clock ⏱ beside a song as it begins: it takes the player's time as that song's start, and as the end of the song before if that has none; the same beside an end.
            (2) PASTE TIMES from the video's description ("Paste times…"): lines like "0:00 Drive My Car", "[2:30] Norwegian Wood", "3. 4:46 You Won't See Me", "Girl - 14:30", or a range "0:00 - 2:30 Title". With as many lines as songs they go by order; otherwise by title (capitals, punctuation and bracketed bits are ignored), and it says how many songs it matched.
            (3) TYPE THEM, and use "Ends from next start" to make each song end where the next begins (the last ends where the video does, when the player has it).
          A ▶ beside a song plays just that part. Times that aren't times, and an end not after its start, are marked in red and block Save; a start earlier than the song before it is noted. A song with no times is left as it was. The dialog leaves the mini-player usable underneath it, since that is how you use it.
          HOW IT IS KEPT: in each song's own YouTube link, as "watch?v=ID&t=284&end=546" (YouTube itself starts the video at t=; end= is ignored by it), saved with the release's track data like any link. So it needs nothing new in your sheet, goes wherever the link goes, and opens at the right place if you click it in YouTube.
          PLAYING: a song in a full-release video plays only its part: the player is told where to start and stop, the mini-player's bar and time show the song (1:00 into a 2:34 song, not 5:46 of a 40-minute video), seeking stays inside it, and when a part ends the next song starts at its own time. If the player doesn't stop at the end itself, CRATE notices and moves on, once.
            Last.fm is told the song's length (not the video's), so a song scrobbles after half of ITS length. An ordinary album's links, queue and scrobbling are untouched (tested).
          VERIFIED: the time, link and description parsing in 24 unit checks (every time format, round trips for 0 to 7300 seconds, descriptions in the styles above); the editor, saving (one save, one link per song), re-opening, the three entry points, the red marks, the clock buttons and the player's loading, position, duration, seeking, the move to the next part and the backup end check in 26 browser checks. Breaking each of eight things
            (the offset, the stop time, the scrobble length, picking the clicked song, seeking, the backup end check, the saved end, the filled ends) fails a test. NOT VERIFIED: against the real YouTube player. The tests use a stand-in that records what CRATE asks of it, so whether the real player honours a stop time and fires its ended event as documented is untested, and the backup check
            is there for exactly that; a video that can't be embedded, or is removed, behaves as it always did. Times typed are whole seconds. A tracklist from Spotify with no manual tracklist works too (links are stored per track number), but if the tracklist is later replaced and renumbered the times go with the old numbers.


  1.51.3 — 2026-10-28 — The "CRATE for YouTube" add-on is much faster and runs in the background: it no longer opens a tab for each question. Needs add-on 1.1.0 (crate-youtube-addon.zip); the page change is only the wording in the song matcher. The backend and the RYM add-on are unchanged.

          WHY IT WAS SLOW: every question opened a real browser tab, waited for it to load, and then waited a forced 9 to 15 seconds before the next: about 15 seconds each. An album costs two questions (find its playlist, read it), and each song searched on its own is another 15 seconds,
            so a 12-song album could take several minutes.
          WHAT IT DOES NOW: the data the add-on reads is already in the HTML YouTube sends for a search or playlist page, so the add-on's background script now ASKS FOR THE PAGE DIRECTLY (with your browser's cookies, as a tab would send) and reads it: about a second per question, nothing opening or
            flashing on your screen, and it keeps working whatever you are doing. The pause between pages is 1.5 to 3 seconds (it was 9 to 15), never under 1 second, whatever CRATE asks. Four questions that used to take about a minute are answered in under 12 seconds in the test; the song matcher now says a
            few seconds, not "half a minute".
          WHAT DID NOT CHANGE: still one question at a time, still at most 250 pages a day (it counts every question, direct or by tab, and that limit, not the speed, is now what ends a long run; say if you want it raised), still remembers every answer (3 days for a search, 7 for a playlist), still only
            opens YouTube search and playlist addresses, and still STOPS at the first cookie question, "unusual traffic" page or "too many requests" answer: it then opens that page in front of you to pass, and Continue retries it.
          THE FALLBACK: if a page can't be read directly (YouTube changes what it sends), the add-on opens it in a background tab as before, with the old 9 to 15 second pause. In the add-on's options a new "Speed" switch ("Open a background tab for every page instead") turns the direct way off altogether.
          THE RISK, plainly: asking for pages directly and faster means more requests per minute from your connection (about 20 a minute at most, against 4 before), to a site whose terms only allow its API. YouTube may be quicker to show a check, or to slow your connection for a while. It stops at the first check instead of
            pushing on, but what YouTube does about it is up to YouTube. Turn the switch on if you'd rather go slowly.
          VERIFIED: with the add-on in a real Chrome against a stand-in YouTube (32 checks: the 21 existing ones, all pinned to the tab path so the fallback stays covered, plus 11 new): four questions answered in under 12 seconds, no tab ever opened and every request arriving as a direct request, a pause of 1 to 3.5 seconds between
            them, the same answers the tab gave, the fallback to a tab when a direct request gets nothing readable, a cookie question and a "too many requests" answer each stopping it and opening the page in front of you, Continue, the options switch; and the whole chain from CRATE's page through the real bridge (9 checks) and
            the song matcher (19). Turning the direct way off, or removing the pause, each fails a test. NOT VERIFIED: against the real YouTube (this machine can't reach it), so whether YouTube serves a direct request the same data as a page, and how it reacts at this speed, is untested until you try it; if the direct way
            can't read the real pages it falls back to tabs, which is slow but works, and the toolbar's "Diagnose this page" shows what it can read.


  1.51.2 — 2026-10-28 — Fix: a track's number of ratings, written on the page as "6k", was being taken for the track's TITLE. Needs RYM add-on 1.12.2 (crate-rym-addon.zip). The backend and the YouTube add-on are unchanged.

          WHAT WENT WRONG (your Rubber Soul report): on a release with many ratings, Rate Your Music abbreviates each track's count ("6k", "5k") and writes it between the rating and the track number. Add-ons 1.12.0 and 1.12.1 only knew counts written as plain numbers ("683", "1,234"), so
            "6k" was left over and, being the first piece with a letter in it, was taken as the title: all 14 tracks came out called "6k" or "5k". I wrote that count-reading without ever seeing this form, which your Diagnose report showed me.
          THE FIX: a count straight after a rating, however it is written ("683", "1,234", "6k", "1.2k", "1.5m"), is read as that rating's count and is never a title; "6k" is read as about 6000 (the page rounds it, so these counts are approximate), "1.2k" as 1200, "1.5m" as 1500000. A track really called
            "6k" that does not follow a rating keeps its title. A list of titles that are mostly numbers or counts is also recognised as wrong and not offered.
          RELEASES ALREADY AFFECTED: if an update from add-on 1.12.0 or 1.12.1 already saved titles like "6k" on a release, CRATE now counts a tracklist whose titles are mostly numbers or counts as NO tracklist, so the next update from Rate Your Music offers the real one as a ticked fill
            (applied by itself in the queue) instead of a difference for you to decide. Run "update ↻" on those releases once you have add-on 1.12.2. A tracklist of yours with real titles that merely differ from RYM's is still left for you to decide.
          VERIFIED: the three real rows from your report ("4.2", "6k", "A1", "Drive My Car" gives Drive My Car, 4.2, 6000); every count shape; a track really called "6k" and one called "Ok"; the "looks wrong" check; in a real Chrome with the extension loaded, a page built from your report's markup (14 tracks, rating then "6k"
            then number then title): all 14 real titles, ratings and counts, in the page and when sent to CRATE; and on the page side, junk titles counting as no tracklist (even with the same number of tracks) while a real list of yours is left alone. Removing the fix reproduces your bug in the real-Chrome test.
            NOT VERIFIED: other ways RYM might write a count (I have seen plain numbers and "Nk"); the next Diagnose report will show it if there are more.


  1.51.1 — 2026-10-28 — The Rate Your Music add-on's limit of 120 pages a day is removed. Needs RYM add-on 1.12.1 (crate-rym-addon.zip); the backend and the YouTube add-on are unchanged.

          WHAT WENT: the add-on no longer counts the pages it opens or refuses a run for having opened too many in the last 24 hours, and the day-by-day count it kept for that is deleted from your browser's storage the next time a run starts. You can run "Update from Rate Your Music"
            as many times in a day as you like.
          WHAT STAYS, AND WHY: one page at a time, 8 to 15 seconds between pages (never under 6), at most 25 pages in a single run, and the run STOPS at the first page that asks for a check or can't be read, leaving it in front of you (then Continue or Cancel). Those are what keep
            a run gentle on the site. You asked only about the daily limit, so the 25-a-run limit and the pauses are untouched; say if you want either changed.
          THE RISK, plainly: with no daily cap, running it for hours makes it more likely that Rate Your Music asks for a check (a captcha or "Just a moment…") or, if it takes against the traffic, blocks your connection for a while. The add-on stops at the first check rather than pushing
            through, but a block is up to the site. If a check appears, pass it and press Continue, or leave it a while.
          WITH THE OLD ADD-ON STILL INSTALLED (1.12.0 or earlier) it keeps refusing past 120 a day; CRATE now says so ("An older version of the add-on is installed and keeps to 120 pages a day (version 1.12.1 removes that limit)") instead of just failing. The YouTube add-on's own limit
            of 250 pages a day is separate and unchanged.
          VERIFIED: with the add-on loaded in a real Chrome, a run of 15 pages is accepted when 500 pages are on record as used in the last day (the old version would have refused it), and the old day-by-day log is deleted; the older add-on's refusal is explained in CRATE; the 25-a-run cut,
            the pauses, the stop at a check, Continue and Cancel are unchanged and still pass their tests. Putting a cap back fails the new test. NOT VERIFIED: how Rate Your Music behaves after hours of use (this machine can't reach it), so the risk above is a judgment, not a measurement.

            both in the track properties box (a new "How many ratings that is based on" field; clearing the rating clears it) and as two new columns you can add to the Tracks view: "RYM track rating" and "RYM track ratings (count)", sorted as numbers.

            RYM's own (the same average). A rating you typed is NEVER replaced, and no RYM count is hung on it. A tracklist filled in by an update comes with its ratings and counts. To pull them for releases you've already updated, run "update ↻" on them again (the "not yet
            updated" menu entry only covers those with no first update).

  1.50.0 — 2026-10-27 — Merge duplicates: find releases that are the same, keep the best of each, and fold them into one. NEEDS THE BACKEND SCRIPT RE-DEPLOYED (one new action, "mergeAlbums"); the add-ons are unchanged.

          WHERE: ⚙ → "Find and merge duplicates…". It lists groups of likely duplicates, most certain first: "Same album" (the artist and title match, ignoring case, accents, "The", "&" and bracketed edition notes, and nothing else differs: the same rules the
            add-time duplicate check uses), "Same Rate Your Music page" (different titles, one RYM link: a typo, say), and "Different editions?" (the same album with a different edition, catalogue number or year: often deliberate, so it is last and never assumed).
          NOTHING IS MERGED FOR YOU. Open a group and the releases sit side by side: pick the one to KEEP (it starts on the one with the most attached to it: a rating, listens, playlist places, a tracklist, a cover), and only the fields they DISAGREE on are laid out
            for you to choose, one choice per field (your review can also keep BOTH, yours first). What merely fills a gap is filled in without asking and listed under "other fields", marked "filled in from another"; the RYM descriptors of both are combined, the
            update logs are combined, and the earliest first-update date is kept. A merge never writes an empty value over one that is there, and only the fields that actually change the kept release are sent. The summary says what will happen (releases removed,
            playlist items that move across, diary entries that follow) and the button needs a SECOND press ("Really merge? Press again"), disarming itself after five seconds.
          WHAT THE SERVER DOES (one request, the new "mergeAlbums" action): it first checks that every row is still the exact release the page showed (id, artist and title), and if ANY is not (the crate changed in another tab or on another device) it refuses and changes
            nothing at all. Then it writes the merged fields to the kept release (never its date added or id), moves the playlist items that pointed at the duplicates to it (an item that would make the kept release appear twice in one playlist is dropped),
            re-points diary entries logged under a duplicate's different title (unless another release still has that text), and only then deletes the duplicates, last row first.
          A SAFETY NET: before anything is sent, a copy of everything removed (every field) is kept on THIS device (the last 15 merges). "Recently merged" at the bottom has "Put back what was removed", which re-adds those releases as NEW ones. Their places in playlists and
            their diary entries are NOT restored, and they get a new date added. If the answer never arrives (a network failure) it says it can't confirm whether the merge happened and keeps the copy; if the server refuses, or doesn't know the action yet (an
            un-redeployed backend script), it says so plainly and keeps nothing, because nothing happened.
          "NOT DUPLICATES" takes a group off the list for good (kept on this device).
          LIMITS: a group shows at most 11 releases (one to keep and ten to fold in); playlist places and diary entries are matched by album id and by artist/title text, as the rest of CRATE does; the lists of listens (scrobbles) are by name and are untouched.
          VERIFIED: the backend action (18 checks against a stand-in spreadsheet, including refusing with NOTHING changed for a wrong id, a wrong title, a row past the end, the same row twice, the kept release among the removed, and a stale keeper; playlist items kept,
            moved and de-duplicated; diary entries; the kept release below the duplicates; releases that never got an id; only the allowed columns), and the page (29 browser checks: the groups and their order, the side-by-side view, the default keeper, which fields
            are offered, the summary, the second press and its timeout, exactly what is sent, each field choice and "keep both", keeping the other release, the stale, old-backend and network-failure answers, the safety copy and putting back, "not duplicates", markup shown
            as text, an empty list). A wrong default keeper, no second press, or treating editions as exact each fails a test. NOT VERIFIED: against your real spreadsheet (and its real Apps Script limits); looked at in the browser with test data only, not on a phone.


  1.49.0 — 2026-10-27 — Pick by mood: choose how it should feel, and CRATE finds what in your crate fits. Page only: the backend and the add-ons are unchanged.

          WHERE: ⚙ → "Pick by mood…". A dialog with every shared sense from the descriptors (sadness, anger, fear & dread, joy, calm, slow, dreamy & ethereal, noise & distortion...), by category, each with how many of your releases have it (the ones
            with none are dimmed). Pick one or several; on the right, what fits appears at once.
          HOW IT RANKS: a release "fits a sense" when any of its descriptors is in that sense. The more of the senses you picked it fits, the higher it goes; then the more of its descriptors that fit; then what you rated highest. Each row shows the descriptors
            that matched, and "2 of 3" when you picked several. "Must fit every sense I pick" keeps only the ones that fit them all; "Not listened to yet" leaves out anything you've logged in the diary (and the counts beside the senses follow it).
          TYPE A FEELING instead: a word like "sorrowful", "joyous" or "eerie" (or half of a descriptor, "melan") finds the shared sense that means it and picks it, saying which ("sorrowful" → sadness). A word the thesaurus doesn't know says so.
          WHAT YOU CAN DO WITH THE RESULT: click one to open it; "Surprise me" plays one of the ten best fits at random and says what it played and why; "Play these" queues up to 100 of the matches, shuffled; "Save as playlist…" opens the new-playlist dialog
            with them in it (up to 100), named after the senses ("Mood: sadness + slow"). What you picked is remembered when you reopen it. Escape or a click outside closes it.
          IT NEEDS DESCRIPTORS: it can only match a release that has some, and says how many of yours do ("7 of your 8 releases"). Updating releases from Rate Your Music, or adding descriptors in a release's details, widens it; with none anywhere it says so and what to do.
          VERIFIED: 25 browser checks (the senses and their counts; nothing picked; picking one, two, "must fit every"; "not listened to yet" and the counts following it; clear; a typed feeling by meaning, by descriptor, by half a word, and an unknown word;
            a sense none of your releases has; Surprise me, Play these, and Save as playlist each checked for exactly what they hand on; reopening; opening a result; markup in a title or descriptor shown as text; Escape and clicking outside; a crate with no
            descriptors). Reversing the ranking, ignoring "must fit every sense", or switching the thesaurus lookup off each fails a test. Looked at in the browser with test data only; NOT looked at on a phone (the layout stacks on a narrow screen), and not yet tried on
            your real crate, where how well it works depends on how many of your releases have descriptors.

          REMOVING AND REORDERING while narrowed or sorted act on the real playlist (the view is a copy: a first version of this would have removed the item from the copy only).
          ONE SET OF RULES: what makes an album pass the search, the browser pick and the filters, and each sort, were pulled out of the Music grid's own code and are used by both the grid and a playlist, so they cannot drift apart.
          NOT DONE: group headings are in the list and covers views of a playlist, not in the Tracks, Album-and-Tracks or Artists views (the last is grouped by artist anyway); those three do obey the browser, search, sort and filters.
          VERIFIED: 29 browser checks (layout; the scope of the browser in albums, artists and genres; a pick narrowing and clearing; genre picks; the search box; "nothing matches"; the Sort & filter option; sorting by artist and
            by rating; Play in the shown order; Playlist order restoring dragging; grouping; removing and dragging on the real playlist; opening another playlist; back to Music; the compact strip; the strip's own width;
            the new-playlist button; the covers view), and the Music suites for search, sort, genre and the left browser still pass. Breaking the narrowing, the real-playlist removal or the browser scope each fails the test.
            Looked at rendered on a desktop-sized window; NOT looked at on a phone, and the strip is still hidden below the existing narrow-screen breakpoint as before.


  1.46.2 — 2026-10-27 — Scrobbles from YouTube tracks played in CRATE were never sent, for two reasons. Both fixed. Page only: no new add-on or backend.

          WHY NOTHING WAS SCROBBLED: (1) when a YouTube track started, the scrobble tracker identified it by a "url" that CRATE's YouTube queue items don't have (they hold the video id and the track name), so it never started:
            no "now playing", no timer, nothing to send when the track ended. (2) Even had it started, every request to Last.fm left out the account's session key, which Last.fm requires for anything written to an account,
            so each scrobble would have been refused (the refusal was only written to the browser's console). Both are fixed: each play is tracked from the moment it starts, and the session key is sent and signed with the request.
          ALSO FIXED in the same code: time spent PAUSED or buffering no longer counts as listening (it used the clock since the track started); the scrobble is sent the moment Last.fm's rule is met (half the track or 4 minutes,
            whichever is less, and the track must be over 30 seconds) instead of only when a track ends, so skipping ahead or closing the player afterwards no longer loses it; playing the SAME track again now scrobbles it again;
            a track CRATE has no real name for ("Now playing") or no artist for is not sent rather than scrobbled under a made-up name; and if Last.fm refuses a scrobble CRATE tries twice more and then SAYS SO, naming the track and
            suggesting you reconnect the account, instead of failing silently.
          NOT CHANGED: Spotify and SoundCloud playback (Last.fm's own Spotify connection normally covers Spotify). Plays from before this version are not sent retroactively. The artist sent is the release's artist, so a track
            on a compilation goes under the compilation's artist.
          VERIFIED (20 checks in the browser, with a stand-in for Last.fm and a controllable clock): "now playing" with artist, track and album; no scrobble at 90 s of a 200 s track, one at 105 s with the time it STARTED, the
            session key and a correct signature, and only once even when the track then ends; under 30 s never; a 10-minute track at 4 minutes; paused time not counted; skipping before half way sends nothing and the next track is
            announced and scrobbled; skipping after keeps it without doubling it; the same track again scrobbles twice; closing early sends nothing, and no stray timer fires later; no connected account sends nothing; a track with no real
            name or no artist is not sent; a track played from a playlist scrobbles under its own artist and album; Last.fm refusing it leads to three tries and a message. Putting back the tracker never starting, the missing session key, or
            the clock running while paused each fails the test. NOT VERIFIED: against the real Last.fm (this machine has no network) or the real YouTube player's events; if "Last.fm didn't accept the scrobble" appears, the
            connection needs redoing in the settings (or the API key and secret there are wrong).

          WHAT CHANGED: the safe changes (a blank filled, a better cover, a fuller tracklist) were already applied by themselves as each page was read. What DIFFERS from what you have (your label "K" against RYM's "K Records",

          STILL YOUR PRESS: nothing is written until you press Apply. The update log (on the album page) keeps what each value was and what it became, with the old value, so a change can be put back by hand.
          NOTE: this means one press can replace values you have typed (genres you chose yourself, say) with Rate Your Music's. If you'd rather decide each one, turn it off: ⚙ → RYM ratings… → "Tick the differences too".
          VERIFIED (39 checks in all for the queue): differences arrive ticked and nothing is written yet; a row opened shows its difference ticked and says so; unticking one keeps it ("Apply 1 ticked") and one press applies

  1.44.0 — 2026-10-26 — Where you are in the catalogue is kept when a release is added, removed or edited, so you don't have to scroll back to find it. Page only: no new add-on or backend.

          WHAT YOU SAW: after adding or removing a release, the catalogue jumped back to the top.
          CAUSE: every add, remove, import, edit and "Refresh" reloads the catalogue, and a reload begins by putting up a "loading" state, which clears the view; by the time the cards were drawn again, the scroll position was gone.
          NOW: just before a reload does that, CRATE remembers the release at the top of your view (and the few after it) and how far each sits below the top, and after the cards are drawn again it puts the first of them that is
            still there back at the same height. It identifies a release by its id (or artist + title + date if it has none), because the sheet's row numbers shift up when one is deleted, so a row number can't be remembered.
            If you removed the very release you were looking at, the next one stays where it was; if everything in view is gone, the old distance from the top is used (kept inside the list). The left-hand genre list keeps its
            place too. The same care is taken when a RYM rating arrives (which redraws the cards) and when you save an edit.
          NOT CHANGED: changing the sort, grouping, view or searching behave exactly as before (they never went to the top on their own). Only the Albums view of the catalogue is covered; playlists, the artist view and the
            other tabs keep their own scrolling. Adding a release that sorts into the top of the list pushes every card along by one place, and the release you were looking at stays at the same height while the ones beside it
            reflow around it.
          VERIFIED, in the browser with CRATE's real code, on a 150-release catalogue scrolled well down (19 checks), with and without release ids, and with the sheet shifting its rows after a delete: removing a release above the
            view; adding one that sorts to the very top; removing the release you are looking at; a RYM rating arriving; grouped by year; the list view; staying at the top when at the top; the left list. Putting the old
            behaviour back (taking the position after the loading state) fails four of them with the jump to the top. NOT VERIFIED: against your real sheet's timing (a slow reload shows the loading screen while it waits, and
            the position is put back when it ends), and a very large catalogue where images are still loading as it is put back.


  1.43.2 — 2026-10-26 — Tracklists: a side called just "A" no longer becomes the title "A", and a title like "1490" is pulled (needs the add-on, now 1.10.1; the page itself is unchanged).

          WHAT YOU SAW: a track on a side numbered only "A" got the title "A", while "B1" and "B2" gave their real titles; and a track called "1490" was missing.
          CAUSE, ONE WEAKNESS TWICE: the reader tells a track NUMBER from a TITLE by what each looks like. It knew numbers like 3, 03., A1 and B2, but not a side on its own: "A" has a letter in it, so it was taken for the title
            and the real one was dropped. And a title made only of digits ("1490") was accepted only when the row had two digit pieces (the number and the title); next to a vinyl number (A1, B2) there is just one.
          NOW: a side letter on its own (A to H, and AA/BB for a double A-side) is the track number whenever the row has something else that can be the title: a piece with letters, or digits after it. A track REALLY called
            "A" still keeps its title (with a number of its own before it, "1" "A"; on a numbered side, "B2" "A"; or alone with a length). A title of digits is pulled whenever the row has a number piece of any kind (3, A1, 1-04,
            a side letter), and a row whose only number is a side letter, or whose only title is 4+ digits ("1490"), now counts as a track row at all even with no length and no rating. A row with only digits and a length and no
            number ("1490", "3:20") is still not a track, as before, so the guards against ratings and vote counts being read as titles are unchanged.
          VERIFIED: 20 rows by hand and 5 new unit checks (55 in all), and the real extension on a page with sides A, B1, B2, C1 (title 1490), D, E, with lengths and without, with the rating first, and tracks really named "A"
            (6 checks); breaking each rule is caught (one of my first two break-tests wasn't, which showed my page didn't exercise it; I added the page that does). Full regression: see below. NOT VERIFIED: a title of only 1-3
            digits next to a bare number ("7" "99") is read as before, which is right for "7 / 99" but would be wrong for a number you wanted as a title when the real number is missing from the page.

            is simply never offered, it was the reading, which this fixes.

          1. TRACKS. On a split release each row is an artist link and a song link side by side ("Dropdead", " - Suck"). The second piece starts with a dash, which my reader takes for a sub-item ("- Piece One 3:58") and folds

            release's own info table, and never in that box.
          3. ARTIST. In a list (genre, chart, artist page) several artists were always joined with " & ". They are now joined the way the page writes them: "A / B" for a split, "A, B", else "A & B". The full release date was
            also being read as just the year when the day and month sit as text beside the year's link ("4 May 1991"); it now reads 1991-05-04.

            tracklist is offered TICKED, "fills a blank", instead of unticked as a difference (and "Apply all" applies it). The review also offers the artist name when yours differs from RYM's only in the separators
            ("Swirlies / & Dropdead" -> "Swirlies / Dropdead"), ticked; a different case or accent is left alone.

            "Artist - Song" with the right ratings, the full date, genres, descriptors, no catalogue number, the 1200 cover from cdn.sonemic.net (11); the update plan (21 checks, with the cases that must NOT change); and

  1.40.0 — 2026-10-25 — Faster imports that run in the background; bigger covers, and covers on update; label, catalogue number and descriptors; and the tracklists that wouldn't pull (needs the add-on, now 1.9.0; the speed-up and the descriptors field also need the backend script re-deployed).

          TRACKS THAT WOULDN'T PULL (your Beat Happening compilation): the page puts a track's rating and vote count BEFORE its number and title ("4.0", "11", "1", "Our Secret", "lyrics", "2:51"), and the add-on
            assumed number, title, length, rating. It took "4.0" for the title, saw every title was a number and (rightly) saved nothing. It now works out what each piece IS (a number, a rating, a vote count, a
            length, a title: the first piece with a letter in it that comes before the length) instead of trusting the order, so either order reads the same. Your 27 tracks, in the layout your Diagnose report showed, come out whole.
          COVER ON UPDATE: "Update from this page" now has a Cover row: it fills a blank cover, or swaps RYM's own smaller picture for a larger one. A cover from anywhere else (Spotify, say) is never replaced.
          BIGGER COVERS: a Rate Your Music picture's address says its size ("…/i/300/…"). The add-on takes the larger of the cover on the page and the page's share image, and CRATE then tries larger sizes of the same
            picture (1200, 800, 600, 500) and keeps the first that actually loads, for imports and updates alike. A cover that won't load from here is still never saved (a broken picture would be worse than none).
          LABEL, CATALOGUE NUMBER, DESCRIPTORS: read off the release page (the "Label", "Catalog #" and "Descriptors" rows) and carried through sends and updates. Label and catalogue number fill their existing fields (an
            automatic CRT-007 number counts as blank; a value that differs from yours is offered unticked). The descriptors go in a NEW field, "RYM descriptors", shown in the release's details and searchable, and NOT
            in the existing "Descriptor" field: that one is the edition label ("Deluxe Edition") and the duplicate check compares it, so putting mood tags there could make an album look like a different edition of itself.
          SPEED: an import used to go strictly one release at a time: a cover check (up to 6 seconds if the picture was slow), then a full round trip to your sheet, for each. Now covers are checked six at a time up front,
            and releases are added 25 to a request (one write per group). 57 releases are 3 requests, not 57. Needs the backend script updated (a new "addAlbums" action, in apps-script-backend.gs): with the old script
            it notices ("Missing row number", before anything is written) and goes one at a time as before. A group that FAILS midway fails alone and is never re-sent one by one (it may have been written).
          IN THE BACKGROUND: the import dialog has "Keep working": it hides the dialog without stopping the run, and a small bar (bottom left) shows the progress with a Cancel, then "57 releases added · Details · ×" when done.
            "Apply all" shows its progress in the same bar.
          NOT DONE / UNKNOWN: I could not reproduce a 15-second rhythm exactly; what I found is the one-at-a-time loop above, so how much faster it is on your sheet depends on your sheet (3 requests instead of 57 is the point).
            The info rows (Label, Catalog #, Descriptors) are found by their heading text because I haven't seen the real markup: if a page still gives none, the add-on popup's Diagnose now includes the info table's own
            markup and what it read back. Releases already in your crate keep their covers until you update them; "RYM descriptors" stays empty until a release is sent or updated.
          VERIFIED: the add-on's row reading (50 checks incl. your real rows) and a 27-track page built like your Diagnose report, with the real extension, plus label/catalogue/descriptors/larger cover from a table and a div
            layout (9); the backend's group add (11); CRATE's details, covers and duplicate check (14); and the speed behaviour: 57 in 3 requests, covers six at a time, an old script, a failing group, background,
            Details and Cancel on the bar (20). Each guard broken on purpose and caught.


  1.39.0 — 2026-10-25 — Batch update from Rate Your Music; and tracklists saved from "Update from this page" now really reach your sheet.

          THE BUG (this is why a tracklist "still didn't get pulled"): saving a tracklist from the update review, and from "Remove from tracklist" on a song, sent it to your sheet without saying what kind of write it was.
            The backend's default branch only handles ratings, reviews, track data and the RYM rating, so it answered "ok" and wrote NOTHING. CRATE kept the tracklist in memory (the deck showed it, the toast said
            "Tracklist updated") until the next reload of the sheet, and then it was gone ("No tracklist found for this release"). It is now sent as editAlbum, which the backend does write. Nothing else sent without an
            action had this problem (ratings, reviews, track data, RYM ratings are all handled there). The Edit form, Add and Import were never affected. If you updated tracklists this way before, they did not stick:
            "Fill tracklists from Rate Your Music…" (below) will put them back from what the add-on has seen.
          BATCH UPDATE, 1: when two or more updates are waiting from release pages, the bar gets "Apply all N". It applies only what is NEW in each (a blank filled, a date made more precise, a tracklist where there
            is none) and never anything that differs from what you have: a release with a difference keeps waiting, so you can open it and choose. A release that is not in your crate is skipped (and released from the
            bar). It says what happened: "Updated 2 releases (6 changes); 1 not in your crate; 1 has differences left for you to review."

            (matched by its saved RYM link, else artist + title + type), it asks first, showing how many and a few examples, then saves them (lengths, headings, and RYM's track ratings where a track has none).
            It leaves alone any release that already has a saved tracklist, and any tracklist that looks wrong (titles that are numbers). Those releases switch from the automatic lookup to the saved list. Run again, it finds nothing left.
          WHAT IT DOES NOT DO: it does not open Rate Your Music pages for you. Only what you have already viewed can be used, and the add-on saves a tracklist when you open a release's page. (Fetching pages on its own would be
            automated browsing of a site that doesn't welcome it.) It does not yet batch-update dates, genres or covers from a genre/artist/chart LIST page; say if you want that.

  1.38.2 — 2026-10-24 — Tracklists from Rate Your Music no longer come through with the ratings as the titles (needs the add-on, now 1.8.2).

          WHAT YOU SAW: a tracklist pulled from a release page had every title replaced by that track's RATING (3.4, 3.5, 4.0…) while the lengths were right (the Keith Tippett Group page, with its A1…B6 vinyl numbers).
          WHAT WAS WRONG (two faults in the add-on, reproduced here): it skips anything inside an element whose class name mentions "credit" so that track credits aren't read as songs, but that can also
            swallow a real title; the rating was then the first piece of text left, and the add-on accepted it as the title. And nothing ever stopped a rating, or a vote count, from being taken as a title.
          NOW: a rating can no longer be a title (a rating-shaped piece after a length, or before a "|" and a count, is the rating; a bare number after a rating or a length is a vote count), so a row whose
            title can't be found gives no track instead of a wrong one. If the first reading finds fewer than two tracks, or titles that are numbers, the add-on reads the page again ignoring "credit" class names;
            if that is no better it saves nothing. A wrong copy an older version kept is removed (or replaced by the right one) the next time you open that release page. And CRATE refuses to offer a saved
            tracklist whose titles are mostly numbers: it says it looks wrong and what to do, instead of letting you use it. The add-on popup's Diagnose now says when the second reading was needed.
          TO FIX A LIST THAT CAME OUT WRONG: install the new add-on, open that release's page on RateYourMusic once, then in CRATE open the album, Edit, press RYM, Use this list (your tracks keep their numbers by title).
          VERIFIED: the row logic (42 checks, each new rule broken on purpose and caught), the Keith Tippett tracklist rebuilt four ways with the REAL extension (a bare-text title, a link, a link whose class mentions
            "credit", loose text), a page with no findable title (saves nothing), a wrong stored copy removed and replaced, and CRATE refusing a list of numbers (28). NOT VERIFIED against real RateYourMusic: I
            can't see its markup, so the "credit" class is my best explanation for what you saw, not a certainty. If a page still reads wrong, the add-on popup's Diagnose gives each row's pieces and markup.
