            CRATE, used and saved (17 + 8 checks); and CRATE's side with stand-ins for the add-on, including silence, a spoofed sender, hostile data and the wrong release type (26).
            NOT VERIFIED against real RateYourMusic: its page markup can't be seen from here. The add-on takes list items or table rows that carry a length and picks the biggest
            group (ignoring "related" areas), reading each row's pieces in order; failing that it reads the area as text. The add-on's options have a selector for a track row, and
            nothing is saved rather than guessed when it can't find a tracklist.
          STILL TO COME (stage 2): a side-by-side comparison of the sources (counts, extra bonus tracks).


  1.35.1 — 2026-10-23 — Player: no more empty band at the top, and a floating player can no longer end up off-screen.

          WHAT YOU SAW: with the player docked at the top (Layout → Player controls in the top panel) the page left an empty 85px band above the tabs and the
            player was nowhere to be seen. Two separate faults produce exactly that picture:
          1. THE SPACE WAS RESERVED WHETHER OR NOT THE PLAYER WAS THERE. The player starts hidden and only appears when playback starts (and "Disconnect
             Spotify" hides it again), but the 85px at the top was reserved by the DOCKED-AT-TOP setting alone. So an idle player docked at the top left a blank
             band; so did a player you had detached into a floating window. Now the space is reserved only while the player is docked at the top AND showing AND
             not floating. (Side effect, intended: with the top dock, the page shifts down by the player's height when playback starts, instead of keeping a
             permanent blank strip.) The default bottom dock is unchanged.
          2. A FLOATING PLAYER COULD COME BACK OFF-SCREEN. A detached player's position is saved in pixels and was restored as it was, so after a smaller window,
             another screen or a bad saved value it could sit entirely outside the screen, unreachable (its dock button goes with it). Its position is now kept
             wholly on screen: when it is restored, when it is shown (its size is only known then), and whenever the window changes size; the corrected position
             is saved, so it stays put. A position that is fine is never moved.
          To get a stuck player back WITHOUT waiting for this: open the browser console and type  setPlayerDetached(false)  (it docks the player again).
          VERIFIED in a browser (22 checks): an idle and a playing player docked at the top, floating on screen, floating at x = 2600 on a 1920 screen (your
            screenshot's state), off the top-left, hidden at an impossible position and then shown, a window made smaller, junk saved values, docking again, the
            bottom dock, and a phone. 11 of them fail on 1.35.0, including both of the faults above.


  1.35.0 — 2026-10-23 — A genre can count as primary, as secondary, or as either.

          A release has two genre lists: Genre (primary: the main flavour of the music, and there can be several, such as Progressive Rock and Psychedelic
            Rock) and Secondary Genre (influences that colour it without dominating, such as Jazz Rock). Until now every genre filter looked at both lists glued
            together, so it couldn't tell "Jazz Rock is what this IS" from "Jazz Rock is a flavour in it". "First genre only" (which is about position in the
            list, not about role) was the nearest thing.
          NEW: ⇅ menu → Genre search → "A genre counts when it's": primary or secondary (the default: nothing changes until you pick something else) · primary
            only · secondary only. It shapes the left panel's genre filter AND its genre tree (a genre only appears, with a count, if some release has it in
            that role, so a count never promises what clicking it can't show), genre: / -genre: searches, the Release Chart, and the panel title
            ("Jazz Rock (secondary genres only)"). The Export dialog has the same choice next to its genre filter; it is saved with saved filters (older
            saved filters count both lists, as before) and noted in the file's filter note.
          "First genre only" stays, and combines with it by taking the first genre of whatever list is left: secondary only + first genre only means the
            first SECONDARY genre. A release with nothing in the chosen list simply isn't in it; "Unknown" still means a release with no genres at all, in
            every setting, so choosing "secondary only" does not turn every release without a secondary genre into "Unknown".
          NOT CHANGED (they still count both lists): a genre page's own release list and top artists, "Play all" / "Export N releases…" from a genre's
            right-click menu, Group by genre, and the Stats map's genre axes and network. Say if you want the choice to reach any of these.
          VERIFIED in a browser (30 checks): every combination on a six-release crate built to tell the cases apart (two primaries, a secondary, a release with
            only a secondary, a release with no genre), the search terms, the tree, the Release Chart, the title, the menu, saved preferences (nonsense falls
            back to "either") and the Export dialog; four deliberately broken versions (filter, tree, export and the Unknown rule ignoring the setting) were each caught.


  1.34.0 — 2026-10-23 — A better tracklist editor, the same one in the Add form, the Edit form and the deck's "+ Add track" (stage 1 of 3).

          PASTE A LIST: "Paste a list…" takes a tracklist from anywhere (RYM, Wikipedia, Bandcamp, a booklet) and shows what it read before you use it
            ("5 tracks · 2 headings · 3 with times"). It understands "1. Title 3:12", "01 - Title", "3) Title", times in brackets or after a dash, "Disc 2" / "CD2" /
            "Side B" / "Bonus tracks" headings, vinyl numbering (A1, A2, B1), tab-separated columns, and ignores "Tracklist" / "Total length" lines. A bare number
            counts as a track number only when it is the next one ("99 Problems" and "1979" stay titles). "Use this list" replaces, "Add to the end" appends.
            Pasting several lines straight into a title box spreads them over rows too.
          ROWS: number, title and length (m:ss) on each row. Drag a row by its handle, or use the ↑ ↓ buttons (the only way on a phone) to reorder; "+" inserts a row
            right below; ✕ removes. Enter in a title moves to the next row (and starts one at the end). "+ Heading" adds Disc 2, Side B, Bonus tracks… and the deck
            shows them above the right tracks. The YouTube and Spotify boxes now sit behind a "Links" button (highlighted when a track has one, and opened by
            itself when auto-match has a suggestion for you to check).
          LENGTHS ARE KEPT: typed, pasted and imported lengths are saved, so the deck's total duration and duration-based song matching work for typed lists too
            (they were dropped before).
          FILL FROM: Spotify, MusicBrainz (needs its release address in Link) and iTunes buttons in both forms (Edit had none). Each says what it found
            ("Spotify has 10 tracks · 38:12") and waits for "Use this list"; nothing changes until you press it.
          YOUR DATA STAYS WITH ITS SONG: replacing a list matches new tracks to old ones by title (case, accents and bracketed notes ignored); a match KEEPS the old
            track's number, so its rating, notes, listened tick and links stay put, and it keeps its old length if the new list gives none. The page says how many were
            matched and warns if a track that had ratings or notes is not in the new list. A new track now takes a number nobody has used, including numbers a DELETED
            track left data under (before, it could inherit that track's rating). The number shown in the deck is the position (1, 2, 3…), not that identity number,
            so reordering never moves anyone's data.
          WHERE A LIST CAME FROM: each track records its source (typed, pasted, Spotify, MusicBrainz, iTunes, or looked up automatically then edited) and the first
            track the date; the deck says so under the list ("Tracklist from Spotify · saved 23 Oct 2026"). Older lists keep working and are simply untagged.
          "+ Add track" in the deck now opens the Edit form with the current list loaded and a new row ready, instead of the old one-field dialog (removed).
          NO BACKEND CHANGE: it all lives in the existing ManualTracks cell; new fields are optional.
          VERIFIED: the paste reader, times and number-keeping on their own (32 checks, with the key rules broken on purpose to confirm the tests notice), and in a
            browser (54): paste, reorder by arrows and by drag, insert, remove, headings, links, saving, an album with real per-track data, the deck, older lists, and the
            three sources with stand-in answers. NOT VERIFIED against the real Spotify, MusicBrainz and iTunes (they were stood in for), nor drag-and-drop on a touch screen
            (arrows work there). STAGES 2 AND 3 TO COME: a side-by-side chooser that compares the sources (counts, extra bonus tracks) and RYM as a source via the add-on.


  1.33.0 — 2026-10-23 — RYM rating history: a record of how each release's rating on Rate Your Music changes over time.

          WHAT IS KEPT: for each release, a list of readings: when, RYM's average, and how many ratings it was based on. A reading is added when
            the average CHANGES (even by 0.01) or the number of ratings has moved by 5% or more (the count creeps up all the time while the average
            barely moves, so every tick would bury the signal). At most one reading a day: a later one the same day replaces that day's. Up to 120
            readings a release; past that the oldest after the very first go first, so where it started and the recent readings stay.
          WHERE YOU SEE IT: the album page has an "RYM history" line under the RYM rating ("RYM history · 4 readings · −0.14 since 7 Apr 2026"); open it
            for a line chart (hover a dot for its date, value and count), how it has moved over about 90 days, and a table of the readings, latest first.
            Hovering the "RYM 3.72" chip on a card also says where it started ("first saved 3.80 on 7 Apr 2026"). ⚙ → RYM ratings & genres… counts
            the releases that have a history.
          WHERE READINGS COME FROM: a rating you type is a reading (dated now). The browser add-on (version 1.5.0, update it) now keeps its OWN trail for each
            release it sees on RateYourMusic, so a change it notices while CRATE is closed is not lost; CRATE joins that trail to what it has (no
            duplicates) whenever it asks. Readings are recorded only when you actually look at the release on RYM (or type a value): there is no
            background checking, so a rating you never visit has no readings in between.
          A RATING YOU TYPED IS STILL NEVER OVERWRITTEN, but what RYM says is recorded in the history beside it, so you can see the two diverge.
          REQUIRES AN UPDATED APPS SCRIPT (apps-script-backend.gs: a new RymHistory column, created on demand; redeploy as a new version). Until then
            nothing is lost: the record is kept in this browser and sent to the sheet by itself once the backend can take it.
          Also changed in the script: a batch write that carries only a history no longer risks clearing the rating (the old batch read "no rating
            given" as "clear it").
          VERIFIED: the rules for what is kept and merged (25 checks), the rating plan with history (27), the page in a browser (26), the backend script
            against a stand-in sheet (12), the add-on's trail (24) and, with the real extension in Chromium, a rating that moved between two visits
            reaching CRATE's album page (28). NOT VERIFIED against real RateYourMusic (its markup can't be seen from here). Not included yet: history is not in
            the export file, and there is no sort or filter by how much a rating has moved.


  1.32.0 — 2026-10-23 — Genre descriptions from Rate Your Music: filled in for you, with RYM's version kept.

          HOW: CRATE can't read RateYourMusic itself (RYM blocks that). The browser add-on (now version 1.4.0, update it) saves the description
            text when you open a genre's page on RYM, and CRATE picks it up by itself, like the ratings. A genre you have no description for
            gets RYM's; "Get it from RYM ↗" on an empty genre page opens that genre's RYM page, and the description appears when you come
            back to CRATE's tab.
          NEVER OVERWRITTEN: a description that has any text in it is never changed by this. RYM's version is saved SEPARATELY beside it
            (with the date it was saved and the page address), so editing your own text never loses it. On the genre page's About card:
            "From Rate Your Music · saved 23 Oct 2026 · open on RYM" when it is RYM's text; "Rate Your Music has a different description"
            with Show (reads it under yours) and Use RYM's (replacing something you wrote takes a second press) when it isn't. If RYM changes its
            text later, the saved version is refreshed and offered; your description is not silently rewritten. A genre that is only
            tagged on your releases gets its entry the first time something is saved for it. Matching is by the genre's name or its RYM address
            only, never guessed (Jazz-Rock finds "Jazz Rock").
          ⚙ → RYM ratings & genres… (renamed): a Genre descriptions section with the fill-in switch (on by default) and what the add-on has handed over.
          IMPORTANT, UPDATE THE APPS SCRIPT (apps-script-backend.gs): all your genres and their descriptions are saved as ONE value, and a spreadsheet
            cell holds at most 50,000 characters. A few dozen descriptions can already be near that; filling in hundreds of genres is far over it, and
            the save to your sheet then fails (quietly: the text stays on this device only). The new script stores a long taxonomy in several
            cells and reads it back whole (tested with 400 long descriptions, 869,000 characters, where the old script fails), and reads a taxonomy
            saved by the old script as it is. Redeploy it as a new version. If you haven't, CRATE tells you once, when your genre data passes the limit.
          VERIFIED: the rules for what may be filled (17 checks), the page (37), the add-on's reading of RYM genre pages with the real extension in
            Chromium, including a comment box that looks like a description, "Read more" clutter, line-break-only layouts, sub-pages and the off
            switch (24), the add-on's text handling (17) and the backend's chunked storage (13). NOT VERIFIED against real RateYourMusic: its genre
            page markup can't be seen from here. The add-on looks for a box whose class or id says description/about/summary and takes the longest
            plain text in it; if RYM's layout differs it says so on the page and in its options (a selector box under Advanced fixes it).


  1.31.1 — 2026-10-23 — MusicBrainz "Years active" chart: it now carries on by itself when MusicBrainz answers 503.

          Before: any one of the dozens of requests that build the chart getting an HTTP 503 (MusicBrainz is busy) stopped the whole run,
          and you had to press Retry again and again. Now each 503 is waited out and asked again automatically: 2 s, then 4, 8, 16, and
          never more than 30 s between tries (or MusicBrainz's own Retry-After, up to a minute, if it sends one). While it waits the panel
          says so ("MusicBrainz is busy (HTTP 503). Trying again in about 4 s (try 2 of 15), by itself.") and how many times it has been
          busy so far, instead of looking frozen. The other requests, and the numbers, are unaffected: what was already counted is never asked again.
          Limits, so it can't loop for ever: a single request is tried again up to 15 times in a row (about five minutes). If MusicBrainz is
          still busy after that the run stops with a clear message (how many times it was retried) and the usual Retry button, which carries
          on from where it stopped. Cancel, or closing the genre's tab, ends a wait within a quarter of a second and leaves what is loaded.
          Also fixed: a 503 during the first check of how MusicBrainz wants a date written was reported as "none of the ways I tried worked";
          it is now reported as MusicBrainz being busy. The one-number count in the meta line ("N on MusicBrainz") waits out 503s too (up to 6 tries).
          Only HTTP 503 is retried automatically; a refused or unreachable MusicBrainz, a 429 (handled as before) or any other error still
          stops the run and offers Retry.
          VERIFIED: the retry rules on their own (21 checks: the waits, the Retry-After handling, giving up, Cancel mid-wait, other statuses
          left alone, and a whole histogram run with every 4th request answered 503 coming out right in one go), and in the page (24 checks).


  1.31.0 — 2026-10-23 — RYM ratings: what each release is rated on Rate Your Music, shown next to your own, with the best in bold.

          SHOWN: "RYM 3.72" sits beside your stars on cards, in list view and on cover tags, and on the album page (with a link to the
            release on RYM). Hover it for the number of ratings. ⚙ → RYM ratings… switches it off.
          BOLD: an album whose RYM rating is at least 3.8 gets a heavy gold-tinted title; a track at least 3.8 is bold in the album page's
            tracklist, the playlist track groups and the album peek. Both thresholds are yours to change. Unrated is never bold.
            (A track's RYM rating is typed in "Edit track properties…".)
          TYPED: an RYM rating (and how many ratings it's based on) can be entered on the album page, in the Add and Edit forms, or in
            ⚙ → RYM ratings… → "Type them in": a list of the releases that have none yet, each with a link to find it on RYM; type, press
            Enter, and it saves and moves to the next. A comma decimal (3,65) and a count with a comma (12,345) are fine.
          SORT / FILTER / SEARCH: "Top RYM rated" sort; ⇅ → RYM rating: at least … and "Missing an RYM rating" (to see what's left to fill);
            search rym:>=4, rym:3.5-4, rym:none.
          FROM THE BROWSER ADD-ON (version 1.3.0, update it too): while you browse RateYourMusic artist, genre and release pages the add-on
            keeps the average and the ratings count it sees, and CRATE picks them up by itself. A release is found by its saved RYM link;
            otherwise by artist + title + type + year. A rating you TYPED is never overwritten; one that came from the add-on is refreshed when
            RYM's average moves; a single's rating never lands on its album; two releases that both fit are left alone, never guessed.
          REQUIRES AN UPDATED APPS SCRIPT BACKEND (apps-script-backend.gs: three new columns RymRating, RymVotes, RymSrc, created on demand;
            redeploy it as a new version). Until then nothing is lost: each rating is kept in this browser and sent to the sheet by itself
            once the backend can take it, and you are told once that it's on this device only.
          FIXED ALONG THE WAY (found by testing): marking a track listened and un-listened now keeps its RYM rating (the "is this track's
            data empty?" test existed in three places); the redraw after a save no longer overwrites a box you are typing in.
          VERIFIED: the matching and update rules on their own (19 checks), the page in a browser (59), the backend script against a stand-in
            sheet (13), the add-on's reading/merging (15) and, with the real extension in Chromium, capture on artist, genre and release pages
            and handover to CRATE (23). NOT VERIFIED: against real RateYourMusic (its current markup can't be seen from here: the add-on reads
            the first d.dd figure on a row and the whole number after it, which is how a discography row looks; the add-on's options have a
            selector override if it differs), and the browser's own permission prompt of the add-on's Connect button, which this test
            environment doesn't grant (the same for the previous add-on version).


  1.30.1 — 2026-10-22 — Sound analysis: Last.fm tags now understand genres, and the source test says exactly what happened.

          A real run of "Test these sources" showed Last.fm as "Reachable, but no mood tags". Two faults: the tag reader only knew
          MOOD words (chill, energetic, sad…), but Last.fm's top tags for an album are mostly GENRES and styles ("death metal",
          "ambient", "house") or things like "seen live"; and the one message covered three different situations, including a
          request that had simply failed.
          Now: genre and style words count as a rough prior (death metal reads as very high energy, ambient/classical/piano as quiet
          and acoustic, house/techno/dance as danceable and not acoustic; plain "rock" only mildly energetic). Tags that mean nothing
          here (seen live, british, favorites) are still ignored. It remains the lowest-priority source: anything from Spotify,
          ReccoBeats or the audio itself wins feature by feature.
          The test now says which case it is: it works (and for how many releases) · Last.fm has no tags for them · it answered
          with tags but none are usable (and shows which tags it saw) · it couldn't be reached (offline or blocked). A request that
          FAILED is no longer remembered as "this release has no tags", so those releases are tried again next time.
          Same run, confirmed in a real browser: ReccoBeats and Apple's previews CAN be read by this page (6 of 11 test songs known
          to ReccoBeats; a preview analysed in 2.5 s), and Essentia.js loads. Spotify's refusal (403) is expected for a Developer
          app created after 27 Nov 2024.


  1.30.0 — 2026-10-22 — Sound analysis and a Map of your crate (Stats → Map).

          THE MAP. Stats has a third mode, Map: every release is a dot, drawn on a canvas you can pan (drag), zoom (scroll, pinch,
          the + − buttons or double-click) and select from (Shift-drag, or the ▭ button where there is no Shift key).
            Views:  Sound map (releases that sound alike sit together) · Genre map (similar genres sit together, using your genre
                    tree) · Mood (sad ↔ happy across, mellow ↔ intense up) · Tempo × energy · Year × energy · Year × tempo ·
                    Year × sound · Year × genre · or any two axes you pick (year, any sound feature, the sound/genre axes).
            The axes say what they mean ("← electric · intense | acoustic · mellow →", from the features that drive them), and a
            year axis spans the years actually plotted. Colour by decade / rating / type / genre family / where the sound data
            came from; size by rating or plays; Find highlights an artist, title or genre and dims the rest.
            Click a dot: what it is, its sound numbers (each with its source) and the 8 releases that sound most like it (by sound
            if it has sound data, else by genre), ringed on the map. Select an area to Play it, Add it to the queue, or Export… it:
            the export dialog opens with exactly those releases (further filters narrow it; Clear all drops the selection; the file's
            "filter" note records it).  A "Genre network" view draws genres as circles sized by your releases, joined when they
            share releases or one falls under another; click one to open its genre page.
          SOUND ANALYSIS (⚙ → Sound analysis…) finds out how releases sound. Where it looks, best first: Spotify's audio features
            (only works for a Developer app created before 27 Nov 2024; skipped if Spotify refuses) · ReccoBeats (free, no key;
            looks songs up by Spotify id: energy, mood, danceability, tempo…) · audio analysed in your browser (iTunes' 30-second
            previews as a fallback, or your own music folder, matched to releases by Artist/Album folder names) · Last.fm tags (a
            rough mood) · optionally Essentia.js. "Test these sources" checks each on a sample before a long run; the run is
            paused/resumed/stopped from the dialog, keeps going across sources, and remembers what each source didn't know for 30
            days. A feature known from several sources uses the best one. Everything is stored in this browser only.
          THE BUILT-IN ANALYSIS is this app's own signal processing (tempo, loudness, brightness, dynamic range, rhythmic density,
            key, an estimated energy and danceability): honest estimates, NOT a trained model. It cannot judge mood (sad ↔ happy):
            that comes only from Spotify, ReccoBeats or tags. Tempo is exact from 70 to 180 BPM on test audio.
          VERIFIED: the engine on synthetic audio of known tempo and key; the pipeline against stand-ins for every service; the map
            on data with planted clusters (it recovers them: groups separate, nearest neighbours come from the right group), through
            real mouse, wheel and pinch input; a phone-sized layout.
          NOT VERIFIED against the real services: whether ReccoBeats and Apple's preview files allow this page to read them (browser
            cross-origin rules; if not, the Test says so and your own files still work), and the real Essentia.js library
            (experimental; only my code around it is tested). Apple limits lookups to about 20 a minute, so previews are slow on purpose.
          Also: the map's colours, ids and classes are prefixed smap- so they cannot clash with the older world map (🗺).

  1.29.207 — 2026-10-20 — Export from the right-click menus (artists and genres), and saved filter presets in the export dialog.

          Right-click menus. A genre row in the left panel's menu now has "Export N releases…" (the same set its "Play all N"
          uses, sub-genres included). Artist rows had no menu at all; they now open one on right-click, or on a long-press on a
          phone like genre rows do: "Open artist page" and "Export N releases…". Both fill the export dialog in exactly as
          the buttons on the Genre / Artist pages do (1.29.206), with the note saying where it came from. Disabled when there
          is nothing to export.

          Saved filters. In "Narrow it down": "Saved filters" lists the combinations you've named, and "Save these filters…"
          names the current one (e.g. "70s prog"): it keeps the genre, years, artist, types, label, rating, dates and query
          exactly as typed. Choosing one fills them all in again; editing any box afterwards clears the choice so it never
          claims more than it is. Saving under an existing name (any capitalisation) replaces it; Delete asks twice. Up to
          30, kept in this browser only (they are not in the export file). "Save these filters…" is enabled only when at
          least one filter is filled in. Damaged saved data is ignored rather than breaking the dialog.
          Not done: a year row's right-click menu; saved filters don't travel between browsers or devices.

  1.29.206 — 2026-10-20 — "Export these…" on the Genre and Artist pages; and a fix for artist names that contain commas in the export filters.

          Genre page: an "Export these…" button beside Play / Shuffle opens the export dialog already filtered to that genre,
          with the page's own choices carried over: "Include sub-genres" as you have it ticked on the page, and the year if you
          picked one in the chart. The file then holds exactly the releases the page lists. Artist page: an "Export these…"
          button next to Play does the same for that artist's releases. A note in the dialog says where it was filled in from;
          everything stays editable (narrow it further by year, type, rating…, or Clear all). Disabled when there is nothing
          to export. Opening Export from the ⚙ menu still starts empty.

          THE COMMA FIX. The Artist box in "Narrow it down" (1.29.205) separates several artists with commas, so a name like
          "Earth, Wind & Fire" or "Crosby, Stills, Nash & Young" was cut into pieces and matched nothing — and the new Artist
          page button would have hit it at once. Now a name containing commas goes in double quotes ("Earth, Wind & Fire", Yes),
          the page button quotes it for you, and a box whose whole text is an artist in your crate (what the pick-list gives
          you) is kept whole without quotes. The file's "filter" note quotes such names too.
          Note: the Artist page lists names EXACTLY as written, while the export compares names the way duplicate protection
          does (case, accents and a leading "The" ignored), so an export from the page for "The Beatles" would also take in
          a separate artist spelled "Beatles" if you have one.

  1.29.205 — 2026-10-20 — Export by parameters: narrow an export by genre, year or year range, artist, type, label, rating, date added, or a search query.

          Export crate… (⚙ menu) could only export everything or "what's showing now". It now has a "Narrow it down"
          section; the old choice is renamed "Start from" and is the base the filters narrow. Everything you fill in must
          match (AND); leave a box empty to not filter on it.
            Genre     a name, or several separated by commas (any of them). Include sub-genres (on: Funk Rock also takes in
                      Funk Metal if your genre tree files it under it, as the left panel does) and First genre only (the
                      genre must be the first one listed, as in 1.29.204).
            Year      from / to, either or both; the same year in both = one year; typed the wrong way round = swapped (and
                      it says so). Decade buttons (1970s...) for the decades you actually have. Releases with no date can't
                      match a year filter, and the dialog says how many were left out.
            Artist    names separated by commas (any of them), matched the way duplicate protection matches: case, accents
                      and a leading "The" ignored. "Partial names" lets "beat" find The Beatles.
            Type      tick the release types to keep (counts shown).   Label  contains, commas = any of them.
            Rating    at least 1-5 stars (used to choose; ratings are still NOT written into the file).
            Added     on or after / on or before a date (both end days included).
            Query     the search box's own language for anything else: scene:cool, year:>=1990 -artist:primus, etc.
          While you type the summary updates ("14 of 312 releases match") and shows what EACH filter would leave on its own
          ("Genre 14 · Years 0"), so an empty result explains itself; "Which releases will be exported" lists exactly what
          will go in (first 200). Nothing matching disables Download/Copy. Filters start empty every time the dialog
          opens, so a subset is never exported by accident.
          The file keeps its format (version 1) and importing it works as before; it just gains a "filter" note before the
          releases saying how it was made (the importer ignores it), and the file name says what's in it, e.g.
          crate-export-funk-rock-1990-1999-2026-10-20.json.
          Reused rules, not new ones: year as the search box's year:, genre through the taxonomy as the left panel, the
          artist normaliser of duplicate protection, and the search language for the Query box. Also fixed while testing:
          the dialog's existing checkbox-row styling was centring my label-over-input fields.
          Not done: filtering the IMPORT side (that already has a review step), exporting from the Genre or Artist pages.

  1.29.204 — 2026-10-20 — "First genre only": a genre filter that only counts a release when that genre is the FIRST one listed on it.

          THE PROBLEM. Every genre filter in CRATE kept a release if the genre appeared ANYWHERE in its genre list. The
          ⇅ menu's "Genre search > Exact genre only" (1.29.172) means "tagged with exactly this genre", not "first", and
          the "Closest genre match" sort and the strip on cards (1.29.127-128) only RANK releases by position; none of them
          removed a release that has Funk Rock as its second or third genre. So filtering to Funk Rock still showed those.

          THE FIX. ⇅ menu > Genre search > "First genre only" (the first entry in that section; the menu now scrolls if the screen is short) (saved in prefs, off by default). While on, a genre only
          counts when it is the release's first genre — the first tag of Genre + Secondary Genre in the order written. It
          applies, through one helper (filterGenreTags), to: the left-panel genre filter, genre: and -genre: searches (in all
          three strictness modes, and the Advanced Search genre field), and the Release Chart, so they always agree. The
          panel title says "Genre: Funk Rock (first genre only)" while it is in effect, and toggling shows a toast.
          Sub-genres still follow the strictness you chose: with the left-panel filter on Rock, a release whose FIRST genre
          is a sub-genre of Rock (Funk Rock) still counts, because that is how the genre tree already works; what changes is
          that a release whose first genre is something else no longer qualifies through a later one.
          Not changed: the left panel's per-genre COUNTS (they still say how many releases carry a genre at all), plain-word
          searches (no genre: prefix), the Genre page, and Group by Genre. A release with no genre matches no genre.
          Note -genre:"funk rock" with this on means "first genre is not Funk Rock" (a release with it second is kept).

  1.29.203 — 2026-10-20 — The browser add-on is now told each release's TYPE (Album, EP, Single, Compilation, Live, Mixtape), so it can tell an album from its single or its music video.

          Why: with the same artist and title, the add-on used to treat an album, its single and its video as one release
          (Cocteau Twins' "Heaven or Las Vegas" is all three on RateYourMusic), so a single you don't have was flagged as
          "in your crate" because you have the album. CRATE's list for the add-on now includes each release's type; the
          add-on (1.2.0) compares it with the type in RYM's release address (/release/album/…, /release/single/…,
          /release/musicvideo/…). Nothing else is shared and nothing changes for you if you don't use the add-on.
          To use it: upload this index.html, reload CRATE (Ctrl+F5), and press "Check and sync now" in the add-on's Options.

  1.29.202 — 2026-10-20 — The CRATE page now marks itself for the browser add-on (<html data-crate-bridge="1.29.202">), so the add-on can tell "this CRATE is too old to share" from "sharing is off" and say so. Nothing changes for you otherwise.

          Why: the add-on said "CRATE · not connected" for anyone whose connection was fine but whose CRATE never sent
          data (typically a CRATE hosted from an older index.html than 1.29.201, which has no add-on support). The add-on
          (now 1.0.1) has a Connection check that tests each link of the chain; this attribute is what lets it recognise
          an old CRATE. To use the add-on, the CRATE you actually open must be 1.29.201 or newer: upload this index.html to
          wherever you host it, then reload CRATE with Ctrl+F5 (Cmd+Shift+R on a Mac).

  1.29.201 — 2026-10-20 — A browser add-on, "CRATE for RateYourMusic", that flags releases on RYM artist pages you already have; and a ⚙ switch for sharing your crate with it.

          The add-on (a separate folder, crate-rym-addon, for Chrome / Edge / Brave / other Chromium browsers) looks at the
          discography items on a RateYourMusic artist page (.component_discography_item, and RYM's older .disco_release) and
          marks the ones already in your crate with a green "In your crate" badge; a release you have in a different YEAR
          gets an amber "Another edition in your crate"; a small "CRATE · 3 of 7" summary can dim or hide the owned ones.
          It matches first on the RYM address (CRATE stores a release's RYM link, and every RYM item links to
          /release/album/<artist>/<title>/, so that is certain) and otherwise on artist and title, with the same rules as
          CRATE's duplicate protection. A compilation by ANOTHER artist is never mistaken for yours by title.
          See crate-rym-addon/README.md for installing and connecting it.

          In CRATE: the add-on can only learn what you have from CRATE, so the library is offered to it as a message CRATE
          posts to its OWN window, addressed to this page's own origin (never "*"): artist, title, release date, descriptor,
          catalogue number and RYM link, and nothing else (no ratings, reviews, links to Spotify/YouTube or listening
          history). It is sent when the library loads or changes and whenever the add-on asks; only an add-on you
          installed and connected to this address can receive it. ⚙ → "Share my crate with the RYM add-on" (on) turns it
          off, and the add-on is then told sharing is off. Nothing changes for you if you don't use the add-on.

          Verified in real Chromium (the add-on's tests are in its own folder): 30 unit checks of the matching, 12 of the
          manifest, 31 of the page script on mock RYM pages, 39 of the options page and popup (with stand-ins for the
          browser's permission API), 20 of CRATE's side of the bridge, and 14 END-TO-END with the real extension loaded
          into Chromium (import a file on the real options page, watch an open RYM page flag releases live, settings
          change live, the page isn't touched on non-artist pages, and the add-on's code is isolated from RYM's own
          scripts). Breaking the compilation guard or the message origin check each makes tests fail.
          NOT VERIFIED: the live rateyourmusic.com. I can't reach it from here and it blocks automated browsers, so the
          add-on's idea of RYM's markup comes from the class you named and RYM's older markup. If RYM's real page differs,
          the toolbar popup's "Diagnose this page" shows what it found and the options let you name the right selectors.

  1.29.200 — 2026-10-18 — Fix: a right-click menu opened while the track popup (Album Quick Peek) is showing was drawn BEHIND the popup, so the part of the menu that overlapped it was hidden.

          Cause: the Quick Peek popup sits at z-index 500 and the detached mini-player at 400, but every right-click /
          action menu (album, song, artist, genre, and the second menu "Add to playlist >" opens) was at 320, so it
          went underneath whatever it overlapped. Measured in a real browser: 266 of 770 points (35%) of an album's
          menu were covered by the popup. Right-click does not close the popup (only a normal click outside does), so the
          two are routinely open together.
          Fix: those menus (.now-playing-add-menu and .track-col-menu) are now at z-index 600, above the popup and the
          mini-player. Their second-level menu is the same element type, so it rose with them.
          Checked and NOT affected: the header dropdowns (the gear, sort and view menus, and the left-panel mode menu).
          Opening any of them is a normal click outside the popup, which closes it, so they are never open together.

          Verified in real Chromium (pw_menu_z.py, 10 checks): an album's menu opened beside the open popup, overlapping
          it, has 0 of 770 points hidden (was 266); its second menu is fully visible; both are stacked above the popup
          and the mini-player; a menu opened from a SONG inside the popup is on top of the popup; a menu over a detached
          mini-player is on top; the popup isn't closed by the right-click. Two of my own first attempts at the test
          were too weak (one passed without opening the submenu) and were tightened until they could fail.

  1.29.199 — 2026-10-18 — Import review: album covers. Every release in the review now shows its cover, as a thumbnail in the list or as a grid of covers you tick directly.

          Views (a switch beside the All / New / Already in your crate / Problems tabs, remembered):
            - List (default): a small cover on every row.
            - Covers: a grid of cards, each a cover with its status on it (New / Owned / Edition? / Repeat / Problem — the
              full wording is the tooltip) and a tick box. Click a card to tick or untick it; the ones that can't be
              added (already owned, invalid) are dimmed and don't respond. A warning mark shows where a release has notes.
            - Text only: no images at all and no image requests (for a huge file or a slow connection).
          Where a cover comes from, in order, and each candidate is TRIED before it's used so a dead address falls through
          to the next: the cover address in the file; the Cover Art Archive's front image when the file links a
          MusicBrainz release; an iTunes lookup that must match artist and title (the same strict matcher as the rest of
          the app). If nothing works the release gets an initials tile, as in the rest of the app. A release you already
          own still shows its cover, which is how you recognise it.
          Only what you can see is looked up: nothing is fetched until a row scrolls into view, at most four lookups run
          at a time, and every answer is remembered, so ticking and unticking (which redraws the list) causes no new
          lookups and a 500-release file doesn't fire 500 requests. Releases that can't be imported are never looked up.
          Safety: the cover address comes from a file you didn't write, so it is only ever put on an image from code and
          never pasted into the page's HTML; a cover address containing quote marks and an event handler is harmless.
          (As with any web image, a cover that is hosted somewhere does mean your browser asks that host for it. "Text
          only" avoids that entirely.) The importer's results are unchanged: an imported release still carries the cover
          address from the file.

          Verified in real Chromium: 34 browser checks (pw_cx_covers.py) with a stand-in for each image source: the
          cover from the file; a dead address falling through to the Cover Art Archive; an iTunes lookup for a release
          with neither; an initials tile; an invalid release never looked up; no re-lookup on redraw; the injection
          attempt; the grid (cards, statuses, ticking, disabled cards, tabs, importing from it); the remembered view;
          Text only making no requests; 80 releases looking up only what's on screen, then more on scroll; at most four
          lookups at once; a phone. Looking everything up at once makes two tests fail.
          Not done: a larger preview of a cover on click; covers in the Export dialog; choosing a different cover.

  1.29.198 — 2026-10-18 — Duplicate protection: adding a release that's already in your crate now warns you, and an exact duplicate can't be added.

          Where it works. A release can only be added through the Add form's Save and through Import, so both are guarded.
            - ADD FORM. As you fill it in (or pick a search result), a banner appears at the top if you already have the
              album, and keeps up as you type the artist, title, descriptor, catalogue number or date:
                * EXACT duplicate (same album, same edition): a red banner — "Already in your crate — Yes — Close to the
                  Edge (1972). Adding it again would create a duplicate, so it can't be added." with an Open it link.
                  Save is switched off.
                * Probably a different EDITION (the descriptor or a bracketed note in the title differs, or the catalogue
                  number, or the year): an amber banner says why ("a different year (2003 vs 1972)") and asks for an
                  explicit "Yes, add this as a separate edition". Save stays off until you tick it, and a tick doesn't
                  carry over if the situation changes.
              Save ALSO checks on its own, so a save that gets past the button (Enter, a script) is still refused. Before
              checking it refreshes the app's copy of your crate if that is more than two minutes old ("Checking your
              crate…" on the button), so an album added from another device or tab is caught too. With a fresh copy
              there's no extra delay.
            - SEARCH RESULTS in the Add modal are marked "In your crate" (same pressing) or "Another edition in your
              crate" (a reissue, a different year, a "(Remastered)" title), in list and grid view.
            - IMPORT follows the same rule: an exact duplicate (already owned, or repeated in the file) can no longer be
              ticked; a different edition can (chip "Another edition in your crate", with the reason). The Add button
              re-checks right before adding (refreshing a stale crate first) and skips anything that has become an exact
              duplicate meanwhile, saying which.
          What counts as "the same album": artist and title match ignoring case, accents (Björk = Bjork), a leading
          "The", "&" vs "and", apostrophes and punctuation, and any bracketed edition note in the title. Titles in other
          alphabets are compared properly (an earlier rule would have treated them as empty). Two different albums with
          the same title by different artists are never flagged. Ratings and other fields don't matter.
          Everything else (Last.fm review "Add…", the new-releases scan, "Add as album" from the player) goes through the
          Add form, so it is protected too.

          Verified in real Chromium: 17 unit checks of the matching and edition rules (test_dup.js), 41 of the import
          logic (test_cx.js) and 100 browser checks (pw_dup.py for the Add form; pw_cx_b.py for import): the banner for
          exact and edition cases, spelling variants, live typing, catalogue numbers, the confirmation tick and its reset,
          Save refusing without the button, the stale-copy refresh (and no refresh when fresh), search-result marks in both
          views, import's tick rules and its skip of a release that became a duplicate mid-review, a phone. Removing the
          Save check, or the stale-copy refresh, each makes tests fail.
          NOT verified against the real backend (stubbed). A duplicate added from another device within the last two
          minutes can still slip through if the other device added it after this page last refreshed AND this page's
          refresh itself fails; the backend can't enforce uniqueness without a script change.
          Not done: a way to merge links into the existing copy (Open it takes you to it); checking when you EDIT a
          release into a duplicate; cleaning up duplicates that are already in your crate.

  1.29.197 — 2026-10-18 — New: export your crate to a file, choosing which links go in (Spotify, YouTube, SoundCloud, other), and import a file into a crate, reviewing everything before anything is added.

          EXPORT (⚙ → "Export crate…"). A file you can keep, share, or import into another crate.
            - Which releases: everything, or just what's showing now (the current filter/search).
            - What goes in: Tracklists and Cover art links (each a checkbox), and Links — Spotify, YouTube, SoundCloud
              and Other (the album link on any other site, and the RYM link) — each a checkbox, with one-click
              presets: Everything / Only Spotify / Only YouTube / No links. Links are sorted by what they point at (the
              host), not by the box they were typed in, so "Only Spotify" really leaves no YouTube address anywhere.
            - A live summary ("4 releases · 7 songs · links: 3 Spotify, 5 YouTube, 3 SoundCloud, 2 other · 2.4 KB") and
              a preview of the file's real contents update as you change the boxes. Download (crate-export-<date>.json)
              or Copy to clipboard. Your last choices are remembered.
            - Not exported, because they're yours: ratings, reviews, your diary and scrobbles. An automatic catalogue
              number (CRT-007) isn't exported; one you set yourself is. A release whose tracklist is looked up
              automatically (not entered by hand) is exported without songs. Song links to sites other than
              Spotify/YouTube/SoundCloud have nowhere to go on import, so they're left out.
          IMPORT (⚙ → "Import crate…"). Choose a file or paste its contents, then REVIEW before anything is added:
            - every release in the file is marked New, Already in your crate (matched the way Last.fm matches albums, so
              "(Remastered)" editions count), Repeated in this file, or Can't import (with the reason: missing
              artist/title, not a release); the invalid ones can't be ticked, a duplicate can if you really want one;
            - what each would bring (songs, links by kind) is shown per row, anything that will be left out is noted
              (an unrecognised date is blanked, a javascript:/non-web link is ignored, an untitled song is skipped);
            - the same choices apply on the way IN: Tracklists, Cover art, and the four link types with presets, so you
              can drop a platform from someone else's file. Totals under the summary follow every tick and choice;
              filter tabs: All / New / Already in your crate / Problems;
            - only the NEW releases are ticked by default. "Add N releases…" then adds them one at a time with a
              progress line and Cancel (Escape while adding also means "stop after this one"), lists any that failed
              with a Retry, and refreshes the library. SoundCloud song links are attached afterwards, once the releases
              exist. Songs are written as a ManualTracks list only when there are songs (an empty list would stop the app
              looking the tracklist up). No backend change: it uses the existing add call.
          Files that are refused with a plain message: not JSON, the wrong shape, from a newer format, no releases, or
          over 5,000 releases.

          Verified in real Chromium: 37 unit checks of the export/import logic (test_cx.js) and 90 browser checks
          (pw_cx_a.py export, pw_cx_b.py import) with a test crate mixing Spotify, YouTube, SoundCloud, Bandcamp and
          RYM links whose totals were worked out by hand: the counts, every preset, each box, the download (name and
          contents) and clipboard, remembered choices, scope; the review's statuses, selection, tabs and totals, import
          options, the exact payloads written, SoundCloud attachment, a second import finding everything already
          there, failure then Retry, Cancel, Escape, bad input, an export read by a different crate, and a phone.
          Breaking the duplicate check or the link choices each makes many tests fail.
          Bugs the tests caught, fixed before release: a start-up crash (the helper was a const used before it
          existed, which stopped the whole app loading) and awkward wording ("invalid link link").
          NOT verified against the real backend (it's stubbed): large imports go one release at a time, so a few
          hundred releases take a few minutes. REMINDER: my earlier regression suites were lost in a sandbox reset; the
          rest of the app was covered by a smoke test and the Last.fm tests only.
          Not done: merging links INTO releases you already own (they're skipped); importing ratings/diary; CSV.

  1.29.196 — 2026-10-18 — New: when CRATE opens it reviews what you played on Last.fm since your last visit, and asks which albums that aren't in your crate you'd like to add. Those plays used to be forgotten; now they're kept, and logged to the new release once you add it.

          Before: at boot the app already fetched new scrobbles, logged the ones for albums you own, and showed a
          toast counting the rest — and then moved its sync point past them, so those albums could be seen once (from
          the toast, in a dialog that had no way to add anything) and never again.

          Now, at boot (when Last.fm is set up and a first import has been done):
            - scrobbles for albums NOT in your crate are KEPT (per Last.fm user, in this browser), merged with earlier
              ones, and re-matched each boot in case you've added the album since (any that now match are logged);
            - if any albums are waiting, a "Since you were last here" dialog opens: a summary (tracks played, plays
              logged to Scrobbles for albums you own, albums and plays not in your crate, plays with no album tag that
              can't be matched), a collapsed list of the albums you own that were logged, and the albums not in your
              crate, newest first, each with its tracks (most played first), plays and last-played day;
            - each album has Add... and Ignore; or tick several: Select all / Add N selected... / Ignore selected;
              "Decide later" (also Escape, the X, or clicking outside) snoozes the review for 12 hours — a NEW album
              you've played brings it back despite the snooze;
            - Add opens the normal Add modal already searching for "Artist Album", one album at a time, with a bar
              "Last.fm review · 2 of 4 — Artist — Album" and Skip / Stop (Stop leaves the form open; closing the modal
              ends the review and says how many are still waiting). When you save, that album's stored plays are
              logged to the NEW release automatically and the next album opens. The same logging happens when you add
              an album the ordinary way. Ignored albums are remembered and never offered again (their plays, old and
              new, are discarded);
            - the sync point now moves to the newest scrobble of any kind (it used to ignore ones with no album, which
              were then fetched again every boot);
            - if writing to the sheet fails nothing is saved or advanced, so nothing is lost and the next boot retries;
              a Last.fm error is silent, as before.
          Setting (⚙ menu): "Review Last.fm listening at startup" (on). When off, plays are still logged and the
          albums still kept, but you get the old toast, which now opens this review when clicked.

          Verified in real Chromium with Last.fm stubbed: 15 unit checks of the merge/rollup logic (test_lfr.js) and 69
          browser checks (pw_lfr_a.py, pw_lfr_b.py): the dialog's contents and numbers, the sync point and requests,
          selecting, snoozing and what beats a snooze, ignoring, closing three ways, a phone, adding one album, a queue
          of two (Skip, Stop, closing), plays logged to the new release, an album added elsewhere caught at boot,
          merging across boots, 450 scrobbles over three pages, the setting and its toast, no baseline / no account,
          a Last.fm error, a failed write then a clean retry. Three protections were mutation-checked (unowned plays kept;
          a new album beats a snooze; a new album's plays are logged). The tests found and I fixed one bug: plays of an
          ignored album were still being stored out of sight.
          NOT verified against the real Last.fm. NOT RE-RUN: my earlier regression suites (about 1,000 checks) were lost
          when the sandbox was reset, so the rest of the app was only smoke-tested this time (Add modal, menu, update log,
          Match songs, genre pages) — not covered as thoroughly as before.
          Not done: matching a Last.fm album to the right MusicBrainz release automatically (you choose the pressing in
          the Add modal as usual); scrobbles with no album tag (counted, not listed); a "play count since" per album.

  1.29.195 — 2026-10-09 — Track Info panel: genre names are now links that open that genre's page.

          In the right-hand Track Info panel (the current track's fields: artist, genre, title, year...) the Genre
          value was plain text. Each genre in it is now a link to that genre's page (description, years chart,
          stats, releases) in its own tab, the same page "View genre description" opens. A release's genre cell can
          hold several, comma-separated; each is its own link, and the text reads exactly as before. They are real
          links (keyboard-focusable, with a tooltip "Open the X genre page") and clicking doesn't move the page's
          address. Other fields stay plain text. Nothing playing from your crate (e.g. a Spotify track that isn't in
          it) has no genre, so no links. Names are escaped (a genre can contain quotes, & or <), stray commas
          don't make empty links, and a genre that is only a tag works as well as a taxonomy one.
          The click is handled by one document-level handler for any .genre-link, so other places that show a genre
          can use genreLinksHtml() and get the same behaviour without more wiring.

          opens the page and a second tab, no navigation, odd names (quotes, <b>, &) as text and opening exactly
