  1.29.172 — 2026-10-03 — Genre search now follows the genre taxonomy, with
          a strictness setting; plus a dropdown-menu stacking bug found while
          verifying it.

          THE PROBLEM. genre:"indie rock" didn't show a Twee Pop release
          whose parents (in the taxonomy) include Indie Rock — although
          picking Indie Rock in the left panel does. Two separate causes:
          (1) genre: was a plain substring test on the release's genre tags
          with no knowledge of the taxonomy at all (the sidebar uses
          genreMatchesFilter, which walks the hierarchy); (2) UNQUOTED,
          "genre:indie rock" tokenizes as genre:indie AND the separate
          free-text word "rock", so even a taxonomy-aware match would still
          have lost Twee Pop to the stray "rock". Fixing only (1) would not
          have fixed the reported query.

          GENRE SEARCH SETTING (⇅ menu > "Genre search", saved in prefs):
            Exact genre only        tagged with exactly that genre
            Include sub-genres      + anything filed under it in the genre
                                    tree — same rule as the sidebar
            Sub-genres + partial    + any genre whose NAME contains the text
              names (default)       (rock -> Krautrock). The old substring
                                    search plus sub-genres, so nothing the
                                    old search found stops matching.
          Applies to genre: and -genre: (and the Advanced Search genre
          field, which builds genre: terms). The left sidebar, Release Chart
          and double-click-to-play are NOT governed by it and behave exactly
          as before — verified the sidebar still includes Twee Pop under
          Indie Rock in every mode.

          UNQUOTED MULTI-WORD GENRES. mergeGenrePhrases folds bare words
          following an UNQUOTED genre: value into it for as long as the
          growing phrase is still contained in some real genre name (catalog
          tags + taxonomy). "genre:indie rock" -> one term; "genre:rock
          beatles" is left alone (not a genre) so beatles stays its own
          AND-ed word; a quoted value is always literal; negation and later
          field terms are preserved. Known limit: if a bare word happens to
          complete a real genre name it is merged even if you meant it
          separately (quote the genre to avoid that).

          MATCHING IS NOW PER TAG, not against all of a release's tags glued
          together with spaces — previously "Indie Pop" + "Rock" as two
          separate tags falsely satisfied genre:"pop rock". This is the one
          deliberate departure from the old behaviour (a bug, not a feature).

          FOUND WHILE VERIFYING THE NEW MENU: the ⇅ menu (and three other
          dropdowns) were being painted OVER by card tags. .nav-view-menu
          asks for z-index: 20, but .panel-mode-menu — same specificity,
          declared later — silently overrode it to 5, the same value as the
          card tags (.catalog-tag/.type-tag), and cards come later in the
          page. Measured by sampling every 6px inside each open menu with
          elementFromPoint: 232 covered points across the four menus (even
          the ⇅ and view menus on desktop); after the fix, 0. This is also
          what the gear/Actions menu in an earlier mobile screenshot was
          showing (card titles across "Add album" / "Map viewer"). Fixed with
          .nav-view-menu.panel-mode-menu and #albumsViewMenu (the one
          dropdown with .panel-mode-menu alone) at z-index: 20; NOT raising
          .panel-mode-menu globally since the JS-built context menus use
          that class with their own z-index.

          Verified: 30 unit checks on the real matching/merging functions
          (every mode, the reported taxonomy, glued-tags case, never-lose-an-
          old-match property, 3000 releases ~8 ms/keystroke); ~25 real-
          browser checks (your query typed exactly as written, quoted form,
          the real ⇅ menu in all three modes, genre:rock across modes,
          negation, sidebar unchanged in two modes, persistence across a
          real reload, phone fit); menu stacking measured before/after.
          Not covered: a per-query override (the setting is global); plain
          free-text search ("indie rock" without genre:) is unchanged and
          doesn't use the taxonomy.

  1.29.171 — 2026-10-03 — Feature: double-clicking a genre in the left panel
          queues and plays every release under it. Includes sub-genres
          (double-click "Jazz" and releases tagged "Jazz Fusion" come along,
          since both this and the grid filter go through genreMatchesFilter),
          honours the search box and the Listened filter, plays in the
          current sort order, and REPLACES whatever was queued/playing — same
          behaviour as double-clicking "All Genres" (1.29.166). The genre
          stays selected afterwards, so the grid matches what's playing.

          Not a copy of the "All ___" row, because of a trap: a row's single
          click is a TOGGLE (clicking the selected row clears the filter), so
          the first click of a double-click selects the genre and the second
          DESELECTS it — by the time dblclick fires state.filtered is the
          whole library, and playing that would have queued everything.
          (Album rows are immune: they play their own entry.item.) The genre
          row therefore re-selects its own genre explicitly before playing.
          Verified the trap is real with a control test (two slow clicks end
          with no filter), not just reasoned about.

          Double-clicks that land on the expand/collapse caret are ignored —
          rapid caret clicks bubble a dblclick up to the row and would
          otherwise start playback. Extracted playFilteredReleases() so the
          "All ___" row and genre rows share one implementation.

          Tested in real Chromium with real double-clicks and a real
          Jazz > Jazz Fusion taxonomy: parent genre queues itself plus its
          child and not the rest; child genre alone; one-release genre; a
          second double-click replaces the queue; search year:2000 narrows
          it; caret double-click plays nothing and leaves collapse state
          alone; All Genres and album-row double-clicks still work. Not
          tested: real audio (stand-in players), touch devices (relies on the
          browser's native dblclick, like the other double-click actions).
          Only genre rows do this — artist rows still just filter.

  1.29.170 — 2026-10-03 — Bugfix: play a SoundCloud track, then queue music
          (e.g. double-click "All Genres") and the new track played OVER the
          SoundCloud one, which kept going. Reproduced first, in a real
          browser with stand-in engines that track what is audibly playing:
          of the six possible engine-to-engine switches, five were fine and
          SoundCloud -> YouTube left BOTH playing. Cause: "stop the other
          engines" was three hand-written copies that had drifted apart —
          starting SoundCloud stopped YouTube and Spotify, starting Spotify
          stopped YouTube/SoundCloud (but only if activePlayerEngine said so),
          and starting YouTube only paused Spotify and never touched the
          SoundCloud widget. The crate queue plays manual-album tracks
          through YouTube, so queuing music after a SoundCloud track hit
          exactly that gap.

          Replaced all three with one function, stopOtherEngines(keep),
          called first by playSpotifyUri / playYtQueueIndex /
          playScQueueIndex, so a fourth engine can't be added with a case
          forgotten. Deliberately not gated on activePlayerEngine alone —
          that is only the last engine to claim the player, and was exactly
          the value the YouTube path flipped away from 'soundcloud' while the
          widget kept playing; if the widget/player exists it is stopped
          (pausing something already paused is harmless). An engine moving
          on within itself (next SoundCloud/YouTube track of an album) is
          not stopped.

          Also closes a latent knock-on: the SoundCloud widget kept its own
          queue and its "standing in for the crate queue" flag after being
          abandoned, so its eventual FINISH event could skip the crate queue
          ahead by a track. Stopping it now clears both.

          Tested (real Chromium, stand-in engines): all 6 engine switches
          leave only the new one audible; the exact reported scenario; SC->SC
          and YT->YT continue without being stopped; crate-queue hand-offs
          YT -> SC -> YT with the stand-in flags moving correctly; a stale SC
          FINISH after the hand-off leaves crateQueueIndex untouched.

          Not tested: the real SoundCloud/YouTube/Spotify players — only
          their stand-ins, so this proves the app issues the right stop calls
          to each engine, not how each real widget responds to them.

  1.29.169 — 2026-10-03 — Feature: Shuffle toggle on the player. A button at
          the left of the transport controls (before Previous), aria-pressed,
          orange with an underline when on (not colour alone), saved in
          preferences so it survives reloads. Works on all three ways this
          app plays things:

          1. Crate queue (double-click an album, Play playlist, Add to queue,
             double-click "All Albums"): the UNPLAYED part of the list is
             reordered in place (Fisher–Yates over list[index+1..]) rather
             than adding a separate "random next" mode. So Next, end-of-track
             handoff and the Up Next panel all keep working unchanged, the
             panel shows what really plays next, and Previous walks back
             through what actually played. Toggling mid-queue never changes
             the current track or what's already played; toggling OFF puts the
             remaining tracks back in their original order (each entry
             remembers it via origSeq, sorted on restore — no second copy of
             the list to go stale). A queue STARTED with shuffle on is
             shuffled whole, so it opens on a random track.
          2. Anything added to a running queue with shuffle on is dropped into
             random positions among the unplayed tracks (crateQueueAppend)
             instead of stacking at the end — this also covers the progressive
             "play everything" loader, which would otherwise quietly un-shuffle
             as releases stream in. That loader also visits releases in random
             order when shuffle is on (positions in the original list kept as
             origSeq), otherwise "shuffle everything" would always open on a
             track from whichever release sorts first, since playback starts
             after only the first release has loaded.
          3. Standalone YouTube/SoundCloud album queues (a track's ▶ YT / SC):
             the clicked track plays first, the rest of the album is shuffled.
          4. Spotify's own album playback (the deck's ▶ Play sends a
             context_uri, so Spotify owns that queue): Spotify's shuffle
             endpoint, called on toggle while Spotify is the active engine
             and again right before each album starts (shuffle lives on the
             device, so a leftover state from an earlier album would
             otherwise override the button). If Spotify refuses, a toast says
             so rather than failing silently. With a crate queue playing, the
             toggle reorders the queue and leaves Spotify's setting alone.

          Verified: shuffle uniformity (chi-square 10.3 vs failure threshold
          49.7, all 24 orderings of a 4-track tail seen, per-position spread
          within 4% for 8 tracks); the loader with real helper code, shuffle
          off and on; and ~45 end-to-end checks in real Chromium with real
          clicks on the real button (toggle/aria/title/persist/reload; queue
          order off vs on; Next follows exactly what Up Next shows; Previous
          history; off restores order; fresh queue; adding to a running queue;
          All Albums double-click; YouTube album queue; Spotify requests;
          phone viewport, no overflow, no JS errors). One of my own test
          expectations was wrong (an off-by-one in how many Previous clicks
          reach a given track); the app was right and the test was fixed.

          Persistence note: reload-persistence initially looked flaky in
          testing (lost 25-60% of runs). Matched controls showed a plain
          localStorage key written with no app code involved, and an
          existing preference (cardSize), were lost at the same rate — a
          property of headless Chromium on file:// pages, not of shuffle
          (it uses the same savePrefs/loadPrefs as every other setting).
          Re-tested over a real HTTP origin: shuffle ON and OFF each restored
          correctly 25/25 reloads, controls 0/25 lost.

          Not verified: real audio — the YouTube/Spotify/SoundCloud engines
          were stubbed (no audio or network here), so what was tested is the
          queue/ordering logic and the exact Spotify requests sent (URL,
          method, token, order relative to the play request, failure toast),
          NOT how Spotify actually behaves when it receives them. Not tested
          on Safari/iOS.

          Behaviour worth knowing: the button lives in the player bar, which
          only appears once something is playing — but toggling mid-play
          reshuffles the rest of the queue immediately, and the setting is
          remembered. One known gap: if shuffle is switched OFF in the middle
          of a shuffle-started "play everything" load, releases still arriving
          are appended at the end rather than slotted into their original
          list position.

  1.29.168 — 2026-10-03 — Bugfix, root cause finally found: the Album Quick
          Peek's ✕ "doesn't close the popup" (reported three times). It
          was never the click handler. .album-quick-peek { display: flex }
          beats the browser's built-in [hidden] { display: none }, so
          closeAlbumQuickPeek()'s el.albumQuickPeek.hidden = true flipped
          the attribute and changed nothing on screen. Reproduced in real
          Chromium (Playwright, real mouse clicks): after clicking ✕,
          hidden === true but display stayed 'flex' with the box still
          320x377 on screen; when "closed" the popup actually sat at y=900,
          just below the fold, which is why a stray box was never noticed.

          Correction to my own earlier fixes: 1.29.159 (menus and the popup
          closing each other) and 1.29.163 (popup z-index 60 -> 500) were
          guesses at what could be covering the ✕, made without being able
          to reproduce it. Both are harmless but neither was the cause.
          Reproducing it first, with a real browser, is what finally
          worked.

          Fix is ONE global rule, [hidden] { display: none !important; },
          not another per-element patch. This same cascade bug had already
          been fixed one element at a time at least five times in this file
          (Track Info seek bar, Now Playing Bar, Chart, etc.) and kept
          returning. The previous "full sweep" checked #id selectors only, so
          it missed anything styled by class. This time the browser itself
          was asked: of 101 elements carrying [hidden], 10 were still being
          displayed — the popup, the "Closest genre match" sort option
          (meant to show only under a genre filter, was always showing), the
          player's fullscreen button, the map's category-value field and time
          scrubber, the quick-actions Listen/Remove rows, the playlist list-
          view button, and two sub-option rows in Panels configuration. All
          now 0. !important because descendant selectors such as
          ".nav-view-menu button" would otherwise still beat it. Checked
          before adding it that nothing combines the hidden attribute with a
          deliberately forced inline display (the only inline display writes
          target elements that never use hidden).

          Verified end-to-end in Chromium: popup opens/✕ closes/outside-click
          closes/re-opens, in floating AND inline styles, after switching
          between them, and at a 390x844 phone viewport; the genre-match sort
          option still appears under a genre filter (so the new rule hides
          what should be hidden without breaking what should appear); the
          Artist Information tabs from 1.29.164 (open, stats, only that
          artist's releases, close, return to Music) — first time those were
          run in a browser at all; and last version's track-search narrowing
          across the popup, Tracks view and Album-and-Tracks (previously only
          unit-tested). Not verified: real Safari/iOS touch behaviour (only
          Chromium was available), or against live Google sign-in/Sheets
          data (a stub token and stub catalog response were used).

  1.29.166 — 2026-10-03 — Feature: double-clicking "All Albums" in the left
          panel queues and plays every release currently included. Search
          to narrow it first (year:1980, genre:punk, artist:..., plain
          words) and it plays only what matches; with no filters it plays
          the whole library.

          "Included" = state.filtered, i.e. exactly what the main view is
          showing: search terms AND the Listened filter AND the chosen
          sort order — NOT sourceRaw, which is what the row's own count
          shows (search only, ignores Listened and sort). The two clicks of
          a double-click already ran the row's single-click handler
          (clears navFilter, recomputes state.filtered) before dblclick
          fires, so state.filtered is current by then. Same row serves
          Albums / Artists / Genres modes ("All Artists", "All Genres"), so
          the double-click behaves the same in all three. With nothing
          matching it says so instead of doing nothing silently.

          New playAlbumsReplacingQueue(items): multi-release sibling of
          playAlbumReplacingQueue — REPLACES the queue (a double-click means
          "play this now", not "add to what's lined up"; use the album
          context menu's Add to queue for appending). Deliberately not
          built like playPlaylistFromStart, which awaits every tracklist
          before starting: that's fine for a playlist but this can be the
          whole library, and getTracksFor() can need a network lookup per
          release. So playback starts the moment the FIRST release's tracks
          are ready (first load is a chunk of 1), and the rest load in the
          background 3 at a time via Promise.all, appended in list order so
          uneven lookup times can't reorder the queue — the end-of-track
          handoff reads crateQueueList live (same as addToCrateQueue
          extending a running queue). crateQueueId is the stale-run guard:
          a new Play or Clear stops the loader appending. Releases with no
          tracklist (or a lookup that throws/times out) are skipped and
          counted in the final toast. Queue panel re-renders are throttled
          to ~600ms since each render rebuilds every row.

          Tested the queue builder in isolation with fake tracklists/
          timings (15 checks: order despite uneven load times, playback
          starts with only release 1 loaded, skipped/rejecting releases,
          late start when the first release has no tracks, nothing
          playable, replaced mid-load, single-release delegation, empty
          input, render throttle).

          Caveats: replaces whatever is playing/queued immediately — no
          confirmation, so double-clicking All Albums with no filter starts
          the entire library. Relies on the browser's native dblclick like
          the existing album-row double-click, so not verified on touch
          devices. A whole-library queue is thousands of rows in the Up Next
          panel. If an album ends before the next release's tracks have
          loaded (only plausible with very short releases on slow lookups),
          the queue would stop at that point.

  1.29.165 — 2026-10-03 — Bugfix: searching for a track title also listed
          every OTHER track on that release in the Tracks view (reported:
          searching "do re mi" from a Woody Guthrie single showed the
          single's other track too; expected only that track). Cause: search
          matches whole releases — a track title counts toward its release's
          searchable text — and the Tracks / Album-and-Tracks views then
          expand every matching release into ALL its tracks.

          New filterTracksBySearch(item, tracks) narrows a matching
          release's tracks to the ones the query was about: a track is kept
          when every free-text / track: term is found in either the
          release's own non-track text (artist, title, year, genres, etc.)
          or that track's title, AND at least one term hit the track's
          title itself — so "woody do re mi" keeps just "Do Re Mi" (woody is
          satisfied by the artist), a track: term must be in the track's own
          title, and a purely release-level search ("woody guthrie",
          "dust bowl", artist:woody) still shows every track exactly as
          before. combinedSearchText was split so the non-track part
          (releaseSearchTextNoTracks) is reusable; its output is unchanged.
          Applied to both the flat Tracks table (row idx still refers to
          the track's position in the FULL tracklist, since it's part of the
          row key when trackNumber is missing) and Album and Tracks (whose
          context-menu handler now indexes the shown rows rather than the
          full list). Unit-tested against the real functions: 11 scenarios
          incl. quoted phrase, artist+track, track:, empty query, a release
          titled the same as its track, and negated terms.

          Known limit (unchanged, not new): release-level search only sees
          manually-entered track titles (see combinedSearchText's comment),
          so a release whose tracklist came from an automatic lookup won't
          match on a track title in the first place. Playlist track views
          have their own listing and don't use this search.

  1.29.164 — 2026-09-27 — Reworked per correction ("add a new TAB — like
          Firefox, like Chrome, see the screenshot, it has tabs"): 1.29.162
          built Artist Information as one fixed tab that got reused/updated
          for whichever artist was clicked last. The MusicBee reference
          shows genuine browser-style tabs instead — several open at once,
          one per thing opened. Now: clicking an artist name in Album Quick
          Peek opens that artist's OWN tab in the top bar, right after
          MUSIC; each has its own ✕ close button; re-clicking an artist that
          already has a tab just switches to it instead of opening a
          duplicate; closing the active tab lands on its neighbor (or back on
          MUSIC when none are left), like a browser. The Music tab itself is
          never touched — its filter/view state stays independent.

          Implementation: state.artistInfoTabs (ordered artist names) +
          state.activeArtistInfoTab replace the single artistInfoCurrent;
          the fixed #tabArtistInfo button is gone, replaced by a dynamic
          #artistInfoTabsContainer that renderArtistInfoTabButtons()
          regenerates (display: contents on the wrapper, so the buttons lay
          out as direct children of .nav-tabs on desktop and the mobile
          segmented bar alike). Click handling is delegated, with the close
          ✕ checked first so closing never also counts as selecting.
          switchTab() re-renders the tab buttons since .active now depends on
          which tab is current.

          Not built: a generic "+" button for opening a blank tab — MusicBee
          has one, but there's no meaningful "empty" tab to open in this app
          yet, so tabs only open by clicking an artist name. Also, tab state
          isn't persisted across reloads (open tabs are session-only).

          Separately investigated a pasted console error ("[UNHANDLED
          PROMISE REJECTION] Failed to fetch" from getCaaJsonUrls/
          resolveCoverArt): no CRATE change — both functions already catch
          their own fetch failures; the unhandled rejection is the Chrome
          extension in the stack trace (requests.js) logging its own wrapped
          copy of a failed request to coverartarchive.org.

  1.29.163 — 2026-09-27 — Bugfix, confirmed via another mobile screenshot:
          Album Quick Peek's close (✕) button not responding again, but
          NOT the gear-menu overlap already fixed — the mutual-exclusion
          fix from that version was checked and confirmed still correctly
          in place. Different root cause, same bug class: the popover's
          own z-index (60) is lower than several other things in this app
          that can plausibly be open at the same time on mobile — the
          drawer backdrop sits at 65, and it's not the only one. Rather
          than keep patching one specific overlapping combination at a
          time as each gets reported, raised the popover's own z-index to
          500 — above everything else in the app short of the one thing
          that should always reasonably win regardless (a full-screen
          loading spinner at 9999) — so this class of "something invisible
          is physically blocking the tap" bug can't recur here no matter
          what else happens to be open when it's showing.

  1.29.161 — 2026-09-27 — Bugfix, confirmed via console error: clicking
          "Release Chart" to enable it threw "TypeError: a.localeCompare
          is not a function" immediately, breaking the whole Layout panel
          interaction. My own bug introduced when building this element —
          getAllKnownGenres() returns {key, label, parents} objects, not
          plain strings (it even sorts internally by .label, which should
          have been the tell) — but renderChartElement() treated its
          return value as if it were already plain genre name strings,
          calling .localeCompare directly on an object with no such
          method. Would have also silently broken the includes()/===
          comparisons further down in the same function even without the
          sort throwing first, since comparing an object to a string is
          just always false. Fixed by extracting .label immediately after
          the call, so everything below keeps working with plain strings
          exactly as it was written to expect. Also caught and fixed a
          stale CURRENT_VERSION header left at 1.29.159 while
          APP_VERSION had already moved on to 1.29.160 last turn.

          album tracks doesn't work" — the button's own click handler was

  1.29.158 — 2026-09-27 — Reworked per direct request/clarification: the
          docked player (the bar with the seek timeline and skip/back
          controls) is now its own element, "Player Controls", genuinely
          separate from Now Playing Bar (display-only art/title/artist,
          Main panel only now — "Bottom" removed from its allowed panels,
          since that's what previously caused the confusing conflation
          this whole rename fixes). Player Controls can be placed at Top
          or Bottom of the screen (new "Top" panel added to the layout
          system, LAYOUT_PANEL_LABELS/state.panelLayout.top) — a new
          applyPlayerControlsPosition() just toggles which edge #miniPlayer
          docks to, deliberately still never touching its visibility,
          which stays entirely governed by the existing playback-state
          logic alone (the exact guarantee the previous version's bugfix
          established, kept intact here).

          Player Controls specifically can never be fully removed from the
          layout (dragged to Available, or unchecked) — guarded at all
          three places that could otherwise do that (a panel row's own
          checkbox, the Available list's master checkbox, and the drop
          handler itself), snapping back with an explanatory toast instead
          — it can only move between Top and Bottom, never disappear
          outright, so there's always somewhere to actually control
          playback from. loadPrefs migrates any existing saved layout with
          the old 'nowPlayingBar' sitting in bottom over to 'playerControls'
          automatically, preserving "docked at the bottom" rather than
          silently losing it, plus a second independent safety net
          ensuring it's never saved in neither panel at all.

          CSS: new .mini-player.docked-top flips which edge it's pinned to
          and which side its border/shadow reads on; the content-clearing
          #app padding (four separate rules across desktop/mobile/notch/
          narrow-phone variants) gets a :has()-based override for each one
          so content never ends up hidden under the bar regardless of
          which edge it's docked to or which device is viewing it.

  1.29.157 — 2026-09-27 — Bugfix, a real design mistake rather than
          another CSS cascade issue: disabling "Now Playing Bar" (removing
          it from every panel) was hiding the ENTIRE docked mini player,
          including its actual transport controls — play/pause, seek,
          skip. applyBottomPanelLayout() was gating #miniPlayer's whole
          visibility on whether that element was placed in Bottom at all,
          conflating a decorative display element with the only UI for
          controlling playback. The docked player now always follows its
          own original playback-state-driven show/hide logic alone,
          completely untouched by the layout system — "Bottom" stays one
          of Now Playing Bar's conceptually allowed panels (so dragging it
          there to represent the docked player in the Layout UI still
          makes sense, consistent with how this whole restriction concept
          was first described), it just no longer has any actual gating
          effect on the player itself. Removed the now-dead CSS
          (#miniPlayer.force-hidden, .now-playing-bar-disabled) that
          existed only to implement the behavior just removed.

  1.29.156 — 2026-09-27 — Bugfix, confirmed and then swept properly this
          time: "Now Playing Bar shows even when disabled" was the exact
          same display-vs-[hidden] CSS cascade bug already fixed once this
          session for .track-info-player-controls — .now-playing-bar-main
          sets display: flex unconditionally, which wins over the
          browser's default [hidden] { display: none } at equal
          specificity. Removing the element correctly updated
          state.panelLayout and set hidden = true in JS the whole time; it
          just had no visual effect.

          Rather than fix only the one reported and move on, did a full
          systematic sweep this time: extracted every element ever toggled
          via .hidden anywhere in the JS (60+), cross-checked each one's
          own CSS for the same "sets display: with no [hidden] override"
          pattern. Found and fixed three more real, if currently latent,
          instances: .chart-element, #musicViewElement, #app, and #mapMain
          — none reported yet, but #app's especially was only not visibly
          broken because #authGate's own full-screen overlay happens to
          sit on top of it while signed out, which is exactly the kind of
          thing a future unrelated change could quietly stop being true.
          One flagged match, #miniPlayer, confirmed as a false positive —
          it already has its own override, just under the .mini-player
          class selector rather than the #miniPlayer id the sweep checked
          for literally.

  1.29.153 — 2026-09-27 — Bugfix, and a better answer than my previous
          guess: pushed back on with "wouldn't it be more a catalogue
          issue, to do with access to the spreadsheet?" — correctly.
          postUpdate() (used by nearly every write in the app — add, edit,
          delete, playlist changes, everything) only ever checked for an
          AUTH error specifically; any OTHER backend failure — a script
          exception during the actual spreadsheet write, entirely
          plausible for a freshly-provisioned multi-tenant spreadsheet —
          fell straight through unnoticed, and the calling code just
          assumed success once the promise resolved at all, regardless of
          what the response body actually said. That matches "happens
          after adding, to anything" far better than a stuck filter would:
          the add can silently never land in the sheet while the toast
          still says "Added to your crate", and the catalog reload then
          correctly shows nothing new, since there genuinely IS nothing
          new — not a display problem, a save that never happened. Same
          fix in spirit as fetchCatalog's own data.error check (1.29.23),
          just applied on the write side this time instead of the read
          side. saveNewAlbum's own catch now surfaces the backend's actual
          error text when there is one, instead of a flat "try again" that
          gave no hint whether this was a one-off network blip or a real,
          recurring spreadsheet problem.

          Scope note: postUpdate() is shared by many other write paths
          across the file: they'll now correctly reject on a generic
          backend error too (better than silently succeeding), but I only
          audited and upgraded Add's own catch block's messaging — some of
          the others may still show a generic failure message, or in the
          worst case surface as an unhandled promise rejection in the
          console, rather than a fully polished, specific error toast.
          Flagging that honestly rather than claiming a full audit that
          didn't happen.

  1.29.152 — 2026-09-27 — Diagnostic improvement in response to a real
          report: a friend (new multi-tenant sign-in) saw "No sleeves
          match your search" after adding a release, for anything they
          added. Two earlier versions (1.29.23, 1.29.47) already fixed
          adjacent-looking causes — a swallowed backend error, and a
          genuinely empty catalog showing the wrong message — but neither
          matches "happens after adding, to everything": that shape points
          at a stuck filter instead (state.raw genuinely has the new item,
          state.filtered just doesn't), most likely a genre/artist/year
          click, a leftover search box query, or "Listened only" toggled
          on — the last one especially would explain "anything I add",
          since a brand-new release has obviously never been listened to
          yet. New describeActiveFilters() names whatever's actually
          active right in the empty-state message now ("You currently have
          the genre filter 'Rock' active — clear it to see everything"),
          rather than a bare "no results" with no way to tell why. Doesn't
          fix a specific stuck filter for anyone already hitting it right
          now — that's a one-click "All Albums" away — but makes this
          self-diagnosable going forward instead of looking like a broken
          add.

  1.29.150 — 2026-09-27 — Bugfix, confirmed via screenshot: Now Playing

  1.29.149 — 2026-09-27 — Bugfix, root cause confirmed via screenshot: the
          seek bar was still showing under the cover art in Track Info
          even after 1.29.148's fix, which was real but incomplete —
          applyTrackInfoPlayerMode() was correctly setting hidden = true on
          #trackInfoPlayerControls the whole time, but .track-info-player-
          controls' own CSS rule sets display: flex unconditionally, which
          — coming later in the cascade at equal specificity — overrides
          the browser's default [hidden] { display: none } behavior
          entirely. Setting the attribute was doing nothing visually. Added
          .track-info-player-controls[hidden] { display: none } to
          re-assert it. Checked the sibling video-wrap element for the same
          pattern — it doesn't set its own display property, so it was
          never affected.

  1.29.148 — 2026-09-27 — Bugfix per direct request: Track Info's "show
          playback progress instead of cover art" / "show video, if a
          YouTube link is available" toggles removed — cover art always
          shows in that slot now. Fixed at the resolution function itself
          (applyTrackInfoPlayerMode no longer reads either flag at all)
          rather than just flipping their defaults off, since anyone who'd
          already toggled one on would still have it sitting true in their
          own saved prefs regardless of a fresh install's default — this
          guarantees it can't recur from an old saved value either way.
          Both checkboxes hidden (not deleted outright) in Panels
          configuration… → element options, so nothing else referencing
          those elements needed touching. The underlying seek-bar/video-
          embed code is left in place as inert dead code rather than torn
          out, given how interconnected it is with pieces (initTrackInfoSeek,
          the shared progress interval) that aren't worth the risk of also
          unpicking right now.

          Also worth noting: caught a false alarm while double-checking
          this — a document-wide HTML balance script I'd relied on for the
          previous version's fix doesn't account for text inside HTML
          comments, and this file's own changelog comments (like this one)
          routinely mention tags such as &lt;div&gt; in prose, which the
          naive version of that check was miscounting as real structure.
          Redone properly (stripping both script contents and HTML comments
          before counting) confirms the document is genuinely balanced —
          the earlier fix was correct, the checking method just needed
          fixing too.

  1.29.146 — 2026-09-26 — Major feature: generic drag-and-drop panel
          layout, modeled on MusicBee's own Panels Configuration. Replaces
          the old system of three independent right-panel booleans and an
          unconditional, fixed main-panel view with a real element registry
          (LAYOUT_ELEMENTS: Music View, Genre Description, Now Playing Bar,
          Up Next Queue, Last.fm History, Track Info), each declaring which
          panels it's actually allowed in — Genre Description can never
          land in Right Panel, for instance, same as certain MusicBee
          elements are Left Sidebar-only. New "layout" box in Panels
          configuration… — drag a chip from Available onto Main/Right/
          Bottom to place it there, drag within a panel to reorder (list
          order = stacking order, top to bottom), drag back out or use ×
          to remove. Enforced at the browser's own dragover level (a
          disallowed drop target never even accepts the drag), not just
          checked after the fact.

          Two new elements built for real, not just registered: Genre
          Description is an editable free-text blurb per genre
          (state.genreTaxonomy[key].description, synced through the exact
          same backend mechanism the rest of the taxonomy already uses),
          shown only while that genre is filtered and staying in sync as
          you click between genres; Now Playing Bar defaults to Bottom (the
          existing docked mini player — this is now the ONLY thing gating
          its visibility, on top of its own unrelated playback-based show/
          hide logic) but can be dragged to Main instead, where it renders
          as a simpler read-only inline strip rather than duplicating the
          full player.

          Existing right-panel elements (Queue/History/Track Info) migrate
          automatically — an existing install's real prior setup carries
          over exactly via migrateLegacyPanelLayout's read of the three old
          booleans, not reset to defaults. Their own DOM sections
          (#rightPanelQueueSection etc.) were reused as-is, just reordered/
          shown via appendChild rather than rebuilt, to keep this a
          reasonable size rather than also rewriting every element's own
          internals. Each element's settings modal (previously five
          separate buttons) consolidated into one openElementConfig(key)
          dispatch, opened via the ⚙ on its chip. Caught and fixed two real
          bugs before shipping: a leftover standalone Track Info config
          handler that would have run twice, and a stale click-outside
          check still referencing a button this replaced entirely (would
          have thrown on every single click in the app).

  1.29.143 — 2026-09-26 — Feature: per-track credits (who played what,
          who wrote it), RYM-style — built from two reference screenshots.
          Track Properties editor gets a new "Credits" textarea, one
          credit per line ("Name — Role(s)"); stores into trackData
          (JSON.parse(trackData).credits) — same JSON-blob persistence the
          rest of that object already uses, so unlike Descriptor/Scene/
          Movement earlier this needed ZERO backend changes to round-trip
          correctly. New parseTrackCredits() turns that text into {name,
          role} entries for display; deliberately only splits on an em-
          dash/en-dash, not a plain hyphen — a hyphenated name like "Jean-
          Paul Smith" would otherwise get its own hyphen mistaken for the
          split point (verified against exactly that case before
          shipping). Deck tracklist gets a new "Show/Hide track credits"
          toggle (only shown when at least one track actually has any,
          same as RYM itself) that expands every track's credits at once,
          not per-row, matching the reference exactly — each shown as
          name (in the accent colour) with its role(s) underneath.

  1.29.142 — 2026-09-26 — Feature: three selectable display styles for the
          right panel's Up Next queue, via a new "View" dropdown in
          Panels configuration… → right panel (separate from the existing
          button, now relabeled "Customize Layout …", which adapts to
          configure whichever style is currently selected rather than

          - Track with Thumbnail (default) — the original row style,

          - Release and Tracks — modeled on the Playlist Explorer's own
            "Album and Tracks" view: a release header shown once, its

            after it). Two field groups under one "Customize Layout" pass
            — release fields (state.fields.queueReleaseInfo) shown once
            per group, track fields (state.fields.queueTrackInfoNested)
            repeated per track with the track's own name always shown —
            same dual-group pattern the Covers view's own two field groups
            already established, reused rather than inventing a new
            mechanism for a second config screen.

          All three emit the same .queue-row-clickable/data-queue-index
          convention, so the single click/keyboard handler driving
          "click a queued row to jump to it" needed no changes at all.

  1.29.141 — 2026-09-26 — Feature: genre taxonomy now syncs per-account
          instead of living only in localStorage (per-browser/per-device —
          a taxonomy built up on one device never showed up on another).
          REQUIRES RE-PASTING THE UPDATED apps-script-backend.gs (provided
          alongside this version) — new Settings sheet (_getSetting/
          _setSetting, generic key/value, currently just the one
          GenreTaxonomy key but built to hold others later without needing
          another sheet), doGet now includes it in the response, doPost
          gained a saveGenreTaxonomy action. Frontend: fetchCatalog applies
          the backend's copy once it arrives (source of truth once the
          account has synced one at all); localStorage stays as the fast
          local cache for the very first render before that response
          arrives, and as the fallback for an account that's never synced
          one yet. saveGenreTaxonomy() now posts to the backend on every
          real edit (fire-and-forget — a taxonomy edit is small/infrequent,
          not worth blocking the UI on), with a skipSync flag so re-
          applying what fetchCatalog just received doesn't immediately
          post it right back. Last-write-wins on conflict, same as
          everything else in this app with no fancier merge logic.

          consistent hashing onto a fixed 10-colour palette
          (mapColorHash/MAP_SYMBOLOGY_PALETTE), so the same genre/artist/
          decade always gets the same colour across renders and reopens
          without needing to persist a colour-per-value mapping anywhere.
          Genre colours by each release's own first-listed (primary) genre
          specifically, not every genre it carries. A location whose

          "Mixed" grey rather than misrepresenting it as one or the other.

  1.29.137 — 2026-09-26 — Feature: Last.fm scrobbling for YouTube-sourced
          plays specifically. Last.fm's own Spotify integration already
          scrobbles Spotify plays automatically, entirely external to
          CRATE — but there's no equivalent for YouTube, since Last.fm has
          no way to see what plays inside an embedded video. Anything
          played via a YouTube link inside CRATE never reached a Last.fm
          profile at all until this.

          New "Last.fm Scrobbling…" settings modal (API Key/Shared Secret,
          same trust tradeoff already accepted for Spotify's Client
          Credentials — the Shared Secret has to live in browser JS to
          sign each request) plus a real Connect flow: Last.fm's classic
          desktop auth (auth.getToken -> approve on last.fm's own site, no
          redirect needed -> auth.getSession exchanges the token for a
          permanent session key). onYtPlayerStateChange now tracks a
          YouTube track's own start time (reset only when the track
          actually changes — identified by its YouTube URL — not on every
          resume-from-pause or seek, both of which also fire PLAYING),
          fires track.updateNowPlaying once a genuinely new track starts,
          and checks Last.fm's real eligibility rule (played past half its
          duration or 4 minutes, whichever is less, and the track itself
          over 30 seconds) before submitting track.scrobble on ENDED.

          A standard public-domain JS MD5 was inlined for Last.fm's
          api_sig request-signing scheme (sort every param but format/
          callback, concatenate key+value pairs, append the secret, MD5,
          hex-encode) since Web Crypto has no MD5 — verified correct
          against three known hash test vectors (empty string, "hello",
          the standard pangram) before shipping, given a silently-wrong
          hash here would make every signed request fail invisibly.

  1.29.136 — 2026-09-26 — Bugfix, root cause confirmed: "descriptors for
          all releases got cleared" was never actual data loss — the
          Descriptor field has been silently failing to persist at all
          since it first shipped (1.29.107), whose OWN changelog entry
          flagged this exact gap at the time ("REQUIRES A BACKEND CHANGE
          THIS FILE CAN'T MAKE... until then the field will save via
          postUpdate but won't round-trip on reload") and it was never
          actually made — including when apps-script-backend.gs was later
          touched for the AlbumId fix, which added AlbumId to addAlbum's/
          editAlbum's `fields` arrays but not Descriptor alongside it.
          Without "Descriptor" in those arrays, _ensureColumn never created
          that column in the actual sheet at all — every descriptor typed
          in was accepted by the UI and shown immediately (the app updates
          its in-memory copy right after saving) but silently dropped by
          the backend, never written anywhere durable, reading back blank
          on every subsequent reload. Frontend's own read/write plumbing
          (fetchCatalog's r.Descriptor, both save payloads) was already
          correct this whole time — confirmed by tracing it before
          touching anything. Fixed by adding 'Descriptor' to both fields
          arrays in the backend (REQUIRES RE-PASTING THE UPDATED
          apps-script-backend.gs — provided alongside this version).
          Anything typed in before this fix is unfortunately not
          recoverable, since it was genuinely never saved anywhere to
          begin with — not something this fix can restore, only prevent
          from continuing to happen.

  1.29.135 — 2026-09-26 — Bugfix: 1.29.133's collapse caret was only ever
          inserted for rows that actually have children — meaning a row
          WITH children sat one caret-width-plus-flex-gap further right
          than a childless sibling at the exact same real taxonomy depth,
          since paddingLeft (the only thing actually reflecting depth) was
          identical for both but the caret ate extra horizontal space on
          top of it for one and not the other. The visible result: two
          alphabetically-adjacent siblings (e.g. Jazz-Rock, then Metal
          right after it) could misalign enough to look like one was
          nested under the other, purely because one of them happens to
          have its own children and the other doesn't — confirmed via a
          real screenshot showing exactly this. Now always inserts
          something in that slot regardless — a real caret for a row with
          children, an inert same-width .genre-tree-caret-spacer for one
          without — so every row at a given depth lines up consistently.

  1.29.125 — 2026-09-26 — Bugfix: 1.29.124's rate-limit handling turned out
          insufficient — a follow-up report showed a whole burst of
          DIFFERENT album/track searches still all hitting 429 together.
          Root cause: that version was purely reactive (check a
          timestamp, wait if already known-rate-limited, record a new wait
          time on 429) — which can only prevent requests made AFTER the
          first 429 is already known. If a batch of calls all fire in the
          same tick, before any of them has returned to actually set that
          flag, every one sees "not rate-limited yet" and fires anyway —
          and Spotify's real limit is tight enough that even a small
          simultaneous burst trips it outright. spotifyApiFetch is now a
          true one-at-a-time QUEUE (spotifyApiQueue, a running promise
          chain) — every call is forced to wait for the previous one to
          fully finish, plus a 150ms minimum gap, before it's even allowed
          to start, regardless of how many things try to call it at once.
          That's what actually prevents the burst itself, rather than only
          reacting after the damage's already done; the existing Retry-
          After-driven cooldown still handles a 429 that gets through
          anyway. Whatever calling code is firing off that many searches
          in one go wasn't tracked down/changed — this fix holds
          regardless of which caller it turns out to be, since every
          caller goes through this same wrapper.

  1.29.124 — 2026-09-26 — Bugfix, root cause confirmed via console screenshot:
          "Spotify lookup completely broken" (Pixies' "Caribou" not coming
          up) was 429 Too Many Requests, not CORS or bad credentials — a
          long cascade of consecutive 429s across both album and track
          searches, exactly what 1.29.123's new error toasts were built to
          surface (and did). New shared spotifyApiFetch() wraps EVERY
          api.spotify.com search/lookup call (artist search, an artist's
          albums, album search, track search all share the same client-
          credentials app-level quota) — waits out any already-known rate
          limit before firing, and if a request comes back 429, records
          how long to wait (Spotify's own Retry-After header, falling back
          to a default) so every OTHER pending/future Spotify call backs
          off too, instead of each one independently hammering the API and
          piling on more 429s. NEW_RELEASE_CONCURRENCY (New Release
          Radar's parallel-artist-check limit, the likely source of the
          initial burst — each artist fires an artist search AND an
          albums lookup) reduced 4 → 2 as the complementary proactive
          half, alongside the reactive backoff.

  1.29.123 — 2026-09-26 — Bugfix (partial — see chat): "Spotify lookup
          completely broken" report (a well-known track, Pixies' "Caribou",
          returning zero results). getSpotifyToken() (the client-
          credentials token exchange against accounts.spotify.com) and
          spotifyTrackSearchRaw() (the actual /v1/search call) both
          silently swallowed EVERY failure before this — a real network/
          CORS error and a genuine "no matches" both looked identical: an
          empty result and a console.warn nobody sees. Both now surface a
          real toast distinguishing an HTTP error status from a request
          that never reached Spotify at all (a fetch() to a CORS-blocked
          endpoint throws before any status code exists to check).

          Root cause NOT confirmed yet — flagged to the user rather than
          guessed at: Spotify has a well-documented, recurring pattern
          (most recently reported mid-2026) of their own API's CORS
          preflight handling breaking for browser requests carrying an
          Authorization header, self-resolving on Spotify's end each time
          historically — but this app calling accounts.spotify.com's
          client-credentials token endpoint directly from browser JS in
          the first place (rather than through a small server-side proxy)
          is what exposes it to that fragility whenever it recurs. Can't
          fix Spotify's own server from this file if that's what's
          happening; asked the user to check their browser console for the
          exact error to confirm before attempting a real fix (a proxy, or
          confirming instead it's an invalid/revoked Client ID/Secret).

  1.29.122 — 2026-09-26 — Tweak: genre weighting refined from a binary
          "1st/2nd tied at full weight, 3rd+ at half" split to a smooth
          harmonic decay — 1st = 1.0, 2nd = 0.5, 3rd = 0.333, 4th = 0.25,
          ... (weight = 1 / position). The binary split didn't actually
          capture the motivating case: a release with only TWO genres
          where the first is clearly the primary identity and the second
          a real but secondary descriptor (RYM lists King Crimson's "In
          the Court of the Crimson King" as primarily Progressive Rock,
          Art Rock second) — under the old scheme those two would've tied
          at equal weight. Straight halving (1.0/0.5/0.25/0.125) was
          considered and rejected — it craters too fast for an album
          that's just thoroughly tagged with 4-5 genres. New
          genrePositionWeight(i) actually extracted as its own function
          this time, replacing an inline calculation whose own comment
          referenced a "genrePositionWeight() below" that was never
          actually written.

          denser (padding 7px/6px → 4px/6px, gap 10px → 7px, title font
          13px → 12px, smaller folder caret/icon, tighter folder indent

          .left-row rules (Albums/Artists/Genres list) are untouched.

  1.29.120 — 2026-09-26 — Bugfix: Map view's basemap showed "API KEY
          REQUIRED" watermarked across every tile, even with a LocationIQ
          key already entered. Root cause: CARTO (basemaps.cartocdn.com,
          the dark_all style this used) started requiring a SEPARATE
          Basemaps API key of their own in late August 2026 — genuinely
          keyless when this was first written, and unrelated to the
          LocationIQ key entered here, which is only ever used for
          geocoding. CARTO doesn't reject the request either — it returns
          a real 200 PNG with the warning text burned directly into the
          tile image, which is why it read as a rendering bug rather than
          an auth failure. Rather than adding yet another API key to
          manage, switched to plain keyless OpenStreetMap tiles
          (tile.openstreetmap.org) and restored the app's original dark
          look with a CSS filter on Leaflet's own tile pane (invert + hue-
          rotate + a touch of brightness/contrast — the standard recipe
          for a dark map from light tiles) rather than depending on a
          third-party dark-styled tile set again. Markers/popups are
          untouched — the filter targets .leaflet-tile-pane specifically.

  1.29.119 — 2026-09-26 — Feature: Compact left nav (icons only) — new
          checkbox in ⚙ → Panels configuration…, under Show Categories.
          Shrinks the far-left Music/Playlist Explorer rail down to just
          fit its icons (148px → 52px) and hides the text labels, which
          stay available as native tooltips via a new `title` attribute on
          each button. state.leftNavCompact, persisted.

          Handled a real conflict rather than shipping something that'd
          silently do nothing for some users: applyLeftNavCompact() sets
          the panel's flexBasis directly rather than trusting the
          .leftnav-compact CSS class alone, because makeResizable's manual
          drag-resize handle (and the saved state.panelWidths.leftNav width
          restored at init) ALSO set that same property as an inline style
          — which beats a CSS class at equal specificity regardless of
          source order, the same class of bug that's bitten .art a few
          times already this session. Also fixed the init call ORDER
          itself (compact now applies after the manual-width restore, not
          before) so turning compact mode back off correctly restores
          whatever width the user had manually dragged it to, rather than
          leaving it stuck at 52px.

  1.29.118 — 2026-09-26 — Tweak: Map view felt cramped/cluttered — the map
          itself (.map-container, flex: 1 1 160px) and the location table
          below it (.map-attr-table-wrap, flex: 1 1 140px) had almost
          identical flex-grow weight, so they split whatever space was
          left over roughly 50/50 regardless of how much room the window
          actually had — the map was never the clearly-dominant element it
          should be. Map now gets 3x the growth priority and a taller
          floor (min-height 220px, up from 140px); the table is capped at
          a 190px max-height so it can't balloon and steal space back,
          scrolling internally instead. Default window size increased
          680×600 → 900×720 (still user-resizable via the existing
          drag handle) so there's simply more room overall. Toolbar
