          (full-screen, auto width/height) is untouched by any of this.

  1.29.117 — 2026-09-26 — Bugfix, the actual cause this time: 1.29.116's
          "fetch if not cached" check only looked at whether ANY cache
          entry existed for a key, not whether it actually matched the
          release's current Cover URL. The deck reads item.cover directly
          every time (item.cover || state.artCache[key]?.art ||
          placeholder), so it always shows the real, current value — but
          the grid card only ever reads state.artCache[key] (via
          applyCachedArtFor), which can go stale: if anything got cached
          for that key BEFORE this release's Cover field was set or
          changed (an earlier iTunes-fallback resolution, a leftover value
          from before an edit), 1.29.116's bare existence check silently
          kept that stale art forever, even with a perfectly good Cover
          URL sitting right there on the item — exactly the reported case
          (confirmed working when opened in the deck, never loading in
          the grid). buildCard now compares the cached art against
          item.cover directly when one is set, and re-fetches whenever
          they don't match, not just when the cache is empty.

  1.29.116 — 2026-09-26 — Bugfix: 1.29.115's cover-art recovery only
          catches a URL that was actually ATTEMPTED and failed (the
          browser's 'error' event) — but a card's <img class="art"> starts
          with NO src attribute at all, relying entirely on the one-time
          prefetchArt(state.raw) batch from fetchCatalog to eventually
          populate it. If that batch ever missed an item (still working
          through a large crate, some transient timing issue), the card
          stayed a permanently blank box forever — no broken-image icon,
          no placeholder, nothing — since no 'error' event ever fires for
          an image with no src to begin with, and 1.29.115's delegated
          listener had nothing to catch. Every card render (buildCard) now
          makes sure its OWN key is at least in flight if it isn't already
          cached, independent of whatever prefetchArt already did or
          hasn't gotten to yet. New artFetchInFlight Set guards against
          firing duplicate iTunes lookups for the same item if a grid
          re-renders rapidly (typing a search query) before the first
          request resolves — cleared in fetchArt at every one of its exit
          points. Same TDZ placement as badArtUrls (1.29.115) applied
          up front this time: declared before startApp(), not next to
          fetchArt further down, since buildCard runs as part of that
          same early render cascade.

  1.29.115 — 2026-09-26 — Bugfix: album cover artwork sometimes not
          loading even when the release genuinely has a Cover URL set.
          Root cause: fetchArt() only ever ran its iTunes-lookup fallback
          when item.cover was BLANK in the first place — a populated Cover
          URL was assigned straight to the <img> with zero validation that
          the image behind it still actually loads. Cover Art Archive/
          MusicBrainz images get replaced or removed by other editors,
          iTunes CDN links occasionally expire — either way, the art just
          silently failed forever with nothing else attempted. New single
          delegated 'error' listener (capture phase — the event doesn't
          bubble) in bindEvents() catches every img.art load failure
          anywhere in the app at once (grid cards, deck, quick peek,
          anniversary banner, timeline, map — all of it, no per-view
          wiring needed) and retries via the same iTunes lookup fetchArt()
          already uses, falling back to the generated placeholder if that
          also comes up empty. badArtUrls tracks the specific URL that
          already failed per key so a permanently-dead link doesn't retry
          every re-render, while still allowing a genuinely new failure
          (a different cover edited in later, or the fallback itself going
          bad) to attempt recovery again.

          Caught before shipping: badArtUrls needed to live above
          startApp() rather than next to recoverFailedArt further down the
          file — bindEvents() (which registers the listener) runs
          synchronously as part of that same early cascade, the identical
          TDZ trap hit twice already this session with the genre taxonomy
          and album-quick-peek variables.

  1.29.114 — 2026-09-26 — A batch of fixes/features from notes:

          Genre weighting: a genre's 1st/2nd position in the combined
          Genre/Secondary Genre field now counts fully in buildGenreTree's
          per-genre counting; 3rd position or later counts at half weight
          — a release's primary sound should carry more than an incidental
          extra tag. Shows up in the left panel's Genre counts and Release
          Stats' "By genre" ranking (rounded to 1 decimal for display,
          full precision used for aggregation/sorting).

          Track-genre scope corrected: 1.29.111 let a per-track genre
          override change which genre(s) the RELEASE itself counted under
          in the taxonomy/filtering/search/grouping — reverted that; a
          track's own genre override now only affects what shows in that
          track's own Genre column in the flat Tracks table (trackColumnVal),
          never the release's own classification. buildGenreTree,
          groupKeysFor, the free-text search index, and the genre: search
          operator are back to album-level genre only.

          Genre tree styling: root genres now render bold/full-brightness;
          children get a "↳" connector, shrink slightly, and dim
          progressively with depth — parent vs. child reads at a glance
          instead of only being implied by indentation.

          Player-stuck bugfix: a Spotify play request failing with
          anything other than 403 was silently swallowed (console.warn
          only, no toast, no recovery) — if the device_id ever went stale
          (a real Web Playback SDK gap: `not_ready` doesn't reliably fire
          for every way this browser's device can drop out from under
          Spotify's own list), every subsequent play request would 404
          silently while whatever was already playing just kept going.
          sendSpotifyPlayRequest now retries once on a 404 by re-finding
          this browser's own device by its fixed name ('CRATE') via
          recoverStaleSpotifyDeviceId, and every failure path shows a real
          toast now instead of failing invisibly.

          Playing a single track now clears the crate queue first
          (playTrackNow) — confirmed safe since playCrateQueueIndex, the
          actual queue-advance path, has its own fully separate playback
          logic and never calls playTrackNow, so this can't sabotage the
          very queue it's supposed to play from.

          Gap-toggle bugfix: "Close gaps between artwork" was only ever
          reducing the horizontal (column) gap between covers in the same
          row — the vertical (row) gap between different rows of covers
          was left at the base 20px regardless. Now reduces both together
          with one toggle (both the desktop and mobile variants).

          Group-by-genre scoping bugfix: with a genre nav-filter active in
          the left sidebar (e.g. Rock), "Group by Genre" in the main panel
          was creating sub-groups for EVERY genre an album carries, even
          ones with nothing to do with the active filter (Baroque Pop,
          Avant-Prog showing up while filtering for Rock, just because a
          Rock-tagged album also had one of those as a secondary genre).
          groupKeysFor now filters a release's own genres down to just the
          ones that actually match or descend from the active genre filter
          before grouping by them.

          Album covers view parity: the quick-peek tracklist (opened by
          clicking a cover) now has the same right-click context menu
          (play, queue, playlist, edit properties, etc.) the flat Tracks
          table and deck tracklist already had — including acting on the
          whole multi-selection if the right-clicked row is part of one —
          and its track rows can no longer have their text highlighted via
          click-drag, matching every other track list in the app.

  1.29.113 — 2026-09-25 — Bugfix: "Cannot read properties of undefined
          (reading 'forEach')" when adding a new genre via the Genre
          Taxonomy editor's "+ Add a genre" button. That handler was still
          writing the OLD pre-1.29.82 single-parent shape
          ({ label, parent: '' }) instead of the current multi-parent array
          ({ label, parents: [] }) — a spot the original migration missed.
          Every taxonomy function (getAllKnownGenres, buildGenreTree, ...)
          calls .forEach directly on entry.parents, which threw the moment
          a genre added this way got looked up (immediately, since
          renderGenreTaxonomyEditor re-renders right after adding one).
          Anyone who already hit this has a broken entry sitting in their
          browser's saved taxonomy — no separate fix needed for that,
          though: loadGenreTaxonomy's existing migration guard already
          repairs any entry missing a real parents array generically, not
          just the specific old `parent` string case, so it self-heals on
          next reload.

  1.29.112 — 2026-09-25 — Feature: Group by now supports a second, nested
          variable — a new "Then by ▸" flyout inside the existing "Group
          by ▸" menu (Artist/Year/Decade/Genre/Type, same options as the
          primary), e.g. group by Year, then within each year sub-group by
          Type. state.groupBy2, persisted, default 'none' (unchanged
          behavior until set). renderGrid() and renderPlaylistCoversView()
          both recurse groupItems() a second time onto each primary
          group's own items when groupBy2 isn't 'none', rendering a
          visually-subordinate sub-header (new .group-header-sub — smaller,
          no border, indented) before each sub-section rather than a second
          peer-level heading. A multi-genre album that already appears
          under two primary groups can land under different secondary
          sub-groups within each, same multi-membership behavior the
          primary grouping already had.

          One real bug caught and fixed while wiring the menu: the existing
          "#groupBySubmenu button" selector (both the click handler and
          updateGroupByMenuUI's highlighting) matched ALL descendant
          buttons, which would have included the new nested "Then by"
          flyout's own parent button and options too — clicking "Then by"
          would have stomped state.groupBy with undefined. Scoped both to
          "#groupBySubmenu > button[data-group]" (direct children only)
          before it ever shipped.

  1.29.111 — 2026-09-25 — Feature: a track's own genre (set via "Edit track
          properties…") now actually propagates through the app instead of
          sitting stored but unused. New allGenresForItem(item) — a
          release's own genre/secondary genre PLUS every distinct genre
          found across its trackData overrides — cheap to compute since
          trackData lives on the crate item itself, no tracklist fetch
          needed. Replaces the plain getGenreList(combinedGenreStr(item))
          call at every place genre actually drives behavior: the Genre
          Taxonomy tree/editor (buildGenreTree/getAllKnownGenres, and by
          extension Release Statistics' "By genre" breakdown, which already
          reuses buildGenreTree), the left panel's Genre filter
          (searchFilteredRaw's matchesNav), "Group by Genre"
          (groupKeysFor), the free-text search index, and the genre:
          search operator — so an album with one Ambient-tagged track
          surfaces under Ambient in all of those without needing the whole
          release tagged Ambient. Left combinedGenreStr's plain-text
          display callers alone on purpose (the Map viewer's attribute
          table, and keyToVal's own 'genre' resolution used by grid/cover
          card fields) — folding every track's genre into a single
          summary column would misrepresent an album as being a genre
          only one of its tracks actually is.

          Also fixed the one place genre is displayed per-track already —
          the flat Tracks table's Genre column (trackColumnVal) now checks
          that specific track's own override first, falling back to the
          album's genre exactly as before when it has none; trackSortValue
          updated to match so sorting by Genre there doesn't disagree with
          what's actually shown.

  1.29.110 — 2026-09-25 — Feature: the real fix for the row-identity bug
          1.29.109's duplicate-row banner could only detect and surface,
          not fix. REQUIRES THE UPDATED apps-script-backend.gs (paste it in
          and run backfillAlbumIds() once) — every album now gets a
          permanent AlbumId (a UUID, generated server-side in addAlbum so
          it can't be spoofed/duplicated by a stale client payload) that
          never changes and is never reused, unlike a spreadsheet row
          number. New playlist items now reference that instead of a row
          number wherever the crate item they're built from has one:
          openTrackContextMenu's single-track case, both multiselect
          selectedItems builders (deck tracklist and the flat Tracks
          table), the album card/deck/quick-actions "Add to playlist"
          entry points — all 7 payload construction sites now send
          albumId alongside row.

          playlistAlbumFor (the actual function that resolves a playlist
          item back to its album, and the site of 1.29.108's earlier
          type-coercion fix) now checks albumId FIRST when the item has
          one, and — importantly — does NOT fall back to a row-based guess
          if an albumId is present but matches nothing, since that would
          risk resurrecting the exact wrong-album bug this exists to
          prevent. Falls back to the legacy row-based lookup only for
          items that predate this change and never got one.
          removeAlbumFromActivePlaylist and activePlaylistItemIdsForTracks
          (the other two spots 1.29.108 patched) got the same albumId-
          first treatment for consistency, though most of their own
          call sites weren't updated to actually supply one — see the
          per-site comments.

          Old playlist items with no albumId keep working exactly as
          before (row-based lookup, including its known duplicate-row
          limitation) — this only closes the door on the bug for anything
          added FROM NOW ON. A specific already-wrong playlist entry needs
          to be removed and re-added to actually pick up a real albumId.

          NOT done in this pass: Diary entries reference albums by Row the
          same way playlist items used to, and are theoretically exposed
          to the identical bug — out of scope here since it wasn't the
          reported symptom, but worth the same treatment eventually.

  1.29.109 — 2026-09-25 — Feature: duplicate-row diagnostic. Root-caused a
          report of playlist tracks showing a completely different album's
          title/artist ("Train In Vain" — a Clash song — showing under a
          different artist entirely) beyond what 1.29.108 already fixed —
          that fix handled the type-mismatch half of row lookups, but
          playlistAlbumFor (and anything else that looks an album up by
          its spreadsheet Row) has no way to disambiguate when two DIFFERENT
          albums genuinely share the same Row number in the sheet itself —
          it deterministically returns the first match, which can be the
          wrong one. That's a data-layer problem this file can't fully fix
          (playlist items would need to reference something sturdier than
          a row number — a backend/apps-script-backend.gs change), so
          instead of leaving it a silent, confusing mismatch: new
          checkForDuplicateRows(), run once per fetchCatalog() load,
          scans state.raw for any Row value shared by more than one album
          and shows a new dismissible banner (#duplicateRowBanner, mirrors
          the existing anniversary banner's exact markup/dismiss-for-the-
          day pattern) listing every colliding row and the albums fighting
          over it, so the actual duplicate can be found and fixed directly
          in the sheet. Also logs full details to the console for anyone
          who wants the exact row numbers.

  1.29.108 — 2026-09-25 — Bugfix: "Playlist view: details get mixed up
          (artist names appear as albums, titles swapped)". Root cause was
          type-inconsistent row matching in playlistAlbumFor() — every
          playlist view (List/Tracks/Album and Tracks/Covers/Artists) goes
          through this one function to turn a playlist item's saved Row
          into the live album object it displays, and it compared
          `r.row === playlistItem.row` with no coercion. PlaylistItems'
          Row column can round-trip from the sheet as either a real number
          or a numeric string depending on how it was written (same class
          of issue already patched for TrackName in 1.29.48), so on a
          mismatch this either matched nothing, or — on a crate with
          recycled/duplicate row numbers (see the still-open "Duplicate
          album in the list" bug) — silently matched a DIFFERENT album
          that happened to share that row, showing its title/artist next
          to tracks that weren't actually its own. loadTracksForPlaylistGroups
          a few hundred lines down already coerced both sides with
          String() for this exact same lookup — playlistAlbumFor was the
          one place that didn't. Now it does, and the same fix went into
          the two other row-identity comparisons that had the identical
          gap: removeAlbumFromActivePlaylist and
          activePlaylistItemIdsForTracks (both used by "Remove from
          playlist"). NOTE: this closes the type-mismatch half of the bug,
          but if the crate ever legitimately has two albums sharing the
          same row number (the duplicate-row bug above), playlistAlbumFor
          will still deterministically return the first match rather than
          the right one — fixing that fully needs playlist items to carry
          something more stable than a spreadsheet row (or the backend to
          guarantee row uniqueness), which is a backend change this file
          can't make.

  1.29.107 — 2026-09-25 — Feature: release-level Descriptor field. New
          free-text field ("e.g. Deluxe Edition, Remastered") alongside
          Catalog No. in "Add a release" and "Edit release" (addDescriptor/
          editDescriptor), plumbed the same way Catalog already was —
          included in saveNewAlbum's/saveEditAlbum's payload, read back off
          activeItem.descriptor in openEditModal, added to ADD_FORM_FIELD_OPTIONS
          (so it can be hidden via ⚙ → Panels configuration…) and to
          FIELD_OPTIONS/keyToVal (so it can be picked as a customizable card
          tag), and folded into the search-matching string alongside
          artist/title/catalog. REQUIRES A BACKEND CHANGE THIS FILE CAN'T
          MAKE: apps-script-backend.gs needs a new "Descriptor" column read
          in fetchCatalog() and written on addAlbum/editAlbum, same as
          Catalog/Location were added before — until then the field will
          save via postUpdate but won't round-trip on reload.

  1.29.106 — 2026-09-25 — Feature: in-app SoundCloud playback. A per-track
          SoundCloud link (1.29.104) previously only ever opened in a new
          tab — "playing" that track had no in-app effect at all. SoundCloud
          is now a full third master-player engine alongside Spotify/
          YouTube (activePlayerEngine can be 'soundcloud'), built on
          SoundCloud's real Widget API (loaded lazily from
          w.soundcloud.com/player/api.js, same lazy-load-once pattern as
          the YouTube IFrame API) rather than a plain unscriptable embed —
          that's what gives it real transport controls (play/pause, seek,
          volume) wired into the same shared mini-player chrome the other
          two engines already share. The widget itself is mounted in a new
          off-screen #scPlayerMount (see .sc-player-mount) rather than a
          visible video-style wrap like YouTube's — SoundCloud is audio
          only, so the mini player keeps showing the track's own art
          (renderMiniPlayerTrack-style item.cover / artCache lookup)
          instead of swapping in a player face. playTrackNow() and
          playCrateQueueIndex() both now check trackData.soundcloudLink
          right after the existing Spotify-override/YouTube-override
          checks (openSoundcloudPlayer / playCrateTrackOnSoundcloud,
          mirroring openYoutubePlayer / playCrateTrackOnYoutube exactly),
          so a saved SoundCloud link now wins over the automatic Spotify
          search and actually plays here — a manually-tracklisted album
          with only a SoundCloud link saved is no longer stuck showing
          "no link saved for this track". Every place that branched on
          activePlayerEngine === 'youtube' for the shared seek bar/volume
          slider/Prev-Next/pause-on-Spotify-start/queue-panel icon picked
          up a matching 'soundcloud' branch (getEstimatedPosition,
          getCurrentDurationMs, updateProgressUI, initSeekDrag,
          masterPlayerStepTrack, playSpotifyUri's mutual-exclusion guard,
          isStaleSpotifyPauseWhileYoutubePlaying, addToCrateQueue's
          already-playing fold-in, the Up Next panel's per-row source
          icon). The existing "SC ↗" per-track link/context-menu row is
          untouched — it still just opens the SoundCloud page in a new
          tab, same as "Open on SoundCloud" always has, for anyone who
          wants the real page rather than in-app playback.

  1.29.105 — 2026-09-25 — Feature: genre autocomplete. The Genre/Secondary
          Genre fields (Add album, Edit album, Edit track properties) now
          suggest genres already used somewhere in the crate as you type,
          via a new shared #genreAutocompleteList <datalist> populated by
          refreshGenreAutocompleteList() (reuses the existing
          getAllKnownGenres() from the Genre Taxonomy editor, so taxonomy-
          only labels suggest too). Refreshed fresh each time one of those
          forms opens rather than kept live, since it's cheap to recompute
          and those are the only places it's read.

  1.29.103 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: "Cannot access 'quickPeekItem' before
          initialization" on every load — the exact same TDZ trap as the
          genre-taxonomy bug earlier in this file's history, just with a
          new victim. closeAlbumQuickPeek() got a defensive call added to
          renderGrid()/renderPlaylistBody() in 1.29.99 (protecting against
          an inline peek getting destroyed by those functions' own
          innerHTML wipe) — but those two functions run synchronously very
          early via the startApp() cascade, before the script's own
          execution pointer had reached the later `let quickPeekItem`
          (and its five sibling `let`s) declared alongside openAlbumQuickPeek
          much further down the file. Moved all six declarations to
          directly above the startApp() call, same fix shape as before —
          the functions themselves (openAlbumQuickPeek/closeAlbumQuickPeek)
          stayed exactly where they were, since function declarations are
          hoisted and safe regardless of position; only the `let` bindings
          needed to move.

          every field in the form (Artist, Release name, Type, Release
          date, Recorded, Genre, Secondary genre, Region/Language,
          Location, Label, Catalog No., Link, RYM link, Cover art URL,
          Tracklist) as its own checkbox — unchecking one hides it from the

          actual HTML carries a matching data-add-field attribute,
          applyAddFormFieldVisibility() just walks them and toggles
          .hidden against state.addFormVisibleFields, so there's no
          per-field show/hide logic to maintain as fields get added later.
          A hidden field's underlying value is left completely alone
          either way — this only controls whether it's shown for editing.

  1.29.93 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix + feature. Bugfix: the "covers are too
          large" report was a real CSS cascade bug, not a preference gap —
          #rightHistoryPanelBody's thumbnails carry the shared .art class
          (for cached-art loading, same as album cards elsewhere), and
          .art's own width:100%/height:100% is declared LATER in the
          stylesheet with equal specificity to .right-history-thumb's
          34x34 sizing, so it silently won and stretched every thumbnail
          to fill the whole row. Fixed with a combined-class override
          (.right-history-thumb.art) that outranks it regardless of source
          order — likely what actually made the whole panel feel "doesn't
          work", not a genuine settings-modal bug (nothing wrong was found
          in configureHistoryBtn/renderConfigSlots('lastfmHistory') on
          review — flag specifics if Panel Settings itself still misbehaves
          after this).

          Feature: full List/Covers parity with the library, per request —
          new ☰/▦ toggle (state.lastfmHistoryViewMode) next to the existing
          ≡/⊟ grouping toggle (renamed from ☰/⊟ to avoid two identical
          glyphs sitting side by side) in the History section's panel-bar.
          Grid mode (new .right-history-grid, properly sized 72px cells
          this time, not the buggy 100%-of-row-width from before) shows
          only the first configured field as a caption under each cover —
          a 3-line layout doesn't fit under a small thumbnail the way it
          does in a full-width row, same reasoning as the Add-album search
          grid cards.

  1.29.86 — 2026-09-16 — Follow-up to 1.29.85: double-clicking a second
          album row while one was already queued/playing was just
          appending onto the existing queue (queueAlbumTracks ->
          addToCrateQueue's normal "extend the tail" behavior) instead of
          replacing it — not what double-click should do. New
          playAlbumReplacingQueue() clears crateQueueList outright and
          starts fresh with just the newly double-clicked release,
          bumping crateQueueId the same way addToCrateQueue's own
          fresh-queue branch does so no stale end-of-track heuristic from
          the old queue can fire against the new one. queueAlbumTracks
          itself (the album card menu's actual "Add to queue") is
          untouched — appending is the correct behavior there.

  1.29.85 — 2026-09-16 — Feature: double-clicking an album row in the left
          panel's Albums list now queues the whole release and starts
          playing it — queueAlbumTracks() (same as the album card menu's
          "Add to queue"), which builds a real tracklist via getTracksFor()
          and hands it to addToCrateQueue(); that already starts playback
          immediately when nothing else is currently playing, so this
          reads as "play" for the common case while also populating the Up
          Next queue. Deliberately not playAlbumNow() (the card menu's
          "▶ Play") — that just hands Spotify one album URI directly, with
          no CRATE-queue integration and no way to work at all for a
          manually-tracklisted (non-Spotify) album.

  1.29.83 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: the "falls under" field's autocomplete
          stopped suggesting anything after the first comma — typing
          "Punk Rock, G" doesn't match any real option, since native
          <datalist> matches the ENTIRE input value, not just whatever's
          being typed after the last comma. Now rewrites the shared
          datalist's own options on every keystroke to include what's
          already typed as a literal prefix ("Punk Rock, Glam Rock"
          becomes a real option the moment "Glam Rock" matches), so each
          comma-separated segment gets its own working suggestions —
          already-used genres for that field are excluded so it won't
          suggest a duplicate.

  1.29.82 — 2026-09-16 — Feature: Genre Taxonomy now supports multiple
          parents per genre, not just one — some genres are a genuine
          fusion (Glam Punk = Punk Rock + Glam Rock), not a single clean
          branch. Storage moved from a single `parent` string to a
          `parents` array per entry (loadGenreTaxonomy migrates any old
          single-parent entry automatically, no data lost). The editor's
          "falls under" field now takes a comma-separated list, same
          convention as the Genre/Secondary Genre fields elsewhere, and
          autocompletes each one individually. getGenreAncestors is now a
          real breadth-first walk over what's a DAG rather than a strict
          tree — collects ancestors reached through EITHER of a genre's
          parents, so filtering by "Punk Rock" OR "Glam Rock" both
          correctly surface Glam Punk-tagged albums, and the left panel's
          tree shows a multi-parent genre as its own row under each
          applicable parent branch (its count still only reflects real
          tagged albums either way — no double-counting distinct albums,
          just appearing in more than one place in the tree). Seeded
          "Glam Punk" itself into the default starter taxonomy as a
          working example of this.

  1.29.81 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: "Cannot access 'DEFAULT_GENRE_TAXONOMY'
          before initialization" thrown on every load. Cause: startApp()
          is invoked as a bare top-level statement partway through the
          script, running synchronously into init() -> loadGenreTaxonomy()
          before the script's own execution pointer had reached the later
          line declaring DEFAULT_GENRE_TAXONOMY/GENRE_TAXONOMY_KEY further
          down the file — a classic const temporal-dead-zone trap; the
          functions referencing them are hoisted and safe regardless of
          position, but the consts themselves are not usable until their
          own line actually runs. Moved the whole taxonomy data/logic
          block (both consts + genreTaxonomyKey/loadGenreTaxonomy/
          saveGenreTaxonomy/getGenreParent/getGenreAncestors/
          genreMatchesFilter/wouldCreateGenreCycle) to before the
          startApp() call. buildGenreTree/getAllKnownGenres/
          renderGenreTaxonomyEditor stayed put — they're only ever
          invoked later via genuinely deferred paths (buildLeftList,
          click handlers), never during this same early synchronous pass.

  1.29.80 — 2026-09-16 — Feature: Genre Taxonomy. Genres can now belong
          under a broader one (Shoegaze under Alternative Rock, itself
          under Rock) — filtering by a parent genre in the left panel's
          Genres list now also surfaces everything beneath it in the
          hierarchy, not just albums tagged with that exact genre string.

          New ⚙ → "Genre Taxonomy…" editor lists every genre CRATE
          knows about (tagged on an album, or purely organizational) with
          a "falls under" field per genre — blank means top-level.
          Autocompletes from existing genre names but accepts anything
          typed, so a parent doesn't need to already be tagged on an
          album itself. Seeded with a modest starter set of common,
          widely-known genre relationships (DEFAULT_GENRE_TAXONOMY) on
          first run only — fully editable/removable, same as anything
          added by hand. Per-browser (localStorage,
          crate_genre_taxonomy_v1), same as Spotify/Discogs/YouTube API
          settings — not crate data, doesn't round-trip through the
          backend.

          Core pieces: genreMatchesFilter() (genre IS the filter, or has
          it anywhere in its ancestor chain via getGenreAncestors()) now
          backs the actual navFilter match, replacing a plain string-
          equality check. The left panel's Genres list (buildGenreTree())
          is now a real indented tree instead of a flat list, with each
          genre's count AGGREGATED to include every descendant's —
          "Rock" shows a count that includes Alternative Rock and
          Shoegaze tagged albums, consistent with what clicking it now
          actually surfaces. wouldCreateGenreCycle() blocks an edit that
          would make a genre its own ancestor.

  1.29.79 — 2026-09-16 — Tweak: mini player looked cramped/unpleasant when
          a modal (Quick Actions) was open above it — sitting flush against

  1.29.78 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: the actual search icon vertical-alignment
          bug (1.29.76/77 fixed real but different issues along the way —
          height and width mismatches that were genuinely there, just not
          THIS one). Desktop's .search-icon rule centers itself inside the
          text input using position:absolute + transform:translateY(-50%).
          The mobile override resets position back to static (turning it
          into a real flex-laid-out button) but never reset transform —
          and transform applies independent of positioning scheme, so that
          leftover translateY(-50%) kept shifting the button upward even
          as a static flex item, while every size/shape property matched
          its .icon-btn siblings exactly (which is why the two inspector
          size checks kept coming back clean — this was never a sizing
          issue). Added transform: none to the mobile rule.

  1.29.77 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix (root cause found via live inspector, per
          screenshots — #searchWrap measured 42x40 while #searchIconBtn
          inside it measured exactly 40x40): the collapsed-state
          #searchInput rule set border-color: transparent but never zeroed
          border-WIDTH, so the base rule's 1px border (2px total, left +
          right) stayed real width even at width:0, padding through
          #searchWrap's own flex sizing as 2px of invisible dead space —
          exactly the stray width making the search icon's wrapper wider
          than its plain 40px .icon-btn siblings, which is what was
          actually throwing off the row's alignment (not a shape or
          height mismatch, both already fine). Now sets border-width: 0
          instead, with the focused/expanded state rule restoring
          border-width: 1px (border-COLOR deliberately left alone there so
          #searchInput:focus's own accent-color override still wins on
          specificity, rather than a redundant color fighting it).

  1.29.76 — 2026-09-16 — Follow-up to 1.29.75: .search-wrap now has an
          explicit height: 40px, matching its sibling .icon-btn's (queue,
          gear) exactly. Without it, the wrapper's height was implicit —
          sized to whatever's tallest inside it, including the collapsed-
          but-still-present #searchInput — and if that invisible input's
          own box ended up even slightly taller than 40px, the wrapper
          (and therefore how .controls centers it against its plain-button
          siblings) could land a pixel or two off despite the visible
          search icon itself sharing identical size/style rules with them.

  1.29.75 — 2026-09-16 — Tweak: mobile topbar height reduced by roughly one
          whole row. .controls (search/queue-toggle/gear — the only icons
          left here after applyMobileOverflowMenu() moves the rest into the
          gear's dropdown) was unconditionally forced onto its own full-
          width row below the wordmark (flex: 1 1 100%), even though those
          3 compact icons comfortably fit inline alongside "CRATE" and the
          ☰ button on one row on any phone width this targets — that
          full-row forcing only actually needs to happen while the search
          box itself is expanded (focused, or holding a query). Now sits
          inline by default; a new :has() rule (.topbar:has(.search-wrap
          :focus-within), .topbar:has(.search-wrap.mobile-search-has-
          -value)) restores the full-row expansion only in those two cases,
          same as before.

  1.29.74 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: the mini player (and the .nav-tabs bar
          it docks above) left an empty gap below them, the height of
          Safari's own bottom toolbar, right after that toolbar collapsed
          on scroll — described as "the bottom tabs hide, leaving a gap
          behind". Cause: a well-known iOS Safari quirk where
          `position:fixed; bottom:0` elements can stay visually anchored to
          the OLD (toolbar-showing) viewport edge for a moment after the
          toolbar retracts, instead of immediately re-anchoring to the new,
          taller visible area. New syncMobileChromeGap() compares
          window.innerHeight (tracks the large/toolbar-collapsed layout
          viewport) against visualViewport.height + offsetTop (the actually
          -visible area right now) on every visualViewport resize/scroll
          event, and feeds the difference into a --mobile-chrome-gap CSS
          custom property that .nav-tabs and .mini-player now add to their
          own `bottom` offset — self-correcting continuously rather than
          only on the next unrelated reflow. Resolves to 0px (no-op)
          anywhere the bug doesn't apply — desktop, Android, or any browser
          without visualViewport support.

  1.29.73 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: Quick Actions opened with its header/title
          cut off above the visible screen, plus a stray empty gap and odd
          mini-player spacing near the bottom — reported right after
          1.29.70/1.29.72's long-press-on-cover added a new way to reach it
          directly, without the deck open first. Cause: iOS Safari's
          dynamic toolbar — .qa-overlay's `position:fixed; inset:0` can
          still size against the LARGER viewport (toolbar collapsed) even
          while the toolbar is actively showing, so a bottom-aligned sheet
          taller than the currently-visible area gets pushed with its top
          portion above what's actually on screen, unreachable since the
          fixed container itself doesn't scroll with the page. Almost
          certainly a latent bug that predates the long-press feature —
          reaching Quick Actions via the deck usually involves scrolling
          first, which collapses the toolbar and happened to avoid
          triggering it. .qa-overlay and .qa-sheet now size against
          100dvh/88dvh (falling back to vh first for anything without dvh
          support) instead, which tracks the actually-visible viewport.

  1.29.72 — 2026-09-16 — Follow-up to 1.29.70: long-pressing a cover now
          opens openQuickActions() (the mobile-native bottom sheet — same
          one the deck's own "Quick Actions" button and the mini player's
          mobile fallback already use), not openAlbumCardContextMenu() (the
          desktop-style floating dropdown positioned at the cursor) — a
          small anchored menu isn't the right shape for a touch hold the
          way it is for a mouse right-click.

  1.29.70 — 2026-09-16 — Feature: holding down on an album card (Albums or
          Covers grid) now opens the same quick menu a right-click does —
          touch has no native contextmenu equivalent, so this never worked
          on mobile before. New attachLongPress() helper (touchstart/move/
          end, 500ms hold, 10px move tolerance before it's treated as a
          scroll instead) is written generically, not album-card-specific,
          so the same "give this element a right-click equivalent on
          touch" behavior can be reused elsewhere later without re-deriving
          the timing logic. Also suppresses the click/dblclick the same
          touch would otherwise still fire afterward (so holding a card
          doesn't ALSO open its deck), and gives a brief opacity dip
          (.long-press-active) while the hold is registering, as feedback
          that something's about to happen.

          transport buttons on the compact mobile bar. Previously the ONLY

  1.29.67 — 2026-09-16 — Feature: New Release Radar. New ⚙ → "New
          Releases" panel checks Spotify for recent releases (last ~9
          months, NEW_RELEASE_WINDOW_DAYS) by every distinct artist already
          in the crate — a multi-artist row's credit is split the same way
          the rest of the app already treats it — and filters out anything
          that looks already owned (crateHasRelease, same normMatchKey
          normalization the mini player's "find crate item for this track"
          lookup already uses) or previously dismissed. Each result shows
          cover/artist/title/date with "+ Add" (opens the Add form
          prefilled, same shape as the existing quick-add-from-track flow)
          and "✕ Dismiss" (permanent, keyed by Spotify album id).

          A silent background check runs once after the catalog loads
          (maybeAutoScanNewReleases, called alongside the existing
          maybeRunBackgroundLastfmSync in init()), throttled to roughly
          once a day (NEW_RELEASE_AUTO_INTERVAL_MS) — it only ever updates
          the menu's badge count, never pops the panel open on its own.
          "Check now" in the panel bypasses that throttle. Needs Spotify
          Client ID/Secret already configured (⚙ → Spotify API…) — no
          separate setup.

          Cost-control notes for a big crate: an artist's resolved Spotify
          ID (or confirmed absence of one) is cached indefinitely in
          localStorage (crate_new_releases_artist_cache_v1) and only
          re-attempted after 30 days if it previously failed, and artist
          checks run with limited concurrency (NEW_RELEASE_CONCURRENCY = 4)
          rather than fully sequential (slow) or fully parallel (hammers
          Spotify's API) — a scan is still one request per artist, so it
          can take a while the first time through a large crate.

  1.29.66 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: opening Edit/Add for an album whose
          ReleaseDate (or Recorded) cell had been auto-converted to a real
          Google Sheets Date (rather than the plain "YYYY"/"YYYY-MM"/
          "YYYY-MM-DD" text _ensureColumn's asText fix is meant to prevent)
          showed completely blank Year/Month/Day fields — even though the
          sheet cell visibly had a date in it. Cause: _readSheet serializes
          a real Date cell back as a full ISO timestamp
          ("2026-04-24T00:00:00.000Z"), and splitReleaseDate/
          splitRecordedDate's regex was anchored with a trailing $, so it
          required an EXACT match against our own plain date format and
          simply failed (returning three blank strings) on anything with a
          time-of-day suffix. The date *display* elsewhere (album cards,
          sorting) already tolerated this fine via a different, more
          forgiving function (formatReleaseDateStr, which just does
          `new Date(str)` and lets JS parse the ISO string natively) — this
          was the one narrow spot that didn't. Removed the trailing $ so
          both now match just the leading YYYY-MM-DD and ignore whatever
          follows, rather than refusing to match at all.

          This is a read-back fix, not a write-side one — if your sheet's
          ReleaseDate/Recorded columns are still Date-formatted rather than
          Plain Text (check: is a cell's text left- or right-aligned?),
          run fixReleaseDateColumnFormats() once from the Apps Script
          editor to stop future entries from getting mangled the same way.

  1.29.65 — 2026-09-16 — Follow-up to 1.29.64: that fix opened the add form
          directly, but the actual desktop equivalent (clicking the album

  1.29.64 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: on mobile, tapping the mini player for a
          track NOT yet in the crate did nothing at all — no way to add it.
          Cause: the "Add to your crate" option only ever existed as
          openAlbumContextMenu, wired to the album name's contextmenu

          mobile whole-bar tap fallback (1.29.something, "reuses the exact
          same Quick Actions sheet") only handled the case where
          nowPlayingItem already pointed at a real crate item
          (if (nowPlayingItem) openQuickActions(...)) and silently did

  1.29.63 — 2026-09-16 — Tweak: the tracklist builder's match panel now
          always shows a "None of these right? Search {platform} yourself"
          link — results found or not. Root issue this addresses: an
          unusual/short/generic artist name (e.g. "a.s.o.", which reads as
          the common "and so on" abbreviation to a search engine) can make
          YouTube's or Spotify's own relevance ranking return confidently
          WRONG results rather than none at all — "no matches found"
          doesn't cover that case, and there was previously no way back to
          manual search without closing the panel and hunting for it.

  1.29.62 — 2026-09-16 — Tweaks to the tracklist builder's per-row match
          panel: (1) capped its height (max-height: 240px, scrolling) —
          previously unbounded, so a full 10-result list just pushed every
          row below it down the page instead of staying contained, unlike
          the already-scrolling Track Properties picker. (2) Picking a
          result ("Use this") now closes/collapses the panel immediately
          instead of leaving the list open with a "Selected" marker —
          renderMatchCandidateList gained an optional onAfterUse callback
          for this (Track Properties omits it and keeps the old
          re-render-in-place behavior, since it has no panel to close).

  1.29.61 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: Spotify's per-track and per-album search
          (spotifyTrackCandidatesLive, spotifySearchDirect,
          spotifyFindAlbumTracklist) all combined field filters —
          "track:X artist:Y" / "album:X artist:Y" — which is stricter than
          a plain search and can come back with ZERO results even for a
          real match on an unusual/short artist name (seen with "a.s.o." —
          "No Spotify matches found" for a track that's genuinely on
          Spotify). All three now fall back to a plain free-text query
          (new spotifyTrackSearchRaw()/spotifyAlbumSearchRaw() helpers)
          when the fielded one comes up empty, before giving up. The
          1.29.58 artist-match check still runs on whatever hit either
          query produces, so this doesn't reopen the wrong-artist bug that
          fix addressed — if anything it matters more here, since the
          free-text fallback is less precise on its own.

  1.29.60 — 2026-09-16 — Feature: YouTube matching now works the same way
          Spotify's does — real candidate videos to preview and pick from,
          not just "open a search tab and paste the link back". Needs a
          free YouTube Data API v3 key (new ⚙ → YouTube API… panel,
          same pattern as the existing Discogs token setup) since actually
          scraping YouTube's search results client-side isn't something
          this app does (no CORS access from a page like this anyway, and
          it'd violate YouTube's terms) — the official API is the real
          equivalent. New youtubeSearchCandidates() (Data API v3 search,
          maxResults 10). Deliberately NEVER auto-fires the way Spotify's
          matching sometimes does — the free tier's quota (100 searches/
          day) would drain almost immediately otherwise — every YouTube
          search across the tracklist builder's per-row "Match" button and
          "Edit track properties…"'s new "Find a YouTube match" section
          requires an explicit click. Falls back to the original "open a
          YouTube search tab" behavior when no key is configured.

          Refactored the Spotify and YouTube pickers onto one shared
          renderer (new toMatchCandidates()/renderMatchCandidateList(),
          replacing the tracklist builder's old renderTracklistMatchList()
          and Track Properties' old rerenderMatchList()) rather than
          duplicating near-identical rendering per platform — YouTube
          results additionally show a small thumbnail.

  1.29.59 — 2026-09-16 — Tweak: the per-row track-match button (1.29.56)
          was a bare 22px circle with just a "▸" glyph and no visible
          label — reported as genuinely hard to spot next to the link
          fields. Now a labeled "Match" pill button instead, same position
          and behavior (tooltip/aria-label unchanged), with the tracklist
          builder's link-group column widened slightly (140px → 170px
          min-width) so the wider button doesn't crowd the link input.

  1.29.58 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: adding a release could offer ("Found a
          matching tracklist on Spotify") and fill in a tracklist for a
          COMPLETELY UNRELATED ALBUM — seen in the wild: a downtempo/dream
          pop release on Low Lying Records got offered a KC and the
          Sunshine Band disco tracklist. Cause: spotifyFindAlbumTracklist
          (and its sibling spotifySearchDirect, used for the plain Spotify
          link shown on an album) both took Spotify's #1 search hit with
          zero validation — "album:X artist:Y" is a fuzzy relevance hint to
          Spotify's search, not a strict filter, so a short/generic title
          can out-rank the real match with something from an entirely
          different artist. New spotifyResultArtistMatches() checks the
          hit's actual artist credit against what was searched for
          (normalized via the existing trackNormKey()) and both functions
          now return null — "no match", same as finding nothing at all —
          rather than confidently offering a wrong result when it fails.
          Concretely, this also means the new MusicBrainz tracklist offer
          (1.29.54) actually gets its chance to run in cases like this one:
          it was never reached before because Spotify's (wrong) match was
          "found" first.

  1.29.57 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: queue auto-advance (1.29.49) still wasn't
          firing reliably even after unifying the check across both
          callers. Real cause was in the check itself, not which caller ran
          it: maybeAdvanceCrateQueueOnEnd compared duration against the
          RAW STORED spotifyLastState.position — but that value only
          updates when a player_state_changed event fires (not
          continuously) or every ~6s via the refreshNowPlayingFromWebApi
          poll, so by the time a track actually ended, the stored position
          could be several seconds stale — nowhere near the 1500ms window
          the check required, independent of the earlier race. Now compares
          against the EXTRAPOLATED position (stored position + real time
          elapsed since spotifyLastStateAt — the same math
          getEstimatedPosition() already does), which reflects where
          playback actually is right now regardless of when the last event
          landed. Bumped CRATE_QUEUE_END_THRESHOLD_MS 1500 → 2500ms for
          event/poll delivery latency now that the comparison itself is
          accurate.

  1.29.56 — 2026-09-16 — Feature: the tracklist builder (Add album, Edit
          album, and "⤓ Load current tracklist") now has a small ▸
          button next to each row's YouTube and Spotify link fields.
          YouTube has no search API wired up, so its button just opens a
          real YouTube search in a new tab (artist + this row's title) to
          eyeball and paste back. Spotify's opens the same inline candidate
          picker "Edit track properties…" got in 1.29.54 — preview with ▶,
          "Use this" to fill the row's Spotify field — scoped to that one
          row via a new .tracklist-builder-match-panel that only appears
          under the row whose button was clicked (only one open at a time).
          Reuses spotifyTrackCandidatesLive() and the .track-props-match-*
          rendering from the Track Properties picker rather than
          duplicating it. wireTracklistBuilderContainer() now takes a
          getArtist callback (read fresh at click time, since the artist
          field can still change after the form's shown) — the two existing
          call sites (Add/Edit forms) were updated, so anything calling it
          directly elsewhere would need the same. Also hit and fixed the
          same [hidden]-vs-unconditional-display:flex cascade bug already
          seen twice before (#deckPlayBtn, .spotify-match-banner) for the
          new match panel.

  1.29.55 — 2026-09-16 — Feature: "Edit track properties…" now has "Track
          name" and "Track length" fields, alongside genre/YouTube/Spotify.
          Both are per-track overrides stored in trackData (same object
          genre/links already live in), applied via mergeTrackLinkOverrides
          so every view (deck, flat Tracks table, "Album and Tracks",
          playlists) shows the corrected name/length everywhere at once,
          same as a saved link already did. Track length accepts "m:ss",
          "h:mm:ss", or a bare number of seconds (new parseLengthToMs(),
          inverse of the existing formatMs()) and prefills from whatever's
          already showing (currentTracklistTracks/state.trackCache) so the
          field starts populated instead of blank. Name only gets written
          as an override when it's actually different from the track's real
          title, so opening and closing the modal unchanged never creates
          one. Caveat noted in the field's own hint text: with no Spotify/
          YouTube link pinned, a renamed track's automatic Spotify search
          now searches using the NEW title, not the original — usually an
          improvement (the rename is often fixing a wrong/malformed source
          title) but worth knowing.

          Also fixed a latent bug this surfaced: the "is this track's
          trackData now completely empty, delete it" cleanup check
          (4 near-identical copies, in updateTrackData/clearTrackSelection/
          etc.) never knew about genre/youtubeLink/spotifyLink's newer
          siblings-to-be — adding name/lengthMs without updating all four
          would have meant a track with ONLY a name or length override got
          silently deleted the moment any of those other code paths ran.

  1.29.54 — 2026-09-16 — Two additions, both about trusting/fixing what the
          app matched a release to:

          (1) "Edit track properties…" now has a real Spotify-match
          troubleshooting tool, not just a raw link field to paste into.
          Opening it for a non-manual-album track auto-loads its top 10
          Spotify search candidates (spotifyTrackCandidates, cached) as a
          scrollable list — each row can be previewed with ▶
          (showMiniPlayer, doesn't touch the crate queue) and picked with
          "Use this", which fills the Spotify link field above; still needs
          the modal's own Save to actually persist, same as every other
          field here. A search box (prefilled with the track title) lets
          you re-search with different wording via the new
          spotifyTrackCandidatesLive() when none of the automatic results
          are right. Hidden entirely for manual albums (isManualAlbum),
          which never use the automatic search. The context-menu "Confirm/
          Try next Spotify match" shortcuts (1.29.53) are unchanged and
          still work as a faster one-click path.

          (2) Adding a release now offers to populate the tracklist builder
          from MusicBrainz, not just Spotify — new addMusicBrainzMatchBanner,
          mirroring the existing addSpotifyMatchBanner (1.29.45). Unlike the
          Spotify offer (a fresh fuzzy search), this fetches the ACTUAL
          tracklist of whichever specific pressing was just picked in the
          release picker (prefill.link, a musicbrainz.org/release/<mbid>
          URL — see renderReleasePickerResults) via the new
          mbFetchTracklistForRelease(), so it's exact rather than a guess.
          Only offered once the Spotify lookup has finished and found
          nothing (showAddForm now chains maybeOfferAddSpotifyTracklist()
          .then(() => maybeOfferAddMusicBrainzTracklist())), so a release
          that's on both sources never shows two competing banners.

  1.29.53 — 2026-09-16 — Feature: right-clicking a track (deck tracklist,
          flat Tracks table, or "Album and Tracks" view — all three already
          funnel into openTrackContextMenu) now offers "✓ Confirm Spotify
          match" and "↻ Try next Spotify match", for when the automatic
          fuzzy search (spotifyTrackId) lands on the wrong recording — a
          cover, a live/remix take, or a same-titled different song.
          spotifyTrackId now caches the FULL list of search candidates
          (spotifyTrackCandidates, limit=10, up from the old limit=1) rather
          than just the top hit, and resolves through a per-track in-memory
          index (spotifyMatchAttemptIndex) so "Try next match" can step
          through real alternatives and immediately replay the track to
          preview each one. "Confirm match" pins whichever recording is
          currently resolved into trackData.spotifyLink — the exact same
          field/precedence "Edit track properties…" already writes to — so
          it survives a reload and always wins over the fuzzy search from
          then on, the same way a manually-pasted link always has. Hidden
          for multi-selections and manual albums (isManualAlbum tracks skip
          the automatic search entirely and have nothing here to confirm).

  1.29.52 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix (supersedes 1.29.51 below, which was correct
          in spirit but didn't actually fix anything): the REAL reason
          Ctrl/Cmd+A never worked, in ANY context — main Tracks view, album
          grid, playlist Tracks view, deck — is that el.deckOverlay is
          shown/hidden via a CSS class (.open), never the `hidden`
          attribute, unlike every other overlay this handler checks.
          el.deckOverlay.hidden was therefore always false, so `modalOpen`
          was permanently stuck at true and every branch below it (deck,
          playlist, main Tracks/Albums view, Diary) has been dead code since
          this shortcut was first written — completely independent of the
          1.29.50/1.29.51 fixes above, which were real gaps but never even
          reachable to test. Now checks el.deckOverlay.classList.contains
          ('open') instead.

  1.29.51 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: Ctrl/Cmd+A while viewing an album's deck
          (release view) did nothing but trigger the browser's own native
          "select all text on the page" — the global select-all keydown
          handler explicitly skips its work whenever the deck is open
          (it uses a different selection model there, per-track "listened"
          flags rather than state.selectedTracks), but had no replacement
          behavior for that case, so it just fell through with no
          preventDefault. The deck already has its own "select all tracks"
          affordance (#tlSelectAllBtn / selectAllTracks()) — Ctrl/Cmd+A now
          calls that directly when the deck is open, ahead of the existing
          modalOpen skip.

  1.29.50 — 2026-09-16 — Feature: Ctrl/Cmd+A now selects all tracks while
          viewing a playlist's own "Tracks" sub-view. The global select-all
          keydown handler only ever checked state.mainViewMode (the main
          library's view), and explicitly skipped everything while a
          playlist was open — but renderPlaylistTracksView reuses the exact
          same flat-table rows/data-track-key/state.selectedTracks setup as
          the library's Tracks view, so selectAllVisibleTracks() already
          worked for it; it just needed a branch that checks
          state.playlistViewMode === 'tracks' instead of bailing out
          whenever state.activePlaylistId is set.

  1.29.49 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: queuing multiple tracks (or "Play playlist")
          would sometimes just stop dead when the first track finished,
          instead of advancing — reproducible-but-not-always, which pointed
          to a race rather than a straightforward logic error. Cause: TWO
          places notice "Spotify just went from playing to paused" —
          onSpotifyPlayerStateChanged (the real-time Web Playback SDK
          listener) and refreshNowPlayingFromWebApi (the 6s poll that also
          catches playback happening on another Connect device, e.g. a TV).
          Only the SDK listener had the "was this the crate queue's current
          track ending naturally?" check that calls playCrateQueueIndex for
          the next entry — the poll just re-rendered the paused state
          directly. Whichever of the two happened to notice the transition
          first won: if the poll's own interval landed on or narrowly beat
          the SDK event, the render happened with no advance at all, and
          nothing else was ever going to call playCrateQueueIndex for it.
          Pulled the check out into a new shared maybeAdvanceCrateQueueOnEnd(),
          called from both places before either one renders the paused
          state — whichever notices first now correctly advances the queue.

  1.29.48 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: a playlist containing an individual track
          whose title is purely numeric (e.g. "21", "1989") crashed the
          playlist's "Tracks" view with "(name || "").toLowerCase is not a
          function". Cause: Google Sheets auto-detects a numeric-looking
          string on write and stores it as a real number, same failure
          family as the ReleaseDate/Recorded issue above — PlaylistItems'
          TrackName column had no equivalent Plain Text protection, and the
          frontend read it with `it.TrackName || ''` (no String() coercion,
          unlike every other field parsed alongside it) so a numeric value
          passed straight through into trackNormKey(), which unconditionally
          calls .toLowerCase(). Fixed on both ends: trackNormKey() now
          coerces with String(name || '') so it can never choke on a
          non-string regardless of where it came from (protects existing
          rows already stored as numbers, no re-adding needed); the
          playlist-item parser in fetchCatalog() now wraps TrackName in
          String(...).trim() like its siblings; and apps-script-backend.gs's
          addPlaylistItems now forces the TrackName column to Plain Text on
          write, so newly-added numeric-titled tracks stop being converted
          in the first place.

  1.29.47 — 2026-09-16 — Bugfix: a brand-new, genuinely empty catalog (e.g. a
          friend's first sign-in under the new multi-tenant backend, before
          they've added anything) showed "No sleeves match your search" —
          the same message used when a search/filter matches nothing. That's
          actively misleading when nothing has ever been added. renderGrid()
          now distinguishes state.raw.length === 0 (nothing in the catalog
          at all — new 'emptyCatalog' message, onboarding-flavored) from
          state.filtered.length === 0 with a non-empty state.raw (an actual
          search/filter came up empty — keeps the original message).

  1.29.46 — 2026-09-15 — Bugfix: the Spotify tracklist-match banner
          (addSpotifyMatchBanner, 1.29.45) showed up blank on every Add-
          album form, immediately and regardless of whether a match was
          ever found — never actually hidden. Cause: .spotify-match-banner's
          unconditional `display: flex` is a plain class selector, same
          specificity as the browser's built-in `[hidden] { display: none }`
          rule, so the author rule won the cascade and the `hidden`
          attribute set in the markup (and by hideAddSpotifyMatchBanner())
          did nothing. Same failure mode already hit and fixed once before
          for #deckPlayBtn (see that rule's comment) — .spotify-match-banner
          just never got the matching [hidden] override. Added
          .spotify-match-banner[hidden] { display: none; }.

  1.29.45 — 2026-09-15 — New feature: when adding an album, if a matching
          release is found on Spotify, a banner now offers to pull its
          tracklist straight into the release ("Found a matching tracklist
          on Spotify (N tracks) — add it to this release?"). Accepting fills
          the tracklist builder from it (title + a per-track Spotify link),
          which then gets saved as this release's ManualTracks same as if
          typed in by hand — the point being those tracks are then
          immediately searchable (see combinedSearchText/trackTitlesFor,
          1.29.44), rather than only ever coming from the lazy MusicBrainz
          auto-lookup (loadTracklist) that's never written anywhere
          searchable. New spotifyFindAlbumTracklist() (artist/title search →
          best album match → paginated /albums/{id}/tracks) and
          maybeOfferAddSpotifyTracklist(), both called from the one place
          every "pick a release"/"enter manually" path already funnels
          through (showAddForm) — so this fires the same way regardless of
          whether the release was found via free-text search, an artist's
          discography, or a track's "+ Add album" shortcut. Skipped
          entirely if the form already has a tracklist prefilled, if
          artist/title aren't both known yet, or if you've already started
          typing your own tracklist by the time the lookup resolves — never
          overwrites anything you're already doing. addSpotifyMatchSeq
          guards against a slow lookup from a release you've already
          navigated away from (picked a different result, or closed the
          modal) popping the banner back up after the fact.

  1.29.44 — 2026-09-15 — Bugfix: searching by track/song name returned no
          results, while searching by album name worked fine. Root cause:
          combinedSearchText() (the plain-word search string, also the
          fallback for an unrecognized "field:" prefix) only ever
          concatenated artist/title(album)/year/catalog/genres — track
          titles were never part of it, so a bare word matching only a song
          name had nothing to match against. Added a trackTitlesFor(r)
          helper (reads r.manualTracks, already loaded upfront for every
          row — no extra fetch) and folded its output into
          combinedSearchText(), plus a new track:/song: field (
          SEARCH_FIELD_ALIASES + searchTokenMatches) for an exact field-
          scoped search. Caveat: this only covers manually-entered
          tracklists — an auto-looked-up MusicBrainz tracklist is fetched
          lazily when its album's deck is opened and isn't available here
          to search until then.

  1.29.43 — 2026-09-13 — Two requested changes, mobile mini player and
          desktop tracklist selection:

          (1) Mobile mini player is now a single compact row instead of
          wrapping onto three (track info, then transport/options, then
          the seek/progress row) — the wrap behavior in the existing
          `@media (max-width: 620px)` rules for .mini-player-row/.mini-
          player-track is what made it so tall. .mini-player-progress-row
          and .mini-player-options (volume, queue, device, detach,
          fullscreen) are now hidden under that same breakpoint, and the
          row stays nowrap with the track info truncating instead of
          claiming a full line, leaving just art + title/artist + the
          transport (prev/play/next) on one short line. Tapping anywhere
          on the mini player other than an actual control (transport
          buttons, seek bar, volume, close, drag handle) now opens the
          Quick Actions sheet for whatever's currently playing
          (nowPlayingItem) — see the new el.miniPlayer click listener and
          isMiniPlayerMobileLayout(). The four click handlers that used to
          fire on tapping the art/title/artist/album directly
          (goToTrackPage etc.) now bail out early under this same
          breakpoint so the tap reaches that new listener instead of also
          navigating; unaffected on desktop. The reduced height meant the
          existing hardcoded 96px mini-player-clearance figure (#app/.deck
          /.qa-overlay padding, see 1.29.38/1.29.41) was leaving a big
          empty gap under the compact bar, so a `max-width: 620px`
          override lowers it to 64px there — placed after the existing
          900px-scoped rules (same source-order reasoning already noted
          next to those) so it actually wins at this narrower width.

          (2) The per-track "mark as listened" checkmark toggle
          (.track-listen-toggle) in the deck's own tracklist is now
          hidden on desktop (`min-width: 621px`) — selecting tracks there
          now goes exclusively through the click-to-select/right-click
          "Add to entry" flow already added in 1.18.0, rather than two
          overlapping ways to do the same thing. Still shown on mobile,
          where that's the easier target. "Add to entry" (addTracksToEntry)
          previously only flagged the track(s) and toasted — it now also
          opens the actual log-a-listen form afterward (opening the deck
          first via openDeck() if it wasn't already showing that album,
          e.g. triggered from the flat Tracks table), same form "+ Log a
          listen" opens, since flagging tracks with no visible way to
          finish logging them was a dead end once the checkmark UI (the
          other path into that same form) is gone on desktop. Left alone
          for a multi-album selection — nothing single form to open there
          — and doesn't reopen/re-toggle a form that's already open for
          that exact album (that case already just refreshes the summary,
          unchanged from before).
