  1.29.41 — 2026-09-13 — Bugfix (screenshot): opening the release/album
          detail view (.deck) on mobile with a track playing showed the
          mini player painted over the bottom of the deck's content —
          tracklist rows visibly cut off/overlapped behind it, tab-bar
          labels showing through underneath. Root cause: .deck caps at
          max-height:88vh with zero awareness that the fixed bottom tab
          bar (1.29.38) and the mini player now permanently occupy real
          space at the bottom of that same viewport, so a tall enough deck
          could extend down into that reserved strip. The mini player's
          z-index (300) is deliberately above every modal's — needed so
          things like the +Playlist flyout menu (z-index 320, opened from
          inside the deck) stay usable rather than getting hidden behind
          it — so raising the deck's z-index instead would have broken
          that. Fix instead shrinks .deck's max-height by
          --mobile-navbar-h (+ 96px extra when body.has-mini-player) so
          the card simply never reaches that occupied space in the first
          place — no z-index touched. Same overlap risk existed for the
          Quick Actions sheet (#qaOverlay, bottom-anchored rather than
          centered) — fixed there via extra padding-bottom on the overlay,
          which its flex align-items:flex-end respects, lifting the sheet
          up clear of the same strip. Scoped to .deck specifically —
          .arrange-modal (Edit/Add album, etc.) already goes full-screen
          at a narrower breakpoint via its own separate
          .arrange-modal:not(.map-modal) rule and wasn't touched, to avoid
          fighting that existing mechanism.

  1.29.40

  1.29.40 — 2026-09-13 — Bugfix: "artist: sylvie" (a space after the
          colon — natural to type, easy to do without thinking) returned
          no matches in both the crate's own search and the Add Album
          MusicBrainz search, while "artist:sylvie" (no space) worked
          fine. Root cause was in the shared tokenizer, tokenizeSearchQuery():
          after consuming "artist:" it started reading the value from
          whatever character came right after the colon — which, with a
          space typed there, was the space itself, and the value-reading
          loop stops immediately at whitespace. That produced a field
          token with an empty value (`artist:`), an invalid/unmatchable
          clause on both the MusicBrainz-Lucene side and the crate's own
          substring-match side, while "sylvie" fell out as a separate
          bare word — the empty artist clause then made the whole ANDed
          query match nothing. Fixed by skipping any spaces/tabs right
          after the colon before reading the value, so "artist: sylvie"
          and "artist:sylvie" now tokenize identically.

  1.29.39

  1.29.39 — 2026-09-13 — Moved "advanced search" to where it was actually
          asked for: the "Add Album" MusicBrainz search (#addSearchInput/
          doAddSearch), not the crate's own internal filter box (which
          1.29.36 added it to instead, on a misread — left in place since
          it's harmless/still useful, but this is the one that was
          actually requested). doAddSearch's ad-hoc "pull a bare year out
          of the query" logic (1.29.34) is replaced by a general field-
          aware query builder, buildMbSearchQuery(), reusing the exact
          same tokenizeSearchQuery() parser the internal search uses (same
          field:value / -word / "quoted phrase" syntax) but translated
          into real MusicBrainz Lucene field names via
          MB_ADD_SEARCH_FIELD_ALIASES: artist:, title:/album:, year:/date:
          (a bare year or a "2020-2022" range becomes date:2022 or a real
          Lucene date:[...TO...] range), catalog:/catno:, label:, format:,
          country:, type:, barcode:, status:. A bare 4-digit year with no
          prefix at all still auto-converts to date:YYYY same as before
          (that behavior wasn't wrong, just needed generalizing rather
          than replacing). Unrecognized "field:" prefixes and everything
          else fold back into a literal free-text clause rather than
          getting silently dropped. The catno OR-fallback still searches
          the complete, untouched original query string, same reasoning as
          1.29.34 — a catalog number can itself contain a 4-digit run or
          look like a field prefix. Added a one-line syntax hint under the
          search box in the Add Album modal for discoverability.

  1.29.38

  1.29.38 — 2026-09-13 — Two more mobile layout changes:

          (1) The MUSIC/DIARY/SCROBBLES/STATS/ARTIST tab bar (.nav-tabs) is
          now a fixed bottom bar instead of sitting under the header —
          position:fixed takes it out of .topbar's flex flow entirely
          (so the topbar just has one less row now), docked to the
          viewport bottom like a native app's tab bar. Its height lives in
          one place (--mobile-navbar-h: 54px) that #app's bottom padding
          and the mini player's position both reference, so neither the
          grid's last row nor the mini player end up hidden underneath it.
          The mini player now docks just above the tab bar (bottom:
          var(--mobile-navbar-h) instead of 0) rather than fighting it for
          the same spot. Getting #app's extra padding right when the mini
          player is active took two attempts — an unconditional (not
          media-scoped) `body.has-mini-player #app` rule already existed
          further down the stylesheet for the safe-area-inset case, and at
          equal specificity it would've won over a mobile-scoped rule
          placed earlier in the file purely by coming later in source
          order, regardless of which one is "more specific" to mobile. Fix
          was to add the mobile override right after that existing rule
          instead of back where the rest of the tab-bar CSS lives.

          (2) #sortSelect (the always-visible "Newest release" dropdown)
          is now hidden on mobile, replaced by a "⇅" icon
          (#sortFilterBtn) that opens a popover (#sortFilterMenu) listing
          all sort options plus a "Listened only" checkbox. Deliberately
          drives the exact same underlying controls rather than
          duplicating their logic: picking a sort option sets
          el.sortSelect.value and dispatches its own 'change' event (so
          the one real change handler still runs), and the checkbox just
          calls el.listenedFilterBtn.click(). renderSortFilterMenu() syncs
          the popover's checkmark/checkbox to current state whenever it
          opens. Same open/close pattern as the existing #layoutMenu
          (toggle on click, dismiss on outside click or Escape).

  1.29.37

  1.29.37 — 2026-09-13 — Mobile search collapsed down to a single 40x40
          icon button (matching every other .icon-btn's size/background/
          border in that row) instead of sitting there as a fixed-width
          text box next to sort/queue/gear. The old decorative <span
          class="search-icon"> is now a real <button id="searchIconBtn">
          (still inert on desktop — pointer-events:none in the base rule,
          untouched) that focuses #searchInput on tap; :focus-within on
          #searchWrap does the actual expand/collapse via CSS, no manual
          open/close state needed for that part. It also stays expanded
          whenever there's a live query — .mobile-search-has-value, toggled
          in the existing input handler and in applyAdvSearch() — so
          walking away from a filtered grid doesn't leave a bare icon with
          no clue why results are narrowed. Expanding claims the whole
          utility row (flex-basis:100%) rather than squeezing sideways;
          since .controls already wraps (flex-wrap:wrap, unchanged), sort/
          queue/gear just flow onto their own line underneath while it's
          open and rejoin the icon on one line once it collapses back —
          same underlying pattern the nav-tabs/brand rows already use, just
          applied one level down. Scoped entirely to the sub-900px media
          query; desktop's search bar is untouched.

  1.29.36

  1.29.36 — 2026-09-13 — New feature: advanced search. The search box's
          matching logic (previously just: lowercase, split on whitespace,
          require every word as a substring somewhere in artist/title/
          year/catalog/genres) was replaced with a small query parser:
            field:value          e.g. artist:sylvie, label:"warp records"
            year:2022 / year:>=2020 / year:<2020 / year:2020-2022
            rating:>=4
            -term / -field:value   (excludes instead of requiring)
            "exact phrase"
            plain words             (unchanged old behavior)
          All tokens still AND together, same as before — just each token
          can now be a field-scoped or negated or ranged clause instead of
          only ever a plain substring. Implementation: tokenizeSearchQuery()
          (manual char-by-char tokenizer — needed to handle an optional
          leading "-" and an optional "field:" prefix combining with either
          a bare or quoted value), searchTokenMatches() (field dispatch +
          parseNumericClause()/numericClauseMatches() for the year/rating
          operators/ranges), all sitting behind the exact same
          getSearchTerms()/matchesSearchTerms() call sites as before
          (searchFilteredRaw + applyFiltersSort), so the left panel and
          main grid both pick this up automatically with no separate
          wiring. An unrecognized "field:" prefix (typo, or just not a
          real field) falls back to literal substring matching on the
          whole token rather than silently matching everything.

          Also added a small "▾" button next to the search box
          (#advancedSearchBtn) opening an Advanced Search modal
          (#advSearchOverlay) with real form fields — Artist, Title, Year
          from/to, Minimum rating, Genre, Label, Type, Catalog, and a
          free-text "Additional keywords" field for anything else — for
          anyone who'd rather fill in boxes than memorize the syntax.
          Submitting just assembles the same query string into
          #searchInput (applyAdvSearch/advQuoteIfNeeded) and runs the
          normal search — there's no separate filtering path to keep in
          sync with the syntax above.

  1.29.35

  1.29.35 — 2026-09-13 — Removed the S/C/R/Y (Spotify/SoundCloud/RYM/
          YouTube) icon row that appeared over album covers on hover.
          Rather than deleting the feature/markup outright (buildCard's
          .cover-links, its hover-resolution logic, etc. — all still
          intact and harmless if re-enabled), flipped the existing
          "Show Spotify / SoundCloud / RYM links on cover art" toggle
          (#toggleCoverLinks/state.showCoverLinks) to default off, and
          stopped restoring that one field from saved prefs — it was
          defaulting to true before, so anyone with an old prefs blob
          would've kept seeing the icons despite the new default otherwise.
          The checkbox is still in the layout menu if this needs to come
          back for anyone.

  1.29.34

  1.29.34 — 2026-09-13 — The "Add Album" MusicBrainz search (doAddSearch,
          #addSearchInput) now recognizes a bare 4-digit year in the query
          and turns it into an explicit `date:YYYY` clause instead of
          leaving it as plain text. Previously "sylvie 2022" went to MB as
          one unprefixed free-text clause OR'd with a catno match — MB's
          default search field for /release is the release title, which
          essentially never literally contains a year, so the "2022" part
          was mostly just dead weight rather than actually narrowing
          anything to that year. Now the year is stripped out of the free-
          text portion and ANDed in as `date:2022` (or used alone if the
          whole query was just a year, e.g. searching "2022" by itself).
          The catno OR-clause deliberately still searches the ORIGINAL,
          un-stripped query — a catalog number can itself contain a 4-digit
          run (e.g. "CRT-2022") that this extraction would otherwise strip
          out from under a legitimate catalog-number search.

  1.29.33

  1.29.33 — 2026-09-13 — Reworked where Quick Actions opens from, per
          feedback: removed the per-card "⋮" button (.card-quick-btn) and
          its CSS entirely — back to the grid having only the S/C/R/Y
          cover-links. In its place, the deck (release) view's own
          Listen/Spotify/SoundCloud/RYM/+Playlist/Edit details button row
          is now visually replaced by a single "⋯ Quick actions" button
          (#deckQuickActionsBtn) that opens the same #qaOverlay sheet.
          The old row is still in the DOM (wrapped in a new
          .deck-actions-legacy div, display:none) rather than deleted —
          those elements' hrefs/dataset/click handlers are threaded through
          a lot of other code (getExternalLinksFor, applyResolvedLinksToDeck,
          the Spotify Connect play flow), so hiding instead of removing
          keeps all of that working unchanged with zero risk of breakage.
          Quick Actions gained two rows to make it a real 1:1 replacement:
          "Listen ↗" (item's manual Link field, hidden when absent, same
          as the old #deckLink's hidden/visible logic) and "Open on
          SoundCloud ↗" (new openAlbumOnSoundcloud(), mirroring
          openAlbumOnSpotify/openAlbumOnRym's resolve-then-open pattern).
          Also fixed an activeItem/body-scroll bug this reshuffle would've
          otherwise introduced: closeQuickActions() used to unconditionally
          null activeItem and reset body overflow, which was fine when the
          sheet only ever opened from a card (nothing else was using
          activeItem), but now it always opens from an already-open deck —
          nulling activeItem under it would break the deck's own rating/
          edit/playlist buttons until reopened. It now checks whether
          el.deckOverlay still has .open and leaves both alone if so.

  1.29.31 — 2026-09-13 — Smaller album covers under the sub-900px
          breakpoint so more fit per row (.size-control, the only user-
          facing way to change this, is display:none on mobile with no
          replacement control). .grid's grid-template-columns normally
          reads minmax(var(--card-min, 190px), 1fr), and state.cardSize
          gets pushed into --card-min as an *inline* style on the grid
          container (see the two el.gridContainer.style.setProperty calls)
          — inline style wins over any plain CSS rule targeting the same
          element/property, so overriding --card-min itself in a media
          query would have been silently ignored. Set
          grid-template-columns directly instead (minmax(105px,1fr), no
          var()) so the mobile size is fixed and independent of whatever
          was last picked on desktop; also tightened the gap to 10px (5px
          for the existing "close gaps" toggle) to match.

  1.29.28 — 2026-09-13 — Mobile topbar decluttering. Under the sub-900px
          breakpoint, .topbar's four direct children (hamburger, brand,
          nav-tabs, controls) were all plain wrapping flex items with no
          row assignment, so on a narrow phone they broke across 3-4
          uneven rows and the five nav tabs themselves could wrap mid-row.
          Used flex `order` to pin brand to row 1, controls (search/sort/
          queue/gear — the icon buttons already relocate into the ⚙ menu
          via applyMobileOverflowMenu, untouched here) to row 2 right-
          aligned, and the nav tabs to row 3 as a single horizontally-
          scrollable strip (overflow-x auto, flex-wrap: nowrap, hidden
          scrollbar) so they never wrap. Also hid the "a listening log"
          tagline (redundant once space is tight), let the search input
          grow to fill its row instead of the old fixed 140px, and
          knocked the brand h1 down from 34px to 26px on the sub-620px
          breakpoint since 34px was competing with the tabs strip right
          below it. Pure CSS — no HTML structure or JS changed, so no TDZ
          risk and no behavior change to what any button/tab/input does.

  1.29.27 — 2026-09-13 — Third instance of the same temporal-dead-zone bug
          class as 1.29.24/1.29.25, found via an on-page diagnostic overlay
          on a phone where it was still failing after those two fixes:
          `let fetchCatalogInFlight = false;` was declared one line above
          fetchCatalog() itself, but fetchCatalog() is called synchronously
          during startup (init()'s `await fetchCatalog()` is reached with no
          preceding await in that function), and its very first real lines
          check/set that flag — so the declaration, further down the file
          than the earlier two fixes even needed to move past, was still in
          its own temporal dead zone at call time: "ReferenceError: Cannot
          access 'fetchCatalogInFlight' before initialization". This one
          isn't gated behind a mobile-only media query like the previous two
          — it likely would have surfaced on desktop too, just later, once
          those two stopped masking it by crashing first inside bindEvents()
          before execution ever reached fetchCatalog(). Moved the
          declaration up next to the other pre-init constants/state. Also
          ran a full pass cross-referencing every top-level let/const
          against every reference to it elsewhere in the file, and manually
          re-traced the entire synchronous startup path (bindEvents() and
          everything it calls directly, plus fetchCatalog()'s own body up to
          its first await) to confirm nothing else in that path is at risk
          the same way.

  1.29.26 — 2026-09-13 — Bugfix: the Add-album MusicBrainz search
          ("doAddSearch", the search box in the Add form) failing almost
          immediately for most real searches. Root cause: the query string
          `(${cleanQuery}) OR catno:"${cleanQuery}"` only stripped quotes
          and backslashes out of cleanQuery — but the `(${cleanQuery})`
          clause is an *unquoted* MusicBrainz/Lucene query fragment, and
          Lucene treats a long list of ordinary punctuation as reserved
          syntax: hyphens (Jay-Z), colons, parentheses ("(Deluxe
          Edition)"), ampersands, slashes (AC/DC), exclamation marks
          (Panic! At The Disco), etc. Most real artist/album names contain
          at least one of these, so most searches were sending MusicBrainz
          a malformed query, which came back as an HTTP 400 that the code
          never checked for — `await res.json()` on that error response
          threw a JSON-parse error straight into the catch block, which is
          what "fails straight away" actually was. Added escapeLuceneTerm()
          (next to mbFetch) to properly escape the bare clause, kept a
          separate quote/backslash-only escape for the already-quoted catno
          clause, and added an explicit `if (!res.ok) throw` in both
          doAddSearch() and openAddModalForTrackAlbum() so a real MusicBrainz
          error surfaces as the existing "Search failed" message instead of
          an opaque parse error.

  1.29.25 — 2026-09-13 — Follow-up to 1.29.24: that fix only moved
          MAP_MOBILE_BREAKPOINT_QUERY, but bindEvents() also calls
          initMobileDrawers() and initMobileOverflowMenu() synchronously
          (same call chain: startApp -> enterApp -> init -> bindEvents),
          which read MOBILE_DRAWER_BREAKPOINT_QUERY and
          MOBILE_OVERFLOW_ACTIONS — both declared later in the file, so
          both were still in their own temporal dead zone at that point.
          Confirmed via console: "ReferenceError: Cannot access
          'MOBILE_DRAWER_BREAKPOINT_QUERY' before initialization" at
          initMobileDrawers, same aborted-init/never-reaches-fetchCatalog
          symptom as before. Went through every function bindEvents() calls
          directly (applyMapWindowPrefs, initMapWindowDrag,
          observeMapWindowResize, initMapMobileBreakpointListener,
          wireMapColumnResize, initPlayerDrag, initMobileDrawers,
          initMobileOverflowMenu, initSeekDrag) plus bindEvents' and init()'s
          own bodies, checking every top-level (non-callback) reference to a
          global CONST against its declaration line — these two were the
          only other offenders. Moved both up next to
          MAP_MOBILE_BREAKPOINT_QUERY. Everything else called from that
          chain either already sat before its dependencies or only touches
          later globals from inside an event-handler callback (which
          doesn't run until well after the whole script has finished
          loading, so no TDZ risk there).

  1.29.24 — 2026-09-13 — Bugfix: catalogue loading "successfully" as
          completely empty again, this time with no error and no Retry
          button — different root cause than 1.29.23. bindEvents(), run
          synchronously during startApp()'s initial init(), calls
          applyMapWindowPrefs() -> isMapMobileLayout(), which reads
          MAP_MOBILE_BREAKPOINT_QUERY — but that constant was declared with
          `const` down in the Map section, thousands of lines later in the
          script. Since script execution hadn't reached that line yet,
          the constant was still in its temporal dead zone: "ReferenceError:
          Cannot access 'MAP_MOBILE_BREAKPOINT_QUERY' before initialization",
          thrown out of bindEvents() and up through init(), aborting it
          before it ever reached fetchCatalog(). Confirmed via console:
          the error trace ran isMapMobileLayout -> applyMapWindowPrefs ->
          bindEvents -> init -> enterApp -> startApp. Moved the constant's
          declaration up to the block of pre-init() constants near the top
          of the script (same fix already applied once before to
          SPOTIFY_PLAYBACK_SCOPES, per the comment there — this one was
          evidently added later without following the same rule). No
          functional change to Map behavior; isMapMobileLayout() and the
          CSS media query it mirrors are untouched.

  1.29.23 — 2026-09-10 — Bugfix: catalog loading "successfully" as
          completely empty (All Genres · 0, "No sleeves match your
          search") with no error shown anywhere. Cause: fetchCatalog()
          only recognized one specific backend error shape
          ({error:'unauthorized'}, via isAuthError()) — any OTHER error
          the Apps Script backend could return (a script exception, a
          malformed response, anything) fell straight through unnoticed,
          and (data.rows || []) a few lines down just quietly defaulted to
          an empty array. The result looked like a fully-loaded, genuinely
          empty crate instead of a failure — worse than an obvious error,
          since there was nothing to even suggest something had gone
          wrong. fetchCatalog() now checks for data.error generically
          (after the specific auth-error check) and shows it as a real
          error via showState('error', 'albums', data.error) — showState()
          gained an optional third `detail` param that, when present,
          shows the backend's actual message ("The crate reported an
          error: '...'") instead of the generic "check your Apps Script
          URL" text, since a real message from the script is more useful
          than a guess whenever one's available.

  1.29.22 — 2026-09-10 — Regression fix: 1.29.21's timeout broke loading
          entirely on desktop. That fix put a 25s ceiling on fetchCatalog's
          request (previously unbounded, which was the actual original
          bug), but 25s turned out to be shorter than this app's own
          normal full-catalog load time — a real load (albums + diary +
          scrobbles + playlists all in one Apps Script response) can
          legitimately take longer than that, especially cold. So instead
          of only catching genuine hangs, it was aborting every load
          before it could finish — "sometimes doesn't load" became
          "doesn't load at all". Raised the ceiling to 90s (comfortably
          past any plausible real load time while still eventually giving
          up on a true hang) and, since the actual "is this frozen?"
          concern is better solved by feedback than by an aggressive
          timeout, added a slowNotice: if loading is still going after 12s
          the "Cataloguing your collection…" message updates to say a
          larger collection or a cold Apps Script instance can take a
          while — so a legitimately slow load reads as "still working",
          not "stuck". Sorry for the regression.

  1.29.21 — 2026-09-10 — Bugfix: "the catalogue doesn't load" (intermittent).
          Root cause: fetchCatalog()'s request to the Apps Script backend
          was a plain fetch() with no timeout, unlike every other network
          call in the app (mbFetch, geoFetch, etc. all go through
          fetchWithTimeout). Google Apps Script web apps are known for
          slow/variable cold-start response times and can occasionally
          hang well past what the browser's own default socket timeout
          would catch — with nothing bounding it client-side, that left
          the UI stuck on "Cataloguing your collection…" indefinitely,
          with no error and no way to recover short of reloading the page.
          Now routed through fetchWithTimeout(..., 25000) — long enough to
          cover a real cold start, bounded enough to actually fail instead
          of hanging. A timeout is distinguished from other failures
          (err.name === 'AbortError') and gets its own message rather than
          the generic "check your Apps Script URL" one, since a slow
          response isn't a config problem. Both the timeout and the
          existing generic error state now show an inline Retry button
          (state-msg-retry-btn) that calls fetchCatalog() again, so
          recovery doesn't depend on finding the Refresh icon (which,
          post-1.29.20, may be tucked into the mobile overflow menu).
          Also added fetchCatalogInFlight, a simple guard against two
          overlapping fetchCatalog() calls (e.g. tapping Refresh while the
          initial load is still pending) racing to overwrite state.raw —
          whichever happened to resolve last previously won regardless of
          which was actually more recent.

  1.29.20 — 2026-09-10 — Four quick mobile fixes:
          (1) iOS zoom-on-focus — every text input/select/textarea in the
          app was under the 16px threshold that stops Safari auto-zooming
          the page on focus. One `@media (max-width:640px) { input,
          select, textarea { font-size: 16px !important; } }` rule fixes
          all of them at once rather than raising each component's
          font-size individually.
          (2) Hover-only UI — .cover-links (album card's Spotify/Discogs
          link icons), .dd-actions (deck diary entry edit/delete buttons),
          and .mini-player-seek-handle were all opacity:0 until :hover,
          making them unreachable on touch. `@media (hover:none)` (checks
          actual pointer capability, not viewport width) makes all three
          always-visible on genuinely touch-primary devices.
          (3) Safe-area insets — env(safe-area-inset-*) padding added to
          the mini player (was sitting right at the bottom edge, under the
          home indicator on notched phones), #app's mini-player-clearance
          padding to match, the mobile nav/queue drawers, the Map's
          full-screen mobile layout, and the generic full-screen modal
          pass — all now stay clear of notches/home-indicators instead of
          assuming a rectangular safe viewport.
          (4) Topbar overflow menu — the six secondary icon buttons
          (listened filter, add album, refresh, Last.fm import, timeline,
          map) were crowding the topbar next to search/sort/nav-tabs on a
          phone. Below the existing 900px drawer breakpoint,
          applyMobileOverflowMenu() relocates them (moved via appendChild,
          not cloned, so their existing click handlers keep working with
          no rewiring) into the gear icon's existing dropdown as a new
          "Actions" section with text labels added (menu-icon-btn-label),
          making the gear the "more actions" entry point on mobile; growing
          back past 900px moves them straight back to their original
          topbar position via topbarControls.insertBefore(...,
          mobileQueueToggleBtn). initMobileOverflowMenu() also makes them
          close the gear menu on tap, matching the menu's other actions.

  1.29.19 — 2026-09-09 — First mobile pass on the rest of the app (the
          Map got its own pass in 1.29.18; this covers everything else).
          Biggest gap: below the existing sub-900px breakpoint,
          .panel-leftnav/.panel-left/.panel-right (Music/Playlist Explorer
          switch, the Artist/Genre/Decade browse tree, the Up Next queue)
          were simply display:none with no other way to reach them. Added
          two off-canvas drawers that reuse those same panel elements
          in place (no content duplication) — a hamburger button
          (mobileNavToggleBtn) opens leftNavPanel+leftPanel from the left,
          a queue button (mobileQueueToggleBtn) opens rightPanel from the
          right, both via one setMobileDrawer('nav'|'queue'|null) toggling
          classes on #panels that a new @media(max-width:900px) block
          turns into fixed, full-height overlays (needs !important to beat
          the existing display:none !important rule and applyPanelVisibility's
          inline styles). Shared backdrop (mobileDrawerBackdrop) to tap-
          close; Escape and growing back past 900px close it too
          (initMobileDrawers's matchMedia listener).
          Also: the Tracks view's fixed pixel columns don't reflow, so
          .grid-wrap gets overflow-x:auto under 900px rather than
          crushing/overflowing them. Icon buttons and nav tabs get
          slightly bigger touch targets under 900px. And a generic pass
          on every .arrange-overlay/.arrange-modal dialog (Add/Edit album,
          Track properties, Last.fm import, playlist create/rename —
          everything except the Map, which already has its own floating-
          window mobile handling and is excluded via :not(.map-modal)/
          :not(.map-overlay)) goes near-fullscreen under 620px with larger
          form fields and buttons, matching the treatment the Deck modal
          already had.
          Not covered in this pass: the Artist tab's sidebar+detail split
          and the Diary/Playlist internals already had some responsive
          handling from before (verified, left alone); anything else not
          mentioned above hasn't been specifically audited.

  1.29.18 — 2026-09-09 — Map window: added mobile handling. Below a 700px
          viewport (isMapMobileLayout(), matching the existing CSS
          breakpoint) the window is pinned full-screen instead of floating
          — drag-to-move and the resize corner are both disabled there
          (dragging would otherwise fight the CSS inset, and neither is a
          great touch interaction anyway), column-resize handles are
          hidden, and the attribute table drops its Genre/Type columns
          (still reachable by horizontal scroll right at the breakpoint
          edge) so Name/Artist/Location/Year stay legible. Touch targets
          (close button, toolbar buttons, table rows) are sized up.
          New initMapMobileBreakpointListener() handles the boundary case
          of the viewport crossing 700px while the map is already open
          (window resize, device rotation) — clears any stale inline
          position/size so the CSS full-screen rule can take over, or
          restores the remembered floating position/size when coming back
          the other way — and re-applies column widths either way, since
          mobile uses its own fixed MAP_COL_MOBILE_WIDTHS rather than
          whatever's persisted from a desktop session. invalidateSize()
          still always runs on any resize (including mobile), since
          Leaflet's tiles need to catch up on rotation even though the
          resize itself isn't persisted there.

  1.29.17 — 2026-09-09 — Map viewer is now a floating, resizable window
          instead of a centered/backdrop modal like the app's other
          overlays: no dimmed backdrop (.map-overlay is transparent and
          pointer-events:none so the rest of the app stays usable while
          it's open), dragged by its header (initMapWindowDrag, same
          clamp-to-viewport pattern as the Spotify mini player's detached
          mode), and resized via the browser's native `resize: both`
          corner handle. Position + size persist to localStorage
          (crate_map_window_prefs_v1) via a ResizeObserver
          (observeMapWindowResize), which also calls
          leafletMapInstance.invalidateSize() on every size change so the
          tiles actually redraw as the window grows/shrinks. Falls back to
          a near-fullscreen, non-resizable layout under 700px.
          Also replaced the flat album list under the map with a proper
          attribute table — Name/Artist/Location/Year/Genre/Type columns,
          each resizable by dragging its header edge (wireMapColumnResize,
          reusing the Tracks table's .ttc-resize-handle styling), widths
          persisted to crate_map_col_widths_v1. New functions:
          applyMapWindowPrefs/saveMapWindowPrefs/initMapWindowDrag/
          observeMapWindowResize/loadMapColWidths/saveMapColWidth/
          applyMapColumnWidths/wireMapColumnResize/renderMapAttrTable
          (replaces renderMapAlbumList).

  1.29.16 — 2026-09-09 — Map viewer: added a "Spread through time" toggle
          — a scrubber (reusing the Timeline viewer's slider+ticks
          pattern) that reveals a location the moment any album tied to it
          was released, so dragging it forward from the earliest release
          shows how the map's geography actually spread over the crate's
          history — a genre/scene's footprint unfolding release by
          release. Year or Month granularity (mapTimeGranYearBtn/
          mapTimeGranMonthBtn); ticks show how many new locations were
          reached each period (respecting the Category filter). Uses each
          album's own Release Date field directly — no scrobble/listen
          data involved (an earlier same-day version of this used
          scrobbles; that wasn't what was wanted — this is release history,
          not listening history). Albums with no release date are excluded
          while the toggle is on, since there's no date to place them at.
          New state: mapTimeEnabled/mapTimeGranularity/mapTimeIndex (all
          transient, not persisted). New functions: periodIndexFor/
          periodIndexToLabel/itemReleasePeriodIndex/refreshMapTimeScrubber/
          renderMapTimeTicks/setMapTimeIndex/mapTimeStep/toggleMapTimeMode/
          setMapTimeGranularity; buildMapGroups() now applies the time
          cutoff alongside the existing Category filter.

  1.29.15 — 2026-09-09 — Three follow-ups to the Map/Timeline viewers:
          (1) Map viewer now filters by Artist/Genre/Media Type/Decade (a
          category + value picker) and sorts by Title/Artist/Year — both
          the pins' popup lists and a new flat album list under the map
          respect the current filter+sort. New buildMapGroups(), a
          mapCategoryFilter()/populateMapCategoryValues()/
          mapSortComparator() trio, and renderMap() replaces the old
          inline geocode-and-render body in openMapModal() so filter/sort
          changes can re-render without reopening the modal.
          (2) Map geocoding switched from Nominatim to LocationIQ, which
          needs a free API key (unlike Nominatim) but is far more
          reliable from a browser — Nominatim's usage policy doesn't
          really permit this kind of client-side lookup and requests were
          getting blocked. New mapKeySetup panel (shown until a key is
          saved, same pattern as the Last.fm setup screen), key stored in
          localStorage (crate_map_apikey_v1, not synced to the backend —
          same treatment as the Last.fm/Spotify/Discogs keys), and an
          "API key" button in the map toolbar to change it later.
          geocodeLocation()/geoFetch() updated for LocationIQ's endpoint
          and faster rate limit (550ms spacing vs Nominatim's 1.1s).
          (3) Timeline viewer now has a Released/Listened toggle. Listened
          mode builds the year ticks from state.scrobbles (total plays per
          year) instead of release dates, and the year's album list shows
          what was actually played that year with a play count per album,
          sorted by most-played — matched back to the crate by artist+
          title the same way the Scrobbles tab does. New state.timelineMode,
          buildListenYearCounts(), renderTimelineListenList(), and
          setTimelineMode(); buildTimelineYearCounts()/
          renderTimelineAlbumList() now branch on the mode.

  1.29.14 — 2026-09-09 — Added a Map viewer: new 🗺 toolbar button opens a
          modal with a dark-themed Leaflet/OpenStreetMap map showing pins
          for every place a "Location" has been entered on an album (where
          the band was formed, the studio it was recorded at, a venue it
          played — whatever's most relevant; free text, same idea as the
          existing Region field but for a specific place rather than a
          country/language). New optional "Location" field on the Add/Edit
          album forms, next to Region. Locations are geocoded client-side
          via OpenStreetMap's Nominatim search API and cached in
          localStorage (crate_geocode_cache_v1) so the same place string is
          never looked up twice; requests are queued through one shared,
          1.1s-spaced queue (geoFetch, modeled on the existing mbFetch
          MusicBrainz queue) to respect Nominatim's rate-limit policy.
          Albums sharing the same location text are grouped under one pin;
          clicking a pin's popup opens straight through to that release,
          same click-through pattern as the Timeline viewer's album list.
          New state: none persisted beyond the geocode cache itself (the
          map re-groups from state.raw's `location` field on every open).
          New functions: openMapModal/closeMapModal/buildMapMarkers/
          renderMapMarkers/geocodeLocation/geoFetch/ensureLeafletMap.
          REQUIRES A BACKEND CHANGE THIS FILE CAN'T MAKE: apps-script-
          backend.gs needs a new "Location" column read on fetch and
          written on addAlbum/editAlbum, same as Catalog/ManualTracks were
          added before — until then the field will save into local state
          for the current session but won't survive a refresh.

  1.29.11 — 2026-09-09 — Right-clicking an album in the left panel's Albums

  1.29.10 — 2026-09-09 — Fixed: adding a release picked from a MusicBrainz/
          search result (or any album with nothing typed into the tracklist
          builder), then editing it — even just adding a Genre — made its
          auto-looked-up tracklist disappear for good. Cause: saveNewAlbum/
          saveEditAlbum always wrote ManualTracks (even "[]" when the
          builder has no rows), and since 1.29.7 isManualAlbum() correctly
          treats any written ManualTracks as manual — including empty —
          this silently flipped the album to "manual, zero tracks" on every
          save, permanently blocking the iTunes/MusicBrainz auto-lookup
          that was previously filling in its tracklist. Both saves now only
          write ManualTracks when the builder actually has rows in it, or
          (editAlbum only) the album was already manual before the save —
          so an unrelated field edit no longer touches tracklist state at
          all, while deliberately emptying an existing manual tracklist
          (1.29.7's fix) still works exactly as before.

  1.29.9 — 2026-09-09 — Configurable field slots for the right sidebar's
          Now Playing queue and the bottom player, matching the existing
          grid/tracks/covers pattern (Panels configuration → "Panel
          Settings…" on Right Panel / bottom player). New state.fields.queue
          (default Artist, Year — printed as a second line under each
          queued track so tracks collectively part of one release read as a
          group) and state.fields.miniPlayer (default Artist, Album —
          printed under the mini player's track title, same two lines as
          before but now customizable to Genre/Year/Rating/etc instead).
          Also fixed the underlying gap this exposed: a YouTube-sourced
          track (manual-album/crate playback) showed a hardcoded "YouTube"
          placeholder instead of its real artist/album, since nothing
          tracked which crate item was behind whatever's currently playing.
          New nowPlayingItem, set at every playback entry point that knows
          it (playTrackNow, playAlbumNow, playCrateQueueIndex, the deck's
          "▶ Play" button, openYoutubePlayer's new item param), backs both
          new field groups with real release metadata for both engines.

  1.29.8 — 2026-09-09 — Ctrl/Cmd+A now selects everything visible in the
          main panel: all rows in the flat Tracks/"Album and Tracks" view,
          or all cards in the Albums/Album covers grid (new selectAllVisible
          Tracks/Albums helpers). Also reworked album-card interaction so
          selecting a release no longer requires opening it: a plain click
          now selects the card (ctrl/cmd toggles, shift range-selects) the
          same way track rows already worked, and opening the release view
          is a deliberate double-click, Enter, or right-click → "View

          String(item.row)) backs this, separate from selectedTracks.

  1.29.7 — 2026-09-09 — Fixed: batch/single "Remove from tracklist" silently
          failed to remove the last track(s) of a manual tracklist, and a
          release could never be left with zero tracks. Cause: isManualAlbum()
          judged "is this album manual" by manualTracks.length, so emptying
          the list to [] made it look like a never-manual album again and
          loadTracklist()/getTracksFor() fell back to the iTunes/MusicBrainz
          auto-lookup, silently repopulating the tracks that were just
          deleted. Added a separate hasManualTracklist flag (derived from
          whether ManualTracks was ever explicitly written, even as "[]")
          so an intentionally-emptied manual tracklist stays empty instead
          of reverting to auto-lookup. No backend change needed.

          cluttered. Back to just the individual ✕ per row. Batch-deleting
