  1.29.4 — 2026-09-08 — "Remove from tracklist" in a track's right-click
          menu now works on a multi-track selection too (previously
          single-track only) — select several tracks in the Tracks table,
          the "Album and Tracks" view, or an album's own tracklist, then
          right-click → Remove from tracklist to delete them all at once.
          A selection spanning more than one album is grouped by album and
          each one's tracklist is rewritten once. See
          quickRemoveTracksFromTracklist.

  1.29.3 — 2026-09-08 — Added batch delete to the tracklist builder (Add/
          Edit album forms): each row now has a checkbox, plus a "Select
          all" checkbox and "Delete selected" button next to "+ Add
          track" — clears out a run of unwanted tracks (e.g. after an
          auto-populated tracklist) in one click instead of hitting each
          row's ✕ individually. See toggleAllTracklistBuilderRows/
          deleteSelectedTracklistBuilderRows/syncTracklistBuilderSelectAll.

  1.29.2 — 2026-09-08 — Fixed the Tracks view getting stuck on "Loading
          tracks…" forever when one album's tracklist lookup hangs (seen
          with a punk rock genre view where one release's iTunes/
          MusicBrainz lookup never resolved). Root cause: plain fetch()
          has no timeout, and loadFlatTrackTable()/loadPlaylistFlatTrackTable()
          only finish once every item settles — a request that never
          resolves OR rejects left them waiting forever, and since all
          MusicBrainz calls share one queue (mbFetch), a hung request also
          jammed every other MusicBrainz lookup queued behind it. New
          fetchWithTimeout() (15s, via AbortController) now wraps every
          iTunes fetch in getTracksFor() and the fetch inside mbFetch's
          run(); new withHardTimeout() (20s) additionally wraps each
          getTracksFor() call in loadFlatTrackTable, loadPlaylistFlatTrackTable
          and loadTracksForList as a second layer, so a stuck item now
          resolves as an empty tracklist and lets the rest of the queue
          keep moving instead of freezing it.

  1.29.1 — 2026-09-08 — Fixed Release date "not always" being saved when
          adding an album via search. buildReleaseResultRow's prefillData.
          releaseDate only ever came from the specific MusicBrainz *release*
          hit's own `date` field (or, for the discography "+ Add" flow
          only, a release-group-year fallback) — but individual release
          resources very often have no date of their own, only their
          release-group's first-release-date reliably does, and the
          free-text search path (doAddSearch) never supplied any fallback
          at all. New resolveReleaseGroupFirstDate() looks that up in the
          background (same inject-after-the-fact pattern the cover art
          fetch just above it already used) and fills prefillData.
          releaseDate — and the row's visible year — once it resolves,
          instead of leaving it silently blank.

  1.29.0 — 2026-09-08 — New "Recorded" date property alongside Release
          date, for when a release's recording date differs from when it
          came out (e.g. a live album, or a reissue of older sessions).
          Same year/month/day precision fields and storage rules as
          1.28.0's release date rework — new addRecordedYear/Month/Day and
          editRecordedYear/Month/Day fields in the Add/Edit modals, backed
          by recordedDate-specific mirrors of that version's helpers
          (splitRecordedDate/combineRecordedDate/recordedDatePrecision/
          formatRecordedDateStr/formatRecordedDate/initRecordedDateFields/
          setRecordedDateFields/getRecordedDateFields — kept separate from
          their release-date counterparts rather than generalized, so this
          doesn't touch that already-working code path). Stored/sent as
          item.recordedDate / payload+column "Recorded", same "YYYY" /
          "YYYY-MM" / "YYYY-MM-DD" precision string as releaseDate. Also
          wired into FIELD_OPTIONS/keyToVal (so it can be picked for grid
          slots and cover tags, same as Release Date) and appended to the
          deck's meta line as "Recorded <date>" whenever it's set.

  1.28.0 — 2026-09-08 — Release date now supports year-only and
          year+month precision, not just a full day. The Add/Edit forms'
          single `<input type="date">` (which silently rejects/blanks
          anything but a complete YYYY-MM-DD — a real problem, since
          MusicBrainz/Spotify both commonly report only a year or
          year+month for older or obscure releases, and that precision was
          getting thrown away the moment such a search result landed in
          the form) is replaced with separate Year/Month/Day fields; Day is
          disabled until a Month is picked, and the value saved is whichever
          of "YYYY" / "YYYY-MM" / "YYYY-MM-DD" the filled-in fields support
          (new splitReleaseDate/combineReleaseDate, wired up by
          setReleaseDateFields/getReleaseDateFields in showAddForm/
          saveNewAlbum and openEditModal/saveEditAlbum). Every place a
          release date is displayed now goes through the stored string's
          own precision (new releaseDatePrecision/formatReleaseDateStr)
          instead of assuming a full date — a year-only release shows just
          "1977", not a fabricated "Jan 1, 1977". Also fixed the
          MusicBrainz "+ Add" fallback that was turning a discography row's
          year-only info into a fake "YYYY-01-01" before this even existed.
          Bonus fix bundled in since it directly affects this: every
          `new Date(releaseDate).getFullYear()` (and the MusicBrainz search
          row's own year display) switched to getUTCFullYear() — a
          date-only string like "2026" or "2026-09" parses as UTC midnight,
          so plain getFullYear() was rolling the displayed year back by one
          for anyone in a negative-UTC-offset timezone the moment local
          time crossed into the previous day.

  1.27.3 — 2026-09-08 — Add-album modal now has a "Skip search — enter
          everything manually" link right under the search box, visible as
          soon as the modal opens. Previously the only way to the blank
          manual-entry form was to type a query into the MusicBrainz search
          and either wait for it to fail or click "Enter it manually" among
          the results (doAddSearch) — fine when the album just doesn't
          match, but there was no way to skip straight past search for
          someone who already knows they want to type every field by hand.
          New button goes straight to the same showAddForm({}) the existing
          "enter manually" links use, just reachable with no search first.

  1.27.2 — 2026-09-08 — Fixed a specific playlist's Tracks tab getting stuck
          on "Loading tracks…" forever (others unaffected). 1.26.2/1.26.3
          only guarded against getTracksFor()'s async lookups rejecting; a
          stray null/undefined entry in one playlist's items array (e.g.
          left behind by an add/remove race) instead threw synchronously
          the moment loadPlaylistFlatTrackTable's album-list `.filter()`/
          `.map()` or its row-building settle() touched it — before either
          function's .catch() logic even ran — aborting silently right
          after "Loading tracks…" was already on screen, with nothing to
          fall through to. Both steps now skip a bad entry instead of
          throwing, and either one failing outright now swaps in a status
          message ("...one of its entries looks corrupted...") rather than
          leaving the loading state in place.

  1.27.1 — 2026-09-08 — Added a YouTube quick-link ("Y") to the album cover,
          alongside the existing Spotify/SoundCloud/RYM ones — starts out as
          a search link (youtubeSearchUrl(), already used elsewhere for the
          Artist tab's discography) and upgrades to a direct video/channel
          link on hover/focus if MusicBrainz has a youtube.com or youtu.be
          url-rel on the release or release-group, same resolution path as
          the other three (getExternalLinksFor/applyRelationUrls, now also
          checking the crate's own Link field for a direct YouTube URL
          first). Deck and right-click menu are unchanged for now — this
          only covers the album cover, as asked.

  1.27.0 — 2026-09-08 — Two RYM improvements. (1) A manually-entered RYM
          Link (Add/Edit modal) now shows up immediately on the cover art's
          "R" quick-link and the deck's RYM link — previously both always
          started out pointing at a plain RYM search and only picked up a
          direct link after the card was hovered/focused (buildCard) or the
          deck's getExternalLinksFor() lookup finished (openDeck), even
          though a manually-entered link needs no lookup at all and was
          known synchronously the whole time. (2) Right-clicking an album
          card now offers "View on RateYourMusic ↗" alongside the existing
          "Open on Spotify ↗", via new openAlbumOnRym() (same
          getExternalLinksFor()-then-fallback-to-search pattern as
          openAlbumOnSpotify).

  1.26.3 — 2026-09-08 — Root-caused the "playlist stuck loading" bug from
          1.26.2 more fully: 1.26.2 only patched loadPlaylistFlatTrackTable
          (the playlist Tracks tab), but getTracksFor() could reject from
          *any* of its 7 call sites, since mergeTrackLinkOverrides() and the
          cache-write line ran outside its own try/catch blocks — a bad
          trackData entry there rejected the whole tracklist fetch. The
          worst-hit caller was playPlaylistFromStart() (the ▶ Play button):
          `await Promise.all(albums.map(getTracksFor))` had no try/catch at
          all, so one rejection threw out of an async function nothing was
          listening on, leaving the "Loading playlist…" toast on screen
          forever with the queue never starting. getTracksFor() now catches
          around both of its mergeTrackLinkOverrides() calls so it's
          guaranteed to always resolve, fixing every caller at once;
          playPlaylistFromStart() also gets its own try/catch as a backstop.

  1.26.2 — 2026-09-08 — Fixed playlists occasionally getting stuck on
          "Loading tracks…" forever. loadPlaylistFlatTrackTable() counts
          down a `remaining` counter as each album's getTracksFor() call
          resolves, only rendering once it hits 0 — but it only chained
          .then(), so an album whose lookup rejected (mergeTrackLinkOverrides
          or the cache write throwing, outside getTracksFor's own try/
          catches) silently dropped out of the count and the table never
          finished loading. Now catches that rejection and falls through
          with no tracks for that album so the counter always advances,
          matching the .finally()-based pattern loadFlatTrackTable already
          uses for the library's own Tracks view.

  1.26.1 — 2026-09-07 — Fixed the "Albums ▾" view dropdown (and, less
          visibly, every other button bound in bindEvents()) intermittently
          stopping working after signing out and back in, or after any
          mid-session re-auth bounce, in the same tab. enterApp() only
          guards against running twice via appEntered, but both signOut()
          and handleAuthError() reset that flag, so a second entry re-runs
          init() on the same live DOM. init() called bindEvents() (and the
          three makeResizable() setups right after it) unconditionally, with
          no removal step, so a second run double-bound every listener they
          attach — for a plain click this mostly just fires a handler twice,
          but for the view dropdown's open/close toggle, two bound listeners
          on the same click flip it open then immediately shut again, so the
          menu appeared to do nothing. New module-level eventsBound flag
          gates that whole block in init() so bindEvents()/makeResizable()
          only ever run on the first entry; everything else in init() (prefs,
          catalog fetch, etc.) still re-runs normally on later entries, same
          as before.

  1.26.0 — 2026-09-07 — Tracks can now be added to or removed from any
          album's tracklist, not just fully manual ones. Three new entry
          points, all sharing one write path (saveManualTracklist, seeded
          from currentTracklistAsManualRows — a snapshot of whatever's
          currently showing, auto-fetched or manual, with links already
          resolved): a track's right-click menu has "Remove from
          tracklist" (confirms first, same pattern as the existing
          playlist/folder delete confirmations); the deck's tracklist
          footer has "+ Add track" (a small form — title plus optional
          YouTube/Spotify links); and the Edit modal's tracklist builder
          has "⤓ Load current tracklist", which now also has a Spotify
          link column alongside the existing YouTube one (both flow
          through the same manualTracks JSON, no backend column needed).
          All three convert the album to a manually-maintained tracklist
          the moment they're used (isManualAlbum) — it stops picking up
          future automatic tracklist lookups, called out in each
          confirmation/hint since there's no way back except retyping it.
          collectTracklistBuilderRows (and the new saveManualTracklist)
          now preserve a row's original track number instead of always
          renumbering 1..N by position — each row's data-track-number
          carries it through — so editing/reordering/removing rows can no
          longer silently reattach an existing rating/note/genre/link to
          the wrong track; only genuinely new rows get a fresh number.

  1.25.0

  1.25.0 — 2026-09-06 — Per-track link overrides: "Edit track properties…"
          (the right-click menu action, previously just Genre) now also has
          "YouTube link" and "Spotify link" fields, saved into the same
          trackData JSON blob as genre/rating/note/listened — so any track
          on ANY album (not just manually-tracklisted ones) can be pinned to
          an exact YouTube video or Spotify track link instead of trusting
          the automatic iTunes/MusicBrainz tracklist match or the fuzzy
          Spotify search-by-name. New trackLinkOverridesFor() (single track,
          used by the crate-queue builders and manualYoutubeLinkFor's old
          call sites) and mergeTrackLinkOverrides() (a whole tracks array,
          called from getTracksFor() and loadTracklist()'s manual-album
          branch right before caching/rendering) are the two read paths —
          both resolve a track's saved YouTube link the same way
          manualYoutubeLinkFor() already did (trackData first, falling back
          to a manual album's own manualTracks entry so existing manual
          tracklists keep working unchanged) and add the equivalent lookup
          for a saved Spotify link via new trackSpotifyUriFromLink(), which
          accepts either a plain open.spotify.com track URL (reusing the
          existing spotifyUri() parser) or a raw spotify:track:ID URI typed
          directly. playTrackNow() (now taking trackNum as a third
          argument), playCrateQueueIndex(), and the flat Tracks table's
          double-click handler all check for a saved Spotify link first,
          then a saved YouTube link, before falling back to their previous
          per-album-type default (manual → YouTube-only, skip if neither is
          saved; auto-lookup → fuzzy Spotify search) — the three separate
          copies of that decision the flat table, deck, and "Album and
          Tracks" play buttons each used to carry are now the one
          playTrackNow() call. The deck and "Album and Tracks" tracklist
          rows now also show the ▶ Spotify button for a manually-tracklisted
          album's track once it has a saved Spotify link (previously that
          button was unconditionally hidden for every manual-album track,
          link or no link).

  1.24.1 — 2026-09-06 — Fixed the master-player merge (1.24.0) flipping back
          to the Spotify face while a YouTube video was still playing, so
          the video kept going audibly behind the hidden video wrap.
          Starting a video calls spotifyPlayer.pause() to yield the audio
          (playYtQueueIndex), but that pause is itself a Spotify state
          change, and two places — the SDK's player_state_changed listener
          (onSpotifyPlayerStateChanged) and the 6-second Web API poll
          (refreshNowPlayingFromWebApi) — treated any "Spotify says paused"
          report as reason to call setMasterPlayerEngine('spotify'), with
          no check for whether a video was the thing actually playing.
          Within moments of switching to a video, one of the two would fire
          and silently hand the shared player back to Spotify. Once that
          happened, activePlayerEngine no longer matched reality, so
          Prev/Next and the video-to-Spotify handoff (playSpotifyUri's
          `activePlayerEngine === 'youtube'` guard before closeYoutubePlayer)
          acted on the wrong engine too — the likely cause of a previously
          playing track not resuming/showing as playing after going back.
          Added isStaleSpotifyPauseWhileYoutubePlaying(), which both call
          sites now check before rendering: a "paused" report is ignored
          whenever the master player is currently on the YouTube engine,
          since a genuine resume or track change from Spotify always
          arrives with paused:false and still gets through untouched.

  1.24.0 — 2026-09-06 — Merged the standalone YouTube player floating window
          (#ytPlayer and its dedicated transport/seek/drag code) into the
          existing #miniPlayer, so there's one shared player UI instead of
          two duplicate ones. #miniPlayerVideoWrap (holding #ytPlayerMount)
          swaps in for the album-art thumbnail when activePlayerEngine is
          'youtube', plus a fullscreen button that only shows for YouTube.
          Play/Pause, volume, and seek (drag + arrow keys) are now single
          handlers keyed off activePlayerEngine instead of duplicate
          Spotify/YouTube versions. masterPlayerStepTrack() gives Prev/Next
          one cross-platform route through the crate queue when one is
          active, falling back to native per-engine skip otherwise. Deleted
          showYtPlayer, ytDefaultPos, initYtPlayerDrag, initYtSeekDrag,
          ytSeekRatioFromEvent, updateYtProgressUI, formatYtTime,
          setYtPlayerMinimized, renderYtQueuePanel, and initYtPlayerUI (all
          folded into the shared master-player equivalents), and the old
          .yt-player CSS block. Deliberately dropped in this merge: the
          standalone YouTube player's "minimize" state and its separate
          mute button — volume-to-zero now serves as mute on both engines,
          matching how the Spotify side already worked.

  1.23.1 — 2026-09-06 — Three fixes:
          (1) The "Up Next" panel (1.23.0) was display-only — rowHtml() in
          renderCrateQueuePanel() had no click handler at all, so there was
          no way to jump to a track in the queue. Upcoming rows are now
          real buttons (data-queue-index) wired to a new click handler that
          calls playCrateQueueIndex(crateQueueId, index) for the row's
          actual position in crateQueueList — the "Now playing" row (when
          item is non-null) is clickable too, replaying that same entry.
          (2) openYoutubePlayer(url, trackName) built its "queue the rest
          of the album" list from the global currentTracklistTracks, which
          only reflects whichever album happens to be open in the deck —
          not necessarily the album the clicked track belongs to. Calling
          it from the deck's own tracklist (the original use case) always
          matched, but the "Album and Tracks" view's ▶ YT button, the flat
          Tracks table's double-click, and playTrackNow()'s manual-album
          branch all called it with no such guarantee: with a different
          album's tracklist loaded in the (possibly closed) deck, the
          clicked video's ID wouldn't be found in that stale list and
          playback silently fell back to ytQueue[0] — an unrelated track.
          This read as "playing a YouTube track after a Spotify track was
          on screen doesn't work," since the reverse direction always did
          a fresh Spotify API search and had no equivalent stale-cache
          path. openYoutubePlayer now takes an explicit third `tracks`
          argument — the correct owning album's track list — and each of
          those three call sites passes it (from state.trackCache, already
          populated for exactly this reason) instead of relying on the
          global; the deck's own call site still omits it and falls back
          to currentTracklistTracks, which remains correct there.
          (3) Hardened "Add to playlist" against duplicate adds: the
          submenu's playlist buttons are now disabled the instant one is
          clicked (re-enabled/removed right after), so a fast double-click
          or a click landing while the menu is already mid-close can't
          fire addPlaylistItems twice for the same selection. items is also
          deduped by (row, trackNum) before the request goes out, in case
          a selection ever ends up listing the same track more than once.
          This is a client-side safeguard — if duplicates are still showing
          up after this, the cause is most likely in apps-script-backend.gs's
          addPlaylistItems handler, which this file can't inspect or edit.

  1.23.0 — 2026-09-06 — New "Up Next" panel: #rightPanel (previously an
          unused "reserved for something later" placeholder) now shows the
          crate queue (crateQueueList/crateQueueIndex — the same
          cross-platform queue "Play playlist", "Add to queue", and
          multi-select "▶ Play" all already drive, see 1.21.0/1.22.0/1.22.2)
          as a live "Now playing" + "Up next" list, each row marked ♫/▶ for
          which engine it'll actually play on. This is deliberately separate
          from the existing #queuePanel popover on the Spotify mini player,
          which only ever shows Spotify's own native device queue — that
          one has no way to represent a YouTube-only track at all, which was
          the point of the whole crate queue system in the first place.
          New renderCrateQueuePanel() (called from playCrateQueueIndex and
          addToCrateQueue, so it stays live through every path that touches
          the queue) and a "Clear" button (new clearCrateQueue()) that stops
          the queue from advancing further without touching whatever's
          currently playing. addToCrateQueue's "fold in what's already
          playing" placeholder entry (1.22.0) now captures the real track
          name from currentSpotifyTrack/ytQueue at fold-in time instead of a
          static "(already playing)" stand-in, so the "Now playing" row
          reads correctly. New #crateQueuePanelBody/#crateQueueClearBtn
          reuse the mini player's existing .queue-list/.queue-row/
          .queue-panel-subheading/.queue-empty CSS for a consistent look.

  1.22.2 — 2026-09-06 — Fixed the track context menu's "▶ Play" over a
          multi-selection: it always called playTrackNow(item, trackName)
          for just the row that was right-clicked, no matter how many rows
          were selected, so only that one track ever played and — since
          nothing backed it with a queue — playback just stopped when it
          ended instead of moving on to the rest of the selection. New
          playTracksNow(trackPlaylistItems), used only when isMultiSelect,
          starts a fresh crate queue (see addToCrateQueue, 1.22.0) from the
          whole selection in order instead. Shares its entry-building with
          queueTracksNow via new buildCrateQueueEntriesForTracks() (pulled
          out of queueTracksNow's body, no behavior change there).

  1.22.1 — 2026-09-06 — #leftNavPanel (the far-left "Music"/"Playlists" rail,
          .panel-leftnav) no longer has its own var(--bg-card) fill —
          dropped that declaration (and its border-radius) so it sits flush
          against the app's base background like the rest of the layout
          instead of standing out as a visibly different-colored panel.
          Added in 1.12.1 to keep the rail from reading as two buttons
          floating in empty space; the full-width rows/hover states already
          do that job on their own.

  1.22.0 — 2026-09-06 — Two changes:
          (1) Double-clicking a playlist's name in the sidebar now plays it
          straight away (opens it and calls playPlaylistFromStart), in
          addition to the existing "▶ Play" button on the detail header.
          (2) "Add to queue" (both the track context menu's queueTracksNow
          and the album card menu's queueAlbumTracks) is now cross-platform:
          previously each pushed straight onto Spotify's own remote device
          queue via the Web API, which has no way to hold a manually-
          tracklisted album's track (see isManualAlbum()) — a mixed
          selection of Spotify and YouTube-only tracks silently dropped the
          latter. The "Play playlist" queue (1.21.0) already knows how to
          sequence a mix of Spotify tracks and YouTube-only ones and hand
          off between them, so that machinery — renamed from
          playlistQueue*/playPlaylistQueueIndex/playPlaylistTrackOnYoutube
          to crateQueue*/playCrateQueueIndex/playCrateTrackOnYoutube since
          it's no longer playlist-specific — is now the one queue everything
          feeds into. New addToCrateQueue(entries) is the shared entry
          point: if a crate queue is already driving playback (a "Play
          playlist" run, or an earlier "Add to queue") the new entries are
          just appended to the tail; if something is playing outside any
          queue (a direct "Play" on a single track/album/video), that's
          folded in as the current entry first so the existing end-of-track
          handoff carries on into what's being queued once it finishes;
          otherwise queuing starts playback immediately. queueTracksNow and
          queueAlbumTracks now just build { item, track } entries (the
          latter via getTracksFor(), matching how playPlaylistFromStart
          already builds a whole album's entries) and hand them to
          addToCrateQueue instead of resolving/POSTing to Spotify's queue
          endpoint themselves.

  1.21.1 — 2026-09-06 — Spotify and the floating YouTube player are now
          mutually exclusive: playSpotifyUri() closes the YouTube player
          (closeYoutubePlayer()) whenever it's open right as a Spotify track
          is about to start, and playYtQueueIndex() pauses spotifyPlayer
          whenever a YouTube video is about to start. This covers every path
          into each engine (manual track/album plays, the mini player,
          openYoutubePlayer, and the "Play playlist" queue's handoffs
          between the two from 1.21.0) since they all funnel through these
          two functions.

  1.21.0 — 2026-09-06 — New "▶ Play" button on a playlist's detail header
          plays the whole playlist through in order, handing off between
          Spotify and the floating YouTube player as needed: standalone
          tracks and whole albums are flattened into one ordered queue
          (same flattening loadPlaylistFlatTrackTable already does), each
          entry plays on Spotify normally, and a manually-added album's
          track (isManualAlbum() — see 1.20.2) plays its saved YouTube
          link in the background instead, with no automatic Spotify
          search. When one track finishes, the next one in the queue
          starts automatically regardless of which engine either one
          needs. See the new "Playlist "▶ Play" queue" section
          (playPlaylistFromStart/playCrateQueueIndex/
          playCrateTrackOnYoutube) — the Spotify Web Playback SDK has
          no real "track ended" event, so that half is a heuristic (went
          from playing to paused within CRATE_QUEUE_END_THRESHOLD_MS of
          the track's own duration); the YouTube half uses the player's
          real ENDED event via a new ytPlayingForCrateQueue flag on top
          of the existing per-album queue system from 1.17.0. A track this
          can't play (not found on Spotify, or a manual track with no
          YouTube link saved) is skipped with a toast rather than stalling
          the whole playlist.

  1.20.2 — 2026-09-06 — Two fixes for manually-tracklisted albums (the
          "Tracklist (optional)" builder on Add/Edit, for releases the
          iTunes/MusicBrainz auto-lookup can't find):
          (1) getTracksFor() — the shared loader behind the flat Tracks
          table, the "Album and Tracks" view, and playlist views — never
          checked item.manualTracks, so those views showed "No tracklist
          found" for a manual album even though the deck (which has its
          own separate manualTracks check in loadTracklist()) showed it
          fine. getTracksFor() now checks manualTracks first, same
          precedence as the deck, so all views agree. saveEditAlbum() also
          now clears state.trackCache on save so an edited manual
          tracklist isn't left showing the old cached one.
          (2) Playing a track from a manually-added album — the deck's ▶
          button, double-clicking a row in the flat Tracks table, the
          "Album and Tracks" view's ▶ button, and the right-click ▶ Play
          menu action — always ran a fuzzy Spotify search
          (`track:X artist:Y`) that just takes Spotify's top hit even when
          it's a different song entirely. For a niche release that isn't
          on Spotify (the reason it was added manually in the first
          place), this meant playing an unrelated track with no warning.
          New isManualAlbum(item) helper: all of the above now skip the
          Spotify search for manual albums and instead play that track's
          saved YouTube link if there is one, or show a toast saying no
          link is saved, rather than guessing. "Add to queue" likewise
          skips manual albums' tracks instead of risking queueing the
          wrong song (no YouTube equivalent exists to queue instead). The
          "Album and Tracks" view gained YouTube-link play button support
          it never had, for parity with the deck.

  1.20.1 — 2026-09-06 — Fixed a stale-load bug where the library's Tracks
          view (loadFlatTrackTable) could overwrite a playlist's Tracks
          table with library tracks: opening a playlist doesn't change
          state.mainViewMode, so an in-flight library track fetch's
          staleness check (which only looked at mainViewMode/token) still
          passed and clobbered the shared #trackTableBody element once it
          resolved. loadFlatTrackTable now also bails when
          state.activePlaylistId is set, mirroring the check
          loadPlaylistFlatTrackTable already had in the other direction.

  1.20.0 — 2026-09-06 — Right-click "Remove from playlist" added to both
          the track context menu (openTrackContextMenu, acts on the whole
          selection) and the album card context menu
          (openAlbumCardContextMenu) — shown only while a playlist is open.
          Removing a track that's part of a whole-album playlist entry
          removes that whole entry, since there's no such thing as a
          partial-album removal (see activePlaylistItemIdsForTracks). The
          album header row in the "Album and Tracks" view
          (buildAlbumTrackGroup) now has a right-click menu too (it was
          click-to-open-deck only before), so removal reaches that view as
          well as Album covers/Artists. removePlaylistItem's single-item
          logic was factored out into removePlaylistItemsByIds() so both
          the existing ✕ button and these new bulk actions share it. Also:
          a new "Release Date" field option sits alongside the existing
          "Release Year" everywhere a field can be picked — grid slots,
          cover tags, list/Tracks columns (see FIELD_OPTIONS / keyToVal /
          new formatReleaseDate()) — and "Edit track properties…"
          (openTrackPropertiesModal) is now a real .arrange-overlay/
          .arrange-modal window like the app's other modals (Add album,
          Edit details, …) instead of a small floating popup; same Genre
          field, same trackData storage, no backend change.

  1.19.0 — 2026-09-06 — The playlist's own "Tracks" view now reuses the
          library's flat Tracks table wholesale (buildTrackTableHeader() /
          renderFlatTrackTable(), see renderPlaylistTracksView) instead of
          its previous bespoke version: identical customizable/resizable/
          reorderable columns (state.fields.tracks/state.trackColWidths are
          shared with the library view, since only one is ever on screen at
          once), identical click-to-select and double-click-to-play-on-
          Spotify row behavior, and a row click no longer opens the album
          deck. The "Customize panel…" menu item is now available while a
          playlist's Tracks view is open too (refreshViewMenuForContext()
          and the menu's click handler are now playlist-aware, reading
          state.playlistViewMode instead of assuming state.mainViewMode).
          New loadPlaylistFlatTrackTable() feeds state.flatTrackRows the
          same way the library's own loadFlatTrackTable() does, just built
          from playlist.items (standalone tracks flattened in place, whole
          albums expanded via getTracksFor()) instead of state.filtered.

  1.18.1 — 2026-09-05 — Track rows in the tracklist (deck, flat Tracks
          table, and "Album and Tracks" view) no longer let plain text
          selection happen on click/shift-click — user-select: none on
          .track-row/.main-track-row — since shift-click for range-select
          (added in 1.18.0) was otherwise also highlighting the track name
          text like a normal text drag-select. The deck's note editor is a
          sibling of .track-row, not a child, so it's unaffected and stays
          normally selectable/editable.

  1.18.0 — 2026-09-05 — The deck's own tracklist (the "main release view")
          gets real click-to-select, matching the flat Tracks table and
          "Album and Tracks" view: clicking a track row highlights it
          (plain/ctrl/shift, same handleTrackRowClick() the other two views
          already share via state.selectedTracks), and its right-click menu
          — openTrackContextMenu() — now offers ▶ Play, Add to queue, Add
          to playlist, Add to entry, and (single-track only) Edit track
          properties…, alongside the existing View album/Search. "Add to
          entry" (new addTracksToEntry()) flags the selected track(s)
          listened for the current album — the same flag the per-track
          checkmark/"Select all" already use — so they show up ready to go
          the next time "+ Log a listen" is opened; opening the log form
          with more than one track already highlighted now folds them in
          automatically for the same reason, so highlight several tracks
          then hit "+ Log a listen" and they're already selected. "Add to
          queue" (new queueTracksNow()) resolves each selected track's
          Spotify ID by name and pushes it to the real Spotify queue,
          reusing the same Web API call queueAlbumTracks() already makes
          for a whole album. "Edit track properties…" (new
          openTrackPropertiesModal()) is a small floating form — currently
          just a Genre field — saved into that track's existing trackData
          blob via updateTrackData(), same column/JSON-blob this file
          already uses for per-track rating/note/listened, so no backend
          change is needed; a filled-in track genre now shows as a small
          badge next to the track name. All three new track-level actions
          (queue/entry/edit) work off state.raw-resolved items, not just
          activeItem, so right-clicking a multi-selected set in the flat
          table or "Album and Tracks" view can span more than one album's
          tracks too. Tracklist cleanup checks (updateTrackData,
          toggleTrackListened, clearTrackSelection) now also check for a
          saved genre before deleting a track's trackData entry, so
          clearing a listen-selection or unrating a track no longer
          silently wipes out a saved genre alongside it.

  1.17.0 — 2026-09-05 — The YouTube track player (added in 1.16.0) is no
          longer a centered modal overlay — it's now a small floating
          window (#ytPlayer) built on YouTube's real IFrame API instead of
          a plain embed src, so it can expose actual transport controls:
          drag it anywhere (drag handle in its header, position persisted
          in localStorage the same way the detached Spotify mini player's
          position is — see YT_PLAYER_PREFS_KEY), minimize it to a slim bar
          that keeps playing audio in the background while you use the
          rest of the app, a seek bar, a volume slider + mute toggle, a
          fullscreen button (requests fullscreen on the video area), and a
          Prev/Next/queue system: clicking a track's "▶ YT" button now
          builds a queue from every track in that album's tracklist that
          has a YouTube link attached (not just the one clicked), with a
          popover (☰) to jump to any of them and auto-advance on end. It
          survives switching tabs/closing the deck since it's a persistent
          fixed-position element outside the tab content, same as the
          Spotify mini player. See "YouTube floating player" in the script
          section (openYoutubePlayer/ensureYtPlayer/playYtQueueIndex/
          initYtPlayerUI etc.) and the .yt-player CSS block, modeled
          closely on the existing .mini-player/.queue-panel patterns.
          Old openYoutubePlayer(url, trackName) call sites are unchanged;
          only the plumbing behind them changed. Loads
          https://www.youtube.com/iframe_api once, lazily, on first use.

  1.16.0 — 2026-09-05 — Manually-added albums can now get a typed-in
          tracklist with a YouTube link per track, plus a way to actually
          play those links. The Add-album form (and Edit-album, so it's not
          a one-time-only thing) got a new "Tracklist (optional)" section:
          repeatable rows of Title + YouTube link, "+ Add track" to append
          another, a per-row ✕ to remove one, and pressing Enter in the last
          row auto-adds the next so a whole tracklist can be typed without
          reaching for the mouse. Saved as manualTracks: [{trackNumber,
          title, youtubeLink}] on the item (see showAddForm()/
          saveNewAlbum()/openEditModal()/saveEditAlbum() and the new
          tracklistBuilder* helpers). In the deck, an album with a manual
          tracklist shows those tracks instead of running the iTunes/
          MusicBrainz auto-lookup (loadTracklist() checks item.manualTracks
          first) — ratings/notes/"mark as listened" all still work per
          track exactly as before, keyed the same way by track number. Any
          track with a YouTube link gets a small "▶ YT" button next to the
          existing Spotify play button; clicking it opens a new lightweight
          overlay (#youtubePlayerOverlay) with an embedded YouTube iframe
          and autoplays that video, closing (and stopping playback) via its
          ✕, clicking outside it, or Escape. This is deliberately a
          separate, simple overlay rather than being folded into the
          existing Spotify Web Playback SDK mini player (spotifyPlayer/
          showMiniPlayer()) — YouTube's IFrame API and Spotify's SDK are
          different playback engines with their own queue/seek/device
          models, and unifying them wasn't worth the complexity for what's
          fundamentally a fallback for albums Spotify doesn't have.
          SCOPE NOTE: only the deck's tracklist view knows about
          manualTracks right now — the "Album and Tracks" grid view and the
          flat Tracks table (buildAlbumTrackGroup() / loadTracksForList())
          are a separate rendering path that still only does the iTunes/
          MusicBrainz auto-lookup, so a manually-typed tracklist won't show
          up there yet. Say the word if that's needed too.
          REQUIRES A BACKEND CHANGE THIS FILE CAN'T MAKE: apps-script-
          backend.gs needs a new "ManualTracks" column read in doGet() and
          written in doPost()'s addAlbum/editAlbum handlers, same JSON-
          blob-in-a-column pattern as the existing Catalog/TrackData
          columns (exact spelling: ManualTracks). The Sheet itself needs
          that column header added too. Until that's done, tracklists
          typed into the Add/Edit forms will appear to save (the toast says
          "Added"/"Changes saved") but won't actually persist anywhere —
          they'll vanish on the next fetchCatalog(), the same failure mode
          the SecondaryGenre/RymLink columns had in 1.9.0 before their
          matching backend change was made.

  1.15.2 — 2026-09-05 — Fixed catalog-number search in the Add-album modal
          not showing catalog numbers on its own results. doAddSearch()'s
          MusicBrainz query already matched on catno:"..." so the right
          pressings did come back, but the fetch only asked for
          inc=genres+tags — never inc=labels — so every hit's label-info
          came back empty and buildReleaseResultRow() had nothing to read a
          catalog number from, leaving the bracketed [catalog#] tag blank
          on every row regardless of what you searched by. Added
          inc=labels to that query. Separately, buildReleaseResultRow() was
          also only ever looking at label-info[0] — a release with more
          than one label/catalog-number pair (a joint release, or a
          reissue that kept the original cat# alongside a new one) could
          have the number you searched for sitting in label-info[1] and it
          would still never show. It now collects every catalog-number
          MusicBrainz has for that release and displays all of them,
          comma-separated, so whichever one matched your search is
          actually visible; the single-value "Catalog No." field on the
          add form still just prefills from the first one, as before, so
          saving an album isn't affected.

  1.15.1 — 2026-09-05 — The prior 1.15.0 entry below described a Playlist
          Explorer folder feature that was never actually finished — it
          left the UI half-wired (a "New folder" button and drag-and-drop
          targets that called openFolderModal()/movePlaylistToFolder()/
          createPlaylistFolder(), none of which existed anywhere in this
          file, so using them would throw) and renderPlaylistSidebar() had
          silently been left as the old flat, folderless list, with no
          folder rows ever actually rendered. It also assumed backend
          support (data.playlistFolders, a FolderId column) that
          apps-script-backend.gs doesn't have, so even the parts that
          "worked" would've been wiped on the next refresh. This entry
          replaces that with a real, working implementation: since a
          backend change isn't possible from this file, folders are now
          kept entirely client-side in localStorage (state.playlistFolders
          + state.playlistFolderMap, both persisted via loadPrefs()/
          savePrefs() next to the existing playlistFoldersCollapsed) rather
          than round-tripping through the sheet. renderPlaylistSidebar()
          now genuinely builds folder rows (caret, name, count) each
          containing their filed playlists, with unfiled playlists listed
          below; folders collapse/expand and persist that state. Playlist
          rows are real HTML5 drag sources (draggable="true" + dragstart
          setting the dataTransfer payload the existing drop handlers
          already expected), droppable onto a folder row or its child list
          to file them, or anywhere else in the explorer to un-file them.
          Right-clicking a playlist row opens a menu (Rename/Edit → the
          existing playlist modal; Move to folder ▸ listing every folder
          plus "No folder" and "+ New folder…"; Delete, which now confirms
          first); right-clicking a folder row offers Rename folder / Delete
          folder (un-files its playlists, doesn't touch them). The
          create/rename folder modal (#folderModalOverlay) is now backed by
          real openFolderModal()/closeFolderModal()/saveFolderModalClick()
          functions. Also removed the leftover dead renderNavPlaylistsMenu()
          function and its orphaned el.navPlaylistsMenu reference — both
          referred to a #navPlaylistsMenu dropdown that no longer exists in
          the HTML (removed when the plain button replaced it), so they
          were silent no-ops; the stale comment describing that dropdown
          in the CSS section is corrected too. The always-visible
          "Playlist Explorer" button itself (vs. the old popup) and the
          per-playlist view modes (Tracks/Album and Tracks/Album covers/
          Artists/List) were already genuinely working and are unchanged.

  1.15.0 — 2026-09-05 — Playlist Explorer reworked per reference screenshot
          (MusicBee's Playlist Explorer): "Playlist Explorer" is no longer
          buried inside the "Playlists ▾" dropdown popup you had to open
          first — the far-left rail now has a plain, always-visible
          "Playlist Explorer" button right next to "Music" (same as
          navMusicBtn), and clicking it swaps the browser column straight
          to the explorer via the existing setLeftNavView('playlists') —
          no popup involved. The old #navPlaylistsMenu dropdown (and its
          quick-jump list + "+ New playlist…" entry) is gone entirely;
          renderNavPlaylistsMenu() and its call site are removed along with
          it, since the explorer panel itself already lists every playlist.
          Also added folders: new state.playlistFolders (array of
          {id, name}) and a folderId on each playlist, with a folder-icon
          "New folder" button next to the existing "+" in the explorer's
          panel bar. renderPlaylistSidebar() now builds folders (each with
          a collapse caret — collapsed state persisted in
          state.playlistFoldersCollapsed via prefs) containing their
          playlists, with any playlist that has no folderId listed at the
          top level below them. Playlists can be dragged onto a folder row
          to file them there (dragging elsewhere in the list files them
          back to the top level) via the existing native-HTML5-drag
          pattern already used for in-playlist reordering. Right-clicking a
          playlist row opens a small floating menu (same
          .now-playing-add-menu pattern as the album/track context menus)
          with Rename/Edit, a "Move to folder ▸" submenu (existing folders,
          "No folder", "+ New folder…"), and Delete; right-clicking a
          folder row offers Rename folder / Delete folder (deleting a
          folder just un-files its playlists rather than deleting them).
          New create/rename folder modal (#folderModalOverlay) mirrors the
          existing playlist modal. REQUIRES A BACKEND CHANGE THIS FILE
          CAN'T MAKE: apps-script-backend.gs needs a new PlaylistFolders
          sheet (Id, Name, CreatedAt), a FolderId column added to the
          Playlists sheet, and doPost() handling four new actions —
          createPlaylistFolder, renamePlaylistFolder, deletePlaylistFolder
          (clears FolderId on any playlists that had it, doesn't touch the
          playlists themselves), and movePlaylistToFolder ({playlistId,
          folderId}, folderId may be blank/null to un-file it) — plus
          doGet() needs to return the new sheet as data.playlistFolders and
          include FolderId on each row of data.playlists.

  1.14.0 — 2026-09-05 — Playlists gained real view modes. Opening a playlist
          used to always show the same fixed drag-to-reorder list no matter
          what — the "Albums ▾" view dropdown stayed visible but did nothing
          useful (clicking Tracks/Album and Tracks/Album covers/Artists just
          bounced you back to the library). That dropdown now drives a new
          state.playlistViewMode while a playlist is open, independent of
          the library's own state.mainViewMode, with four real layouts:
          Tracks (flat table, every album's tracks expanded), Album and
          Tracks (per-album header + full tracklist), Album covers (plain
          covers grid), and Artists (grouped by artist) — the last three
          reuse the library's own buildAlbumTrackGroup()/buildGridSection()/
          groupItems() so they look and behave identically (right-click
          menu, Spotify/SoundCloud/RYM links, cached art, click to open the
          deck). A new "List" option (playlist-only, hidden for the library)
          gets back to the classic ordered view. Group by/Customize panel
          are hidden from the menu while a playlist is open since they don't
          apply. No backend changes — this is main-panel rendering only.
  1.13.0 — 2026-09-05 — Fixed "Add to playlist" on a track to ignore any
          multi-selection: right-clicking one of several selected tracks
          (flat Tracks table, ctrl/shift-click) still only added the single
          track under the cursor, not the whole selection. openTrackContextMenu()
          now takes an optional selectedItems array — when the right-clicked
          row is part of the current selection, "Add to playlist" acts on
          every selected track instead; right-clicking a row outside the
          selection collapses to just that row first (so the old
          single-track behavior is exactly what you get when nothing else
          is selected). The menu title also reflects it ("3 tracks
          selected" vs. the track name). Also gave the per-album "Album and
          Tracks" view genuine click-select (plain/ctrl/shift, same
          handleTrackRowClick() the flat table uses, now generalized to
          take a container/selector so shift-range stays scoped to one
          album) — it had no selection at all before, only single-track
          right-click. Right-clicking an album card is unaffected and still
          adds the whole album (all tracks) — that path already worked as
          described.

  1.12.2 — 2026-09-03 — Removed #app's max-width: 1450px + margin: 0 auto
          centering — that (pre-existing, unrelated to the 1.12.x sidebar
          work) was what caused the large blank gutters either side of the
          whole app on wide/maximized windows that showed up in feedback
          screenshots. The app now just fills the full window width at any
          size, keeping only its existing 24px/40px breathing-room padding.

  1.12.1 — 2026-09-03 — Follow-up to 1.12.0's new far-left nav rail: it was
          two small floating buttons in an otherwise-empty column, unlike
          MusicBee's actual sidebar (all one filled panel, full-width rows).
          #leftNavPanel now has its own background (var(--bg-card)) so it
          reads as a real panel instead of blending into empty space, and
          the Music/Playlists buttons are full-width icon+label rows
          (edge-to-edge, left-aligned) instead of small centered icon-over-
          label squares. It's also drag-resizable now — new #resizerLeftNav
          between it and #leftPanel, wired through makeResizable() (which
          picked up optional minWidth/maxWidth args, 64–220px here vs. the
          existing panels' 160–420px) and a new state.panelWidths.leftNav
          (default 88, persisted same as the other panel widths — loadPrefs
          now merges saved panelWidths over the defaults instead of
          replacing the object outright, so old saved prefs without a
          leftNav entry still pick up the new default). Also added
          .panel-leftnav to the existing sub-900px "hide side panels" media
          query — it was missing before, so on a narrow window the rail
          would've kept floating there alone after #leftPanel disappeared.

  1.12.0 — 2026-09-03 — Replaced the 1.11.0 nested-tree left panel with a
          genuine new far-left nav column: a narrow #leftNavPanel section
          sitting to the left of the thumbnail-browser column, containing
          just two buttons, "Music" and "Playlists". #leftPanel itself now
          holds two swappable views — #musicBrowserView (today's Albums/
          Artists/Genres browser, unchanged) and #playlistsBrowserView (the
          flat playlist list) — with exactly one visible at a time via new
          setLeftNavView('music'|'playlists'), replacing the old collapsible
          "Playlists" tree node entirely (no more caret/expand-in-place).
          Clicking "Music" still calls exitPlaylistView() same as before;
          opening a playlist (openPlaylist()) now switches the rail to
          "Playlists" instead of auto-expanding a nested node. Old
          setPlaylistsTreeExpanded()/updateLeftTreeActiveState() helpers and
          state.playlistsTreeExpanded are gone, replaced by setLeftNavView()
          and state.leftNavView ('music' | 'playlists', default 'music').
          #leftNavPanel hides/shows together with #leftPanel via the
          existing "Show Categories" layout toggle. No backend/data-model
          changes — this is left-panel markup/CSS/JS only.
  1.10.0 — 2026-09-01 — Added a Playlist system. Playlists get their own
          persistent section in the left panel, below the Albums/Artists/
          Genres list (MusicBee-style: a fixed "Playlists" list with a
          "+" to add one, always visible regardless of which category is
          active) — not a Category dropdown option. Picking a playlist
          shows its ordered items in the main panel in place of the album
          grid. Playlists can mix whole albums and individual tracks in any
          order. "+ Add to playlist" is on the album card right-click menu
          and the track right-click menu (deck tracklist, flat Tracks
          table, and Album-and-Tracks view); the deck header also got a
          "+ Playlist" button for the currently open album. From an open
          playlist you can rename it, delete it, remove individual items,
          and drag-reorder items (native HTML5 drag-and-drop on each row).
          Synced to the sheet backend via two new sheets (Playlists,
          PlaylistItems). New state.playlists/state.activePlaylistId,
          populated in fetchCatalog() from data.playlists/data.playlistItems.
          REQUIRES A BACKEND CHANGE THIS FILE CAN'T MAKE: the Apps Script
          backend (apps-script-backend.gs) needs the matching update —
          doGet() reading two new sheets (Playlists, PlaylistItems) and
          doPost() handling six new actions (createPlaylist, renamePlaylist,
          deletePlaylist, addPlaylistItems, removePlaylistItems,
          reorderPlaylist). Paste the updated apps-script-backend.gs into
          the Apps Script project and redeploy (or update the existing
          deployment) for playlists to actually save anywhere.

  1.9.0 — 2026-09-01 — Two new manual fields in Add/Edit: "Secondary Genre"
          (a second comma-separated genre field, RYM-style — shown in the
          deck meta line as "Primary (Secondary)" and folded into genre
          search/filter/grouping everywhere via new combinedGenreStr()) and
          "RYM Link" (a dedicated field, separate from the general Link
          field, since that one's already spoken for by Spotify/SoundCloud/
          MusicBrainz on releases that have one of those). A filled-in RYM
          Link always wins over the automatic MusicBrainz/Wikidata
          resolution added in 1.8.0 — it's the manual override for whatever
          that lookup can't find. Editing either field on an existing album
          now also clears that album's getExternalLinksFor() cache entry
          (state.linkCache), which previously was never invalidated on
          edit at all, so a changed Link/RYM Link needed a refresh/re-add
          to actually show up in the deck.
          REQUIRES A BACKEND CHANGE THIS FILE CAN'T MAKE: the Apps Script
          backend (apps-script-backend.gs) needs to read/write two more
          columns — "SecondaryGenre" and "RymLink" — on addAlbum/editAlbum,
          and the Sheet itself needs those two column headers added (exact
          spelling, matching how Catalog/TrackData were added before).
  1.8.0 — 2026-09-01 — RYM links now resolve to a real release page instead
          of almost always falling back to a search link. RateYourMusic has
          no public API, but Wikidata's SPARQL endpoint is public, CORS-
          enabled, and — for albums tagged by its WikiProject Albums
          editors — often carries both a MusicBrainz release-group ID
          (P436) and a Rate Your Music release ID (P8392) on the same
          item, so a single query joining on the release-group ID we
          already have from MusicBrainz can hand back the exact RYM slug.
          New fetchRymFromWikidata() tries that first; if the item exists
          but lacks the RYM id (or MusicBrainz found no release-group at
          all), a second query falls back to matching by artist/title
          label instead of just giving up. Only if both come back empty
          does it fall through to the old rymSearchUrl() search link.
          Wired into getExternalLinksFor() as an additional source
          alongside the existing MusicBrainz url-rels checks, and cached
          in the same per-album linkCache so it's still only ever looked
          up once.
  1.7.0 — 2026-09-01 — Every version bump now has to show up in two places
          a person can actually see, not just this comment: the browser
          console (already had a console.log, but it printed an unrelated
          hardcoded string that had drifted out of sync with this log —
          now prints CURRENT_VERSION's value) and, new, a small "CRATE
          vX.X.X" line at the bottom of the ⚙ Layout menu so it's visible
          on-site too without opening devtools.
  1.6.0 — 2026-09-01 — Fixed "Artist" tab discography needing several
          Retry clicks to load. Root cause: every MusicBrainz-hitting
          feature (left-panel artist/year badges, the Artist tab's
          discography, Add-album pressing lookups) paced its OWN requests
          independently, so two of them running at once (e.g. the left
          panel's badge queue still going in the background while you
          open the Artist tab) could still collide and exceed
          MusicBrainz's real rate limit even though each looked correctly
          paced on its own. Added a single shared mbFetch() request gate
          that ALL MusicBrainz calls now go through — one queue, one
          clock, so they can never overlap — and it auto-retries a 429
          with backoff instead of surfacing it as a dead end needing a
          manual Retry. Removed the now-redundant per-call 1100ms
          setTimeout delays that were sprinkled across each feature.
  1.5.0 — 2026-09-01 — Right-click on a track (flat Tracks table, "Album
          and Tracks" view, and the album deck's own tracklist) now opens
          a context menu with "View album" and a "Search ▸" flyout ("Find
          artist …" / "Open discography"), reusing the same submenu the
          album card's right-click menu already had. New
          openTrackContextMenu() wires all three track-row locations to it.
  1.4.0 — 2026-09-01 — Search box now filters the whole Music section, not
          just the main grid: typing narrows the left panel too, so
          Artists/Genres/Albums mode only lists entries that match (e.g.
          "beatles" leaves just The Beatles' artist row/albums; a genre
          name leaves just albums tagged with it and the artists who have
          one). Also added genre to what the main-grid search matches
          against, so genre searches work there too. New shared helpers
          matchesSearchTerms()/searchFilteredRaw() back both the grid
          filter and buildLeftList().
  1.3.0 — 2026-08-29 — Diary "By track" search/manual-search-in-Add-modal fix:
          the general Add Album search (typing into the search box) picked a
          result and jumped straight to that ONE MusicBrainz release's data,
          so a free-text query that only surfaced one hit for an album never
          showed its other pressings/labels even though MusicBrainz had them.
          Search-result rows now drill into a proper release-group-scoped
          pressing lookup on click (same lookup the Artist-tab flow already
          used), so every "Add album" path now consistently offers a label
          picker instead of only some of them doing so.
  1.2.0 — 2026-08-29 — Found the real cause of the "By track" view still
          looking narrow after 1.1.0: #diaryWrap wasn't stretching to fill
          its flex parent's width at all (a column-flex + overflow:auto
          quirk), so every max-width set on it was moot — it was shrink-
          wrapping to content. Added explicit width:100% to .diary-wrap.
  1.1.0 — 2026-08-29 — Reworked Diary → "By track" view from one full-width
          row per track (with just a single small entry pill in it, leaving
          most of the row blank) into a responsive card grid that actually
          fills the available width.
  1.0.0 — 2026-08-29 — "Add as Album" from the mini player's now-playing menu
          now runs the MusicBrainz release-group pressing lookup (same as the
          Artist tab's + Add flow) so a label/pressing can be picked, instead
          of landing straight on a form with Label left blank.
